**Wiping.** The dust of non-reception is wiped off the feet (ἀπομάσσω). The woman wiped his feet with her hair (ἐκμάσσω, 7:38, 7:44). Love wipes the feet of the Divine; the messengers wipe their own feet of what did not receive. Nothing is burned (9:54–55).

**Joy moved upward.** The seventy rejoice that the daimons are ranked under them. They are told to rejoice instead that their names are written in the heavens. Power over falsity is his; the quality written in heaven is theirs as received. They tread snakes and the power of the enemy, while the enemy is loved (6:27).

**The law of veiling, stated as thanksgiving.** What was hidden from the wise is revealed to infants (10:21). All things are handed over (παραδίδωμι) to him from the Father, as he will be handed over into the hands of men (9:44). The knowing of the Father and the Son is mutual, and is given by revealing. Then: "blessed are the eyes that see".

**How do you read?**
- A lawyer stands up (ἀνέστη) to test him.
- The answer is love with the whole heart, soul, strength and mind, and the neighbour as oneself.
- "Do this and you will live": the first promise of life, which reaches to "the Living One" (24:5).

**The wordings.**
- Most of the adopted Marcionite readings are neutral by their words. Some carry a thread: εὐχαριστῶ (→ 17:16, 22:19), "life" (→ 10:28, 24:5).
- One strains: "Lord of heaven" without "and of the earth" (10:21). It separates the Lord from the earth, where in this text he has authority (5:24) and where the seed is received (8:8).

**The four unprinted passages all continue by their words, and none closes a gap.**
- *10:12–15:* Capernaum, where the word came down, is asked whether it will be lifted to heaven, and goes down to Hades. This is the law of the printed 18:14 said of the place of the beginning.
- *10:18:* Satan falls like lightning from heaven.
- *10:29–37:* a man going down from Jerusalem, the opposite direction to the road, is passed by the priesthood and received by a Samaritan into the "all-receiving" inn.
- *10:38–42:* Martha receives him straight after Samaria did not. Mary hears at his feet, and her chosen part is not taken.

In this chapter the passages that are not printed carry more of the up-and-down mechanics than the printed text does: the heights and the depths, the falling from heaven, the going down from Jerusalem.

### Threads, carried forward and added

- drawing near (ἐγγίζω): the kingdom, then the road (→ 18:35; 21:28; 22:47; 24:15, 24:28);
- before his face;
- the midst (lambs among wolves);
- casting out (into the harvest);
- peace given, resting, returning;
- setting before (παρατίθημι);
- wiping (μάσσω);
- treading (πατέω);
- ranking under (τάσσω);
- the enemy loved, his power trodden;
- names written in heaven;
- joy (→ 15:7–10; 24:41);
- thanksgiving (→ 17:16; 22:19);
- hidden and revealed (κρυπτός, ἀποκαλύπτω);
- handing over (παραδίδωμι) both ways;
- Father (natural father left; the Father revealed);
- knowing;
- blessed eyes;
- testing (→ 11:4, 11:16, 22:40, 22:46);
- reading;
- love with the whole;
- the self as measure;
- life and living (→ 24:5);
- upright (→ 13:13);
- going down from Jerusalem (in the test passage);
- lifted up and brought down (→ 18:14);
- the inn that receives all;
- the good part not taken away (→ 16:3).

---

## Chapter 9

*11:1–52. Grey: 11:6, 11:10, 11:16–17, 11:23, 11:44–45. Not printed: 11:24–26 (U?); 11:29b "except the sign of Jonah" (attested omission); 11:30–32 (A−); 11:34–36 (U?); 11:49–51 (A−); 11:53–54 (U?). Adopted Marcionite wordings: 11:2 a petition for the holy spirit where NA27 has "hallowed be your name"; 11:3 "your bread"; 11:38 "debating within himself"; 11:42 "the calling" for "justice".*

### 11:1

*Καὶ ἐγένετο ἐν τῷ εἶναι αὐτὸν ἐν τόπῳ τινὶ προσευχόμενον, ὡς ἐπαύσατο, εἶπέν τις τῶν μαθητῶν αὐτοῦ πρὸς αὐτόν· Κύριε, δίδαξον ἡμᾶς προσεύχεσθαι, καθὼς καὶ Ἰωάννης ἐδίδαξεν τοὺς μαθητὰς αὐτοῦ.*

- **"as he was praying in a certain place"**: prayer.
  - *Thread:* 5:16; 6:12; 9:18; 9:28–29, where his face changed.
  - The disciples have watched him pray. Now they ask to be taught.
- **"as John taught his disciples"**: John's disciples fast and pray (5:33). The one who prepared the way also taught prayer.

### 11:2

*Ὅταν προσεύχησθε, λέγετε· Πάτερ, ἐλθέτω τὸ πνεῦμά σου τὸ ἅγιον· ἐλθέτω ἡ βασιλεία σου·*

- **"Father"**: the Father revealed through the son (10:22), now addressed by those to whom he reveals.
- **"let your holy spirit come"**: the adopted Marcionite reading. NA27 has "let your name be hallowed".
  - By its words, the spirit petition is answered within the passage: "how much more will the Father from heaven give the holy spirit to those who ask him" (11:13).
  - The name would join the thread of the name: "in your name" (9:48; 10:17); "your names are written in the heavens" (10:20).
  - **Neutral:** each reading continues.
- **"let your kingdom come"** (ἐλθέτω): coming.
  - The kingdom "has come near" (ἤγγικεν, 10:9, 10:11); it "has arrived upon you" (ἔφθασεν, 11:20). Both petitions ask for something to come down.

### 11:3

*τὸν ἄρτον σου τὸν ἐπιούσιον δίδου ἡμῖν τὸ καθ’ ἡμέραν·*

- **"your bread"**: the adopted Marcionite reading; NA27 has "our bread".
  - "Your bread" joins the bread he takes, blesses, breaks and gives (9:16), and "this is my body" (22:19).
  - "Our bread" joins the three loaves the friend asks for (11:5).
  - **Neutral.**
- **"give us daily"** (τὸ καθ’ ἡμέραν): *the words of 9:23: "let him take up his cross daily (καθ’ ἡμέραν)".* The cross is taken up daily and the bread is given daily. The day as state (9:28): each state has its bread.

### 11:4

*καὶ ἄφες ἡμῖν τὰς ἁμαρτίας ἡμῶν, καὶ γὰρ αὐτοὶ ἀφίομεν παντὶ ὀφείλοντι ἡμῖν· καὶ μὴ εἰσενέγκῃς ἡμᾶς εἰς πειρασμόν.*

- **"let go (ἄφες) our sins, for we ourselves let go (ἀφίομεν) everyone who owes (ὀφείλοντι) us"**:
  - *ἀφίημι:* sins let go (5:20–24; 7:47–49); Levi left all (5:28); "let the dead bury their dead" (9:60).
  - *Owing:* the two debtors (7:41–43), forgiven, love in the measure of what is let go. *Forward:* "how much do you owe (ὀφείλεις)?" (16:5, 16:7).
  - The letting go asked for is the letting go that is done (6:37: "release, and you will be released").
- **"and do not bring (εἰσενέγκῃς) us into temptation"**: εἰσφέρω.
  - The friends sought to bring in (εἰσενεγκεῖν) the paralysed man to him (5:18–19). One can be carried in to healing, or into temptation.
  - Temptation: the lawyer "testing" (10:25). *Forward:* 11:16; "pray that you may not enter into temptation" (22:40, 22:46).

### 11:5–6 (11:6 grey)

*Τίς ἐξ ὑμῶν ἕξει φίλον καὶ πορεύσεται πρὸς αὐτὸν μεσονυκτίου καὶ εἴπῃ αὐτῷ· Φίλε, χρῆσόν μοι τρεῖς ἄρτους, ἐπειδὴ φίλος μου παρεγένετο ἐξ ὁδοῦ πρός με καὶ οὐκ ἔχω ὃ παραθήσω αὐτῷ·*

- **"a friend"**: the centurion's friends (7:6). *Forward:* the friends who rejoice (15:6, 15:9); "make friends for yourselves" (16:9).
- **"he goes (πορεύσεται) to him at midnight (μεσονυκτίου)"**: the road (πορεύομαι), at the middle (μέσος) of the night.
  - The night: his night of prayer (6:12). *Forward:* "this night your soul is required" (12:20); "in this night two in one bed" (17:34).
  - The midst, now of the night.
- **"lend me three loaves"**: bread, the good. Three: the whole (?).
- **"a friend of mine has come from the way (ἐξ ὁδοῦ)"**: one who comes from the road.
- **"and I have nothing to set before (παραθήσω) him"**:
  - παρατίθημι: the bread given "to set before" (παραθεῖναι) the crowd (9:16); "eat what is set before (παρατιθέμενα) you" (10:8).
  - The one asking has nothing of his own to set before the one from the way: "we have no more than five loaves" (9:13).

### 11:7–8

*κἀκεῖνος ἔσωθεν ἀποκριθεὶς εἴπῃ· Μή μοι κόπους πάρεχε· ἤδη ἡ θύρα κέκλεισται, καὶ τὰ παιδία μου μετ’ ἐμοῦ εἰς τὴν κοίτην εἰσίν· οὐ δύναμαι ἀναστὰς δοῦναί σοι. λέγω ὑμῖν, εἰ καὶ οὐ δώσει αὐτῷ ἀναστὰς διὰ τὸ εἶναι φίλον αὐτοῦ, διά γε τὴν ἀναίδειαν αὐτοῦ ἐγερθεὶς δώσει αὐτῷ ὅσων χρῄζει.*

- **"the one inside (ἔσωθεν)"**: the inside. *Thread: "the inside (ἔσωθεν) of you is full of plunder… did not the one who made the outside make the inside also?" (11:39–40).*
- **"the door is already shut (κέκλεισται)"**: the door.
  - *Forward:* "strive to enter through the narrow door… once the master of the house has risen (ἐγερθῇ) and shut (ἀποκλείσῃ) the door, and you begin to stand outside and knock at the door, saying, Lord, open to us" (13:24–25).
  - The door, the one inside who rises, the knocking and the opening are all here first, and all come back in 13:25, reversed.
- **"my children are with me in bed (κοίτην)"**: at rest.
- **"I cannot rise (ἀναστάς) and give"**.
- **"though he will not rise (ἀναστάς) and give because he is his friend, because of his shamelessness, being raised (ἐγερθείς), he will give him as much as he needs (χρῄζει)"**:
  - *Both rising verbs, ἀνίστημι and ἐγείρω, are said of one who gets up in order to give.*
  - Rising has gone with something done ever since the text began:
    - "rising, she served" (4:39, test);
    - "rising, he followed" (5:28);
    - "rise and walk… rising, he took up his bed" (5:23–25);
    - "rise and stand" (6:8).
  - Here rising is for giving.
  - Need (χρεία, 5:31; 9:11; χρῄζω here): he gives as much as is needed.

### 11:9–10 (11:10 grey)

*Κἀγὼ ὑμῖν λέγω, αἰτεῖτε, καὶ δοθήσεται ὑμῖν· ζητεῖτε, καὶ εὑρήσετε· κρούετε, καὶ ἀνοιγήσεται ὑμῖν· πᾶς γὰρ ὁ αἰτῶν λαμβάνει, καὶ ὁ ζητῶν εὑρίσκει, καὶ τῷ κρούοντι ἀνοιγήσεται.*

- **"ask, and it will be given to you"**: "give, and it will be given to you" (6:38). The same passive, the same measure.
- **"seek, and you will find"**: seeking.
  - The crowds sought him to hold him back (4:42). Herod sought to see him (9:9). Others seek a sign (11:16, 11:29).
  - *Forward:* "the Son of Man came to seek and to save the lost" (19:10); "why do you seek the Living One among the dead?" (24:5).
  - Seeking can be for holding, for seeing, for a sign, or for finding. Only the last is promised.
- **"knock, and it will be opened"**: the door of 11:7. *Forward:* the eyes "were opened" (διηνοίχθησαν, 24:31).
- **"everyone who asks receives (λαμβάνει)"**: λαμβάνω.
  - He took the bread (9:16). A spirit takes the boy (9:39). The days of his being taken up (ἀνάλημψις, 9:51).

### 11:11–13

*τίνα δὲ ἐξ ὑμῶν τὸν πατέρα αἰτήσει ὁ υἱὸς ἰχθύν, καὶ ἀντὶ ἰχθύος ὄφιν αὐτῷ ἐπιδώσει; ἢ καὶ αἰτήσει ᾠόν, ἐπιδώσει αὐτῷ σκορπίον; εἰ οὖν ὑμεῖς πονηροὶ ὑπάρχοντες οἴδατε δόματα ἀγαθὰ διδόναι τοῖς τέκνοις ὑμῶν, πόσῳ μᾶλλον ὁ πατὴρ ὁ ἐξ οὐρανοῦ δώσει πνεῦμα ἅγιον τοῖς αἰτοῦσιν αὐτόν.*

- **"a fish… a snake; an egg… a scorpion"**:
  - *Snakes and scorpions are the pair of 10:19, trodden underfoot by the seventy.* What is trodden on the way is not what the Father gives.
  - Fish are the truths of the natural (5:6; 9:13). An egg is life still enclosed, a beginning (?).
- **"if you, being evil (πονηροί), know how to give good gifts"**:
  - Evil: "the evil man out of the evil treasure" (6:45); "he is kind to the ungrateful and evil" (6:35).
  - The own is evil, and still knows how to give good gifts. The giving does not come from the own.
- **"how much more will the Father from heaven give the holy spirit to those who ask him"**:
  - The earthly father and the Father from heaven: the natural father left (9:59–60), the Father revealed (10:21–22).
  - What is given from heaven is the holy spirit, the spirit asked for in 11:2.

### 11:14

*Καὶ ἦν ἐκβάλλων δαιμόνιον καὶ αὐτὸ ἦν κωφόν· ἐγένετο δὲ τοῦ δαιμονίου ἐξελθόντος ἐλάλησεν ὁ κωφός.*

- **"a daimon, and it was mute"**: κωφός, deaf or mute. "The deaf (κωφοί) hear" (7:22). A falsity that makes one unable to hear or speak.
- **"when the daimon had gone out, the mute spoke"**: coming out (4:35; 8:2; 8:33). The dead young man "began to speak" (7:15).

### 11:15–16 (11:16 grey)

*τινὲς δὲ ἐξ αὐτῶν εἶπον· Ἐν Βεελζεβοὺλ τῷ ἄρχοντι τῶν δαιμονίων ἐκβάλλει τὰ δαιμόνια· ἕτεροι δὲ πειράζοντες σημεῖον ἐξ οὐρανοῦ ἐζήτουν παρ’ αὐτοῦ.*

- **"by Beelzebul, the ruler (ἄρχοντι) of the daimons"**: the rulers of 3:1; the ruler of the synagogue (8:41). Falsity is given a ruler.
- **"testing, they sought from him a sign from heaven"**:
  - "From heaven": fire from heaven (9:54).
  - *Forward: Herod "hoped to see some sign (σημεῖον) done by him" (23:8), and he answered him nothing (23:9).*
  - A sign is sought, as Herod sought to see (9:9). Seeking for the wrong thing.

### 11:17–18 (11:17 grey)

*αὐτὸς δὲ εἰδὼς αὐτῶν τὰ διανοήματα εἶπεν αὐτοῖς· Πᾶσα βασιλεία ἐφ’ ἑαυτὴν διαμερισθεῖσα ἐρημοῦται, καὶ οἶκος ἐπὶ οἶκον πίπτει. εἰ δὲ καὶ ὁ Σατανᾶς ἐφ’ ἑαυτὸν διεμερίσθη, πῶς σταθήσεται ἡ βασιλεία αὐτοῦ;*

- **"knowing their thoughts"** (διανοήματα): the mind (διάνοια, 10:27), here the thoughts behind the testing. He knows the reasonings (5:22; 6:8; 9:47).
- **"every kingdom divided against itself (ἐφ’ ἑαυτήν) is made desert (ἐρημοῦται)"**:
  - Division. *Forward:* "who made me a divider (μεριστήν)" (12:14); "not peace but division (διαμερισμόν)" (12:51); "take this and divide (διαμερίσατε) it among yourselves" (22:17).
  - The desert (4:42; 8:29; 9:12). *Forward:* "its desolation (ἐρήμωσις) has come near" (21:20).
  - Against itself: the self divided.
- **"house falls on house"**: the house that fell (6:49).
- **"how will his kingdom stand (σταθήσεται)?"**: standing (6:8; 8:44; 9:47). Satan is named here, in the printed text.

### 11:19–20

*εἰ δὲ ἐγὼ ἐν Βεελζεβοὺλ ἐκβάλλω τὰ δαιμόνια, οἱ υἱοὶ ὑμῶν ἐν τίνι ἐκβάλλουσιν; διὰ τοῦτο αὐτοὶ ὑμῶν κριταὶ ἔσονται. εἰ δὲ ἐν δακτύλῳ θεοῦ ἐγὼ ἐκβάλλω τὰ δαιμόνια, ἄρα ἔφθασεν ἐφ’ ὑμᾶς ἡ βασιλεία τοῦ θεοῦ.*

- **"your sons… will be your judges (κριταί)"**:
  - The judges are not imposed from outside. They are one's own: those who do the same thing.
  - *This is the form judgement takes in the printed text:*
    - "do not judge, and you will not be judged" (6:37);
    - "why do you not judge for yourselves what is right?" (12:57);
    - "out of your own mouth I will judge you" (19:22).
- **"by the finger of God"**: the finger is power in its outermost, the smallest part of the hand (?).
  - *Thread of the finger:* the lawyers "do not touch the burdens with one of your fingers" (11:46); "that he may dip the tip of his finger in water and cool my tongue" (16:24).
  - The finger of God frees. The lawyers' finger does not touch.
- **"then the kingdom of God has arrived (ἔφθασεν) upon you"**: "it has come near (ἤγγικεν) upon you" (10:9). Here it has arrived.

### 11:21–22

*ὅταν ὁ ἰσχυρὸς καθωπλισμένος φυλάσσῃ τὴν ἑαυτοῦ αὐλήν, ἐν εἰρήνῃ ἐστὶν τὰ ὑπάρχοντα αὐτοῦ· ἐπὰν δὲ ἰσχυρότερος αὐτοῦ ἐπελθὼν νικήσῃ αὐτόν, τὴν πανοπλίαν αὐτοῦ αἴρει ἐφ’ ᾗ ἐπεποίθει, καὶ τὰ σκῦλα αὐτοῦ διαδίδωσιν.*

- **"the strong man (ἰσχυρός), fully armed, guards (φυλάσσῃ) his own (ἑαυτοῦ) courtyard"**:
  - Strength: "love with your whole strength (ἰσχύϊ)" (10:27). The river "had no strength" against the house on the rock (6:48).
  - Guarding: Legion was "guarded" (φυλασσόμενος) in chains (8:29). *Against it: "blessed are those who hear the word of God and guard (φυλάσσοντες) it" (11:28).*
  - The courtyard (αὐλή). *Forward: the courtyard where Peter denies him (22:55, in the unprinted 22:54–62).*
  - "His own": the own, armed.
- **"his possessions (τὰ ὑπάρχοντα) are in peace"**:
  - Possessions: the women served from their possessions (8:3). *Forward:* "life is not from possessions (ὑπαρχόντων)" (12:15); "he was squandering his possessions" (16:1); "half of my possessions" (19:8).
  - The peace of the own over what it holds. Against it, the peace given to a house (10:5–6).
- **"when one stronger than he comes upon him and conquers him, he takes away (αἴρει) the armour he had trusted in (ἐπεποίθει)"**:
  - *αἴρω:*
    - what one only seems to have is taken (8:18);
    - "take nothing for the way" (9:3);
    - "take up the cross" (9:23).
  - *Trust: "some who trusted (πεποιθότας) in themselves that they were righteous" (18:9).*
  - The armour that the own trusted in is taken off it.
- **"and distributes (διαδίδωσιν) his spoils"**:
  - *Forward: "sell all that you have and give to the poor" (18:22). The printed text has δός ("give"), the adopted Marcionite reading; NA27 has διάδος ("distribute"), the verb of 11:22.*
  - What the own held is given out.

*Here the text says in a parable, at the level of the soul, what it has been doing since 4:31:*
- the own, armed and at peace over its possessions;
- the coming of one stronger;
- the taking away of what it trusted in;
- the giving out of what it held.

### 11:23 (grey)

*ὁ μὴ ὢν μετ’ ἐμοῦ κατ’ ἐμοῦ ἐστιν, καὶ ὁ μὴ συνάγων μετ’ ἐμοῦ σκορπίζει.*

- **"whoever is not with me is against me"**:
  - The test passage 9:50 has the opposite saying: "whoever is not against you is for you".
  - The two are not about the same thing. 9:50 is about someone the disciples would shut out of their group. 11:23 is about the self in relation to him.
  - Toward others, wide. Toward oneself, no middle.
- **"whoever does not gather (συνάγων) with me scatters"**: gathering. *Forward:* "where to gather (συνάξω) my fruits" (12:17–18), the rich man who gathers for himself.

### Test: 11:24–26 (U?)

*Ὅταν τὸ ἀκάθαρτον πνεῦμα ἐξέλθῃ ἀπὸ τοῦ ἀνθρώπου, διέρχεται δι’ ἀνύδρων τόπων ζητοῦν ἀνάπαυσιν, καὶ μὴ εὑρίσκον λέγει· Ὑποστρέψω εἰς τὸν οἶκόν μου ὅθεν ἐξῆλθον· καὶ ἐλθὸν εὑρίσκει σεσαρωμένον καὶ κεκοσμημένον. τότε πορεύεται καὶ παραλαμβάνει ἕτερα πνεύματα πονηρότερα ἑαυτοῦ ἑπτά, καὶ εἰσελθόντα κατοικεῖ ἐκεῖ, καὶ γίνεται τὰ ἔσχατα τοῦ ἀνθρώπου ἐκείνου χείρονα τῶν πρώτων.*

- **"when the unclean spirit has gone out (ἐξέλθῃ)"**: coming out.
- **"it passes through (διέρχεται) waterless places"**:
  - Passing through (4:30; 8:22; 9:6).
  - Waterless: the seed on the rock "dried, for lack of moisture" (8:6). The deserts (8:29).
- **"seeking rest (ἀνάπαυσιν), and not finding"**:
  - Rest: peace "will rest" (ἐπαναπαήσεται) on the son of peace (10:6). *Forward:* "soul… rest (ἀναπαύου), eat, drink" (12:19).
  - Seeking and not finding: against "seek and you will find" (11:9).
- **"I will return (ὑποστρέψω) to my house from which I came out"**: returning (8:37–40; 9:10; 10:17), said by the falsity.
- **"it finds it swept (σεσαρωμένον) and adorned"**:
  - *Forward: the woman "sweeps (σαροῖ) the house and seeks until she finds" (15:8).* There the sweeping is in order to find what was lost. Here the house is swept, and nothing is in it.
  - Adorned (κεκοσμημένον): the world (κόσμος, 9:25).
- **"it takes along seven other spirits more evil than itself"**: "seven daimons had gone out" of Mary Magdalene (8:2). Seven, the whole.
- **"the last state of that man becomes worse than the first"**.

**Verdict: continues.**
- It is the sequel of 11:21–22. The armour is taken and the spoils distributed. If the house is then left swept and empty, what went out comes back with more.
- It is the inner law of the new skins (5:37–38): what is emptied must be filled.
- 11:28 answers it: the house is kept by hearing the word and guarding it.
- 11:23 → 11:27 runs ("as he was saying these things…"), so it does not close a gap.

### 11:27–28

*ἐπάρασά τις φωνὴν γυνὴ ἐκ τοῦ ὄχλου εἶπεν αὐτῷ· Μακαρία ἡ κοιλία ἡ βαστάσασά σε καὶ μαστοὶ οὓς ἐθήλασας· αὐτὸς δὲ εἶπεν· Μενοῦν μακάριοι οἱ ἀκούοντες τὸν λόγον τοῦ θεοῦ καὶ φυλάσσοντες.*

- **"blessed the womb that carried (βαστάσασα) you"**:
  - The natural origin blessed. Nazareth, "where he had been brought up" (4:16); mother and brothers standing outside (8:20).
  - Carrying (βαστάζω): the bearers of the bier (7:14); "carry no purse" (10:4); "burdens hard to carry" (11:46). *Forward:* the man carrying a jar of water (22:10).
- **"blessed rather those who hear the word of God and guard (φυλάσσοντες) it"**:
  - 8:21 again: "my mother and brothers are those who hear the word and do it".
  - *Guarding:* the strong man guards his court (11:21); the hearer guards the word.
  - What the own guarded is taken. What the hearer guards is kept (10:42: "it will not be taken away").

### 11:29

*Τῶν δὲ ὄχλων ἐπαθροιζομένων ἤρξατο λέγειν· Ἡ γενεὰ αὕτη γενεὰ πονηρά ἐστιν· σημεῖον ζητεῖ, καὶ σημεῖον οὐ δοθήσεται αὐτῇ.*

- **"this generation is an evil generation"**: "O faithless generation" (9:41).
- **"it seeks a sign, and a sign will not be given to it"**:
  - Seeking the wrong thing (11:16).
  - "Will not be given" (οὐ δοθήσεται) is the reverse of "ask, and it will be given" (δοθήσεται, 11:9). What is asked in need is given; what is sought in testing is not.
  - *The sign that is given in the printed text is at the end, unsought:* "great signs from heaven" (21:11); "signs in sun and moon and stars" (21:25). Herod, who hoped for a sign, gets no answer (23:8–9).

### Test: 11:29b–32 (attested omission and A−)

*εἰ μὴ τὸ σημεῖον Ἰωνᾶ. καθὼς γὰρ ἐγένετο Ἰωνᾶς τοῖς Νινευίταις σημεῖον, οὕτως ἔσται καὶ ὁ υἱὸς τοῦ ἀνθρώπου τῇ γενεᾷ ταύτῃ. βασίλισσα νότου ἐγερθήσεται ἐν τῇ κρίσει μετὰ τῶν ἀνδρῶν τῆς γενεᾶς ταύτης καὶ κατακρινεῖ αὐτούς· ὅτι ἦλθεν ἐκ τῶν περάτων τῆς γῆς ἀκοῦσαι τὴν σοφίαν Σολομῶνος, καὶ ἰδοὺ πλεῖον Σολομῶνος ὧδε. ἄνδρες Νινευῖται ἀναστήσονται ἐν τῇ κρίσει μετὰ τῆς γενεᾶς ταύτης καὶ κατακρινοῦσιν αὐτήν· ὅτι μετενόησαν εἰς τὸ κήρυγμα Ἰωνᾶ, καὶ ἰδοὺ πλεῖον Ἰωνᾶ ὧδε.*

- **"except the sign of Jonah… so will the Son of Man be to this generation"**:
  - The sign that is given is the Son of Man himself.
  - Jonah is the one who went down into the deep and came up (?). That would join "after three days be raised" (9:22).
- **"the queen of the south will be raised (ἐγερθήσεται)… the men of Nineveh will rise (ἀναστήσονται)"**: *both rising verbs, said of the dead rising "in the judgement".* Rising "from the dead" had been in the text only as Herod's rumour (9:7).
- **"and will condemn (κατακρινεῖ) them"**:
  - The judges are those who heard and turned. *This is the form of 11:19: "your sons will be your judges".* The judgement is not brought from outside.
- **"she came from the ends of the earth to hear the wisdom"**: hearing (11:28); the earth; wisdom (σοφία; "hidden from the wise", 10:21).
- **"they repented at the proclamation (κήρυγμα) of Jonah"**:
  - Proclaiming (κηρύσσω: 4:44; 8:1; 8:39; 9:2) and repenting.
  - *Forward, in the printed text: "and repentance… be proclaimed (κηρυχθῆναι) in his name to all the nations" (24:47).* The Ninevites are a nation.
- **"behold, more than Solomon… more than Jonah is here"**: greatness (7:28; 9:46–48).

**Verdict: continues.**
- Its words take up the rising verbs and the form of judgement of 11:19, and they reach 24:47 (proclamation and repentance to the nations).
- *The pericope pass (`proto_luke_in_correspondence.md`) marked 11:30–32 as an inserted passage that breaks. Read by its words, it does not break. That earlier verdict was drawn from the letter.*
- 11:29 → 11:33 runs: no sign is given to the seekers; the lamp is not hidden from those who enter. So it does not close a gap.

### 11:33

*Οὐδεὶς λύχνον ἅψας εἰς κρύπτην τίθησιν οὐδὲ ὑπὸ τὸν μόδιον ἀλλ’ ἐπὶ τὴν λυχνίαν, ἵνα οἱ εἰσπορευόμενοι τὸ φῶς βλέπωσιν.*

- **"no one puts a lamp in a hidden place (κρύπτην)"**: 8:16 again, now with "hidden" (κρυπτός, 8:17; 10:21).
- **"nor under the measure (μόδιον)"**: the measure (6:38) is for giving, not for hiding the light under.
- **"so that those coming in (εἰσπορευόμενοι) may see the light"**:
  - The light is for those who come in.
  - No sign is given to the seekers outside (11:29), but the light is not hidden from those who enter.
  - *Against it: "you did not enter, and you hindered those entering (εἰσερχομένους)" (11:52).*

### Test: 11:34–36 (U?)

*ὁ λύχνος τοῦ σώματός ἐστιν ὁ ὀφθαλμός σου. ὅταν ὁ ὀφθαλμός σου ἁπλοῦς ᾖ, καὶ ὅλον τὸ σῶμά σου φωτεινόν ἐστιν· ἐπὰν δὲ πονηρὸς ᾖ, καὶ τὸ σῶμά σου σκοτεινόν. σκόπει οὖν μὴ τὸ φῶς τὸ ἐν σοὶ σκότος ἐστίν. εἰ οὖν τὸ σῶμά σου ὅλον φωτεινόν, μὴ ἔχον μέρος τι σκοτεινόν, ἔσται φωτεινὸν ὅλον ὡς ὅταν ὁ λύχνος τῇ ἀστραπῇ φωτίζῃ σε.*

- **"the lamp of the body is your eye"**:
  - The lamp of 11:33, turned inward.
  - The eye: the beam "in your own eye" (6:41–42); "blessed are the eyes" (10:23). *Forward:* the eyes held, then opened (24:16, 24:31).
- **"when your eye is single (ἁπλοῦς)… when it is evil (πονηρός)"**: a single eye looks one way (?). Evil: "being evil" (11:13); "an evil generation" (11:29).
- **"see whether the light in you is darkness"**: the light inside (ἐν σοί), against the inside full of plunder (11:39).
- **"as when the lamp lights you with its flash (ἀστραπῇ)"**: *the flashing of 9:29 (ἐξαστράπτων), 17:24 and 24:4.*

**Verdict: continues.**
- It carries the lamp of 11:33 inward to the "inside" (ἔσωθεν) of 11:39–41: lamp for those entering → light in you → inside of the cup.
- This is the turn the text made at 6:42, from the other's eye to one's own.
- 11:33 → 11:37 runs, so it does not close a gap. Without it, the turn inward is made at 11:39 alone.

### 11:37–38

*Ἐν δὲ τῷ λαλῆσαι ἐρωτᾷ αὐτὸν Φαρισαῖος ὅπως ἀριστήσῃ παρ’ αὐτῷ· εἰσελθὼν δὲ ἀνέπεσεν. ὁ δὲ Φαρισαῖος ἤρξατο διακρινόμενος ἐν ἑαυτῷ λέγειν διὰ τί οὐ πρῶτον ἐβαπτίσθη πρὸ τοῦ ἀρίστου.*

- **"entering, he reclined (ἀνέπεσεν)"**: ἀνα-πίπτω, "fall back", the posture at table. *Forward: "when the hour came, he reclined (ἀνέπεσεν), and the apostles with him" (22:14).*
- **"the Pharisee began debating within himself (ἐν ἑαυτῷ)"**:
  - The adopted Marcionite (D) wording. It joins the Pharisee at the first meal: "he said within himself (ἐν ἑαυτῷ)" (7:39).
  - NA27's "seeing, he marvelled" joins the thread of marvelling.
  - **Neutral.**
- **"why he did not first (πρῶτον) wash (ἐβαπτίσθη)"**:
  - "First": the own's "first" (9:59, 9:61), against the "first" of peace (10:5).
  - Washing as an outward act.
  - *Forward:* "I have a baptism (βάπτισμα) to be baptized with, and how I am held fast (συνέχομαι) until it is accomplished!" (12:50). The washing that matters is his own passage.

### 11:39–41

*Νῦν ὑμεῖς οἱ Φαρισαῖοι τὸ ἔξωθεν τοῦ ποτηρίου καὶ τοῦ πίνακος καθαρίζετε, τὸ δὲ ἔσωθεν ὑμῶν γέμει ἁρπαγῆς καὶ πονηρίας. ἄφρονες, οὐχ ὁ ποιήσας τὸ ἔξωθεν καὶ τὸ ἔσωθεν ἐποίησεν; πλὴν τὰ ἐνόντα δότε ἐλεημοσύνην, καὶ ἰδοὺ πάντα καθαρὰ ὑμῖν ἐστιν.*

- **"you cleanse (καθαρίζετε) the outside (ἔξωθεν) of the cup and the dish"**:
  - Cleansing: the leper (5:12–14); "lepers are cleansed" (7:22).
  - The outside: "outside" (ἔξω, 4:29; 8:20).
  - The cup is a vessel (σκεῦος, 8:16; the skins, 5:37). *Forward:* "this cup is the new covenant in my blood" (22:20); "Father, if you will, remove this cup from me" (22:42).
- **"but your inside (ἔσωθεν) is full (γέμει) of plunder and evil"**:
  - The inside: the one inside who rises to give (11:7).
  - Filling: the nets filled (5:7), the boat filling (8:23), the days filling (9:51). Here the inside is filled with plunder.
- **"did not the one who made the outside also make the inside?"**:
  - One maker, of both.
  - *This is the sentence in the printed text against separating the outer from the inner. It is the same relation the reading found strained by "Lord of heaven" without "and of the earth" (10:21).*
- **"give as alms (ἐλεημοσύνην) the things within (τὰ ἐνόντα), and see, all things are clean for you"**:
  - The inside is cleansed by giving it out.
  - Alms is mercy (ἔλεος: "the one who did mercy", 10:37, test).
  - This is 11:22 again, from the inside: the spoils distributed.

### 11:42–44 (11:44 grey)

*Ἀλλὰ οὐαὶ ὑμῖν τοῖς Φαρισαίοις, ὅτι ἀποδεκατοῦτε τὸ ἡδύοσμον καὶ τὸ πήγανον καὶ πᾶν λάχανον, καὶ παρέρχεσθε τὴν κλῆσιν καὶ τὴν ἀγάπην τοῦ θεοῦ· ταῦτα δὲ ἔδει ποιῆσαι κἀκεῖνα μὴ παρεῖναι. οὐαὶ ὑμῖν τοῖς Φαρισαίοις, ὅτι ἀγαπᾶτε τὴν πρωτοκαθεδρίαν ἐν ταῖς συναγωγαῖς καὶ τοὺς ἀσπασμοὺς ἐν ταῖς ἀγοραῖς. οὐαὶ ὑμῖν, ὅτι ἐστὲ ὡς τὰ μνημεῖα τὰ ἄδηλα, καὶ οἱ ἄνθρωποι οἱ περιπατοῦντες ἐπάνω οὐκ οἴδασιν.*

- **"you tithe mint and rue"**: *forward: the Pharisee in the temple, "I tithe (ἀποδεκατῶ) all that I acquire" (18:12).*
- **"and pass by (παρέρχεσθε) the calling and the love of God"**:
  - Passing by: the priest and the Levite "passed by on the other side" (10:31–32, test).
  - "The calling" (κλῆσιν) is the adopted Marcionite reading; NA27 has "justice" (κρίσιν). By their words:
    - "calling" joins "call the poor" (14:13) and "he called many" (14:16);
    - "justice" joins the judging of 6:37, 11:19 and 12:57.
  - **Neutral.**
- **"you love the first seat (πρωτοκαθεδρίαν)"**: "first" again. Love turned to the first place, against loving God with the whole heart (10:27). *The first places at table are the subject of the unprinted 14:7–11.*
- **"the greetings in the marketplaces"**: "greet no one along the way" (10:4).
- **"you are like unseen tombs (μνημεῖα), and people walking over them do not know"**:
  - The tombs: Legion lived among them (8:27). *Forward: the tomb of the end (24:2, 24:9, 24:22).*
  - Hidden death: "nothing hidden that will not become manifest" (8:17).

### 11:45–46 (11:45 grey)

*Διδάσκαλε, ταῦτα λέγων καὶ ἡμᾶς ὑβρίζεις. ὁ δὲ εἶπεν· Καὶ ὑμῖν τοῖς νομικοῖς οὐαί, ὅτι φορτίζετε τοὺς ἀνθρώπους φορτία δυσβάστακτα, καὶ αὐτοὶ ἑνὶ τῶν δακτύλων ὑμῶν οὐ προσψαύετε τοῖς φορτίοις.*

- **"burdens hard to carry"** (δυσβάστακτα): carrying (11:27).
- **"you do not touch the burdens with one of your fingers"**:
  - *The finger of God casts out daimons (11:20). The lawyers' finger does not touch.*
  - Touching (5:13; 8:44–47).

### 11:47–48

*οὐαὶ ὑμῖν, ὅτι οἰκοδομεῖτε τὰ μνημεῖα τῶν προφητῶν οἱ δὲ πατέρες ὑμῶν ἀπέκτειναν αὐτούς. ἄρα μάρτυρές ἐστε καὶ συνευδοκεῖτε τοῖς ἔργοις τῶν πατέρων ὑμῶν, ὅτι αὐτοὶ μὲν ἀπέκτειναν αὐτοὺς ὑμεῖς δὲ οἰκοδομεῖτε.*

- **"you build (οἰκοδομεῖτε) the tombs of the prophets"**:
  - *Building:* the house on the rock and the house on the earth (6:48–49). *Forward:* "I will pull down my barns and build (οἰκοδομήσω) greater" (12:18).
  - Here what is built is tombs: building over the dead.
- **"your fathers killed them"**:
  - "Their fathers did the same to the prophets" (6:23, 6:26).
  - "The Son of Man must… be killed" (9:22).
  - The fathers: the natural origin, left to bury its dead (9:59–60).
- **"you are witnesses and you approve (συνευδοκεῖτε)"**: approving, εὐδοκία, the word of the Father's good pleasure (10:21). Their good pleasure is in the works of the fathers.

### Test: 11:49–51 (A−)

*διὰ τοῦτο καὶ ἡ σοφία τοῦ θεοῦ εἶπεν· Ἀποστελῶ εἰς αὐτοὺς προφήτας καὶ ἀποστόλους, καὶ ἐξ αὐτῶν ἀποκτενοῦσιν καὶ διώξουσιν, ἵνα ἐκζητηθῇ τὸ αἷμα πάντων τῶν προφητῶν τὸ ἐκκεχυμένον ἀπὸ καταβολῆς κόσμου ἀπὸ τῆς γενεᾶς ταύτης, ἀπὸ αἵματος Ἅβελ ἕως αἵματος Ζαχαρίου τοῦ ἀπολομένου μεταξὺ τοῦ θυσιαστηρίου καὶ τοῦ οἴκου· ναί, λέγω ὑμῖν, ἐκζητηθήσεται ἀπὸ τῆς γενεᾶς ταύτης.*

- **"therefore the Wisdom of God said"**: it follows straight on from 11:47–48 ("therefore"). Wisdom (10:21; *forward:* "I will give you a mouth and wisdom", 21:15).
- **"I will send (ἀποστελῶ) them prophets and apostles, and some of them they will kill and persecute (διώξουσιν)"**:
  - Sending: "I send you as lambs in the midst of wolves" (10:3). Apostles (6:13; 9:10).
  - *Forward:* "they will lay their hands on you and persecute (διώξουσιν) you" (21:12).
- **"the blood of all the prophets, poured out (ἐκκεχυμένον)"**:
  - *Pouring:* the new wine "poured out" from old skins (5:37); the measure "poured over" (6:38).
  - *Forward: "the new covenant in my blood, poured out (ἐκχυννόμενον) for you" (22:20).* The blood of the prophets poured out, and his blood poured out: one verb.
  - Blood is truth (8:43–44). Truth's blood shed is truth killed (?).
- **"from the foundation (καταβολῆς, 'down-casting') of the world"**: the world (9:25).
- **"Zechariah, who perished (ἀπολομένου) between the altar and the house"**: perishing (4:34; 8:24; 9:24).
- **"it will be sought out (ἐκζητηθήσεται) from this generation"**:
  - Seeking (11:9–10; 11:16; 11:29).
  - The generation that approves the killing (11:48) takes on what it approves. That is the form of 11:19 and 11:31–32, and the measure of 6:38.

**Verdict: continues.**
- It follows 11:47–48 with "therefore". Its words reach 10:3 (sending among wolves), 21:12 (persecution) and 22:20 (blood poured out).
- *The pericope pass marked 11:49–51 as an inserted passage that breaks. Read by its words, it does not.*
- 11:48 → 11:52 runs, so it does not close a gap.

### 11:52

*οὐαὶ ὑμῖν τοῖς νομικοῖς, ὅτι ἤρατε τὴν κλεῖδα τῆς γνώσεως· αὐτοὶ οὐκ εἰσήλθατε καὶ τοὺς εἰσερχομένους ἐκωλύσατε.*

- **"you took away (ἤρατε) the key of knowledge"**:
  - αἴρω again: the armour taken from the strong man (11:22).
  - Here the lawyers take away the key.
  - The key opens the door: the door shut (11:7); "knock, and it will be opened" (11:9–10). Knowledge: knowing the Father and the son (10:22).
- **"you did not enter yourselves, and you hindered (ἐκωλύσατε) those entering (εἰσερχομένους)"**:
  - The light is for "those coming in" (11:33).
  - κωλύω: "do not forbid" (9:50, test); "do not hinder" (18:16).

### Test: 11:53–54 (U?)

*Κἀκεῖθεν ἐξελθόντος αὐτοῦ ἤρξαντο οἱ γραμματεῖς καὶ οἱ Φαρισαῖοι δεινῶς ἐνέχειν καὶ ἀποστοματίζειν αὐτὸν περὶ πλειόνων, ἐνεδρεύοντες αὐτὸν θηρεῦσαί τι ἐκ τοῦ στόματος αὐτοῦ.*

- **"to press hard (ἐνέχειν)"**: holding against him, of the family of holding fast (συνέχω, 8:37, 8:45; 12:50).
- **"lying in wait to catch (θηρεῦσαι) something out of his mouth"**:
  - *Forward:* "watching, they sent spies… so that they might take hold of his word" (20:20); "they could not take hold of his saying" (20:26); "we have heard it ourselves from his mouth (στόματος)" (22:71).
  - The mouth: "out of the overflow of the heart the mouth speaks" (6:45).

**Verdict: continues.** It begins the lying in wait that runs to 20:20 and 22:71. 11:52 → 12:1 runs ("in these circumstances…"), so it does not close a gap.

### What came out of the reading of chapter 9

**Rising is for giving.**
- In the parable of the friend at midnight, both rising verbs (ἀνίστημι, ἐγείρω) are said of the one inside who gets up to give bread (11:7–8).
- Since 4:39 (test), every rising in this text has been toward something done: serving, following, walking, standing, giving.
- The door, the one inside, the rising, the knocking and the opening all return, reversed, at 13:24–25.

**The prayer.**
- Father; the spirit or the name; the kingdom coming down.
- Bread given daily, in the words of the cross taken up daily (9:23).
- Letting go as we let go.
- Not being carried into temptation.

**Ask and it will be given; seek; knock.**
- What the Father gives is not the snake and the scorpion trodden on the way (10:19), but the holy spirit.
- The own is evil and still gives good gifts. The good does not come from it.

**The strong man (11:21–22) is the mechanic of the soul, said outright.**
- The own, armed, guards its own court, and its possessions are at peace.
- One stronger comes, takes away the armour it trusted in, and distributes the spoils.
- It is followed by:
  - "whoever does not gather with me scatters";
  - (in the test passage 11:24–26) the house swept and empty, reoccupied by worse;
  - "blessed are those who hear the word and guard it".
- What the own guards is taken. What the hearer guards is kept.

**Inside and outside.**
- The lamp is not hidden, nor put under the measure.
- (In the test passage 11:34–36) the lamp of the body is the eye, and the light in you.
- The outside of the cup is cleansed while the inside is full of plunder.
- "Did not the one who made the outside make the inside also?" The inside is cleansed by giving it out as alms.
- The printed text here says outright that the outer and the inner have one maker. That is why the reading weighed "Lord of heaven" without "and of the earth" as a strain (10:21).

**Judgement in this text is the measure returning, and the judges are one's own.**
- "Your sons will be your judges" (11:19). The verb κρίνω is printed (6:37; 12:57; 19:22).
- The noun κρίσις ("judgement", "justice") does not occur anywhere in the printed text. Its places are:
  - 10:14 (test);
  - 11:31–32 (test);
  - 11:42, where the adopted reading has κλῆσις.
- The passages not printed in this chapter and the last that speak of judgement — 10:12–15; 11:29b–32; 11:49–51 — state it in the printed text's own form:
  - the city that lifts itself up goes down;
  - those who heard condemn those who did not;
  - the generation that approves the killing takes on the blood.
- *What this pattern means for the history of the text (excision from, or addition to, an earlier form) is a separate question, which the reading does not decide.*
- By their words, the passages continue the line.

**The two "breaks" of the pericope pass do not hold.** 11:30–32 and 11:49–51 were marked there as inserted passages that break. Read word by word, both continue:
- the first through the rising verbs, the form of judgement, and the proclamation to the nations (24:47);
- the second through "poured out", which joins the blood of the prophets to "my blood poured out for you" (22:20).

The earlier verdicts were drawn from the letter.

**The key taken away.** The lawyers take away the key of knowledge, do not enter, and hinder those entering. The lamp is for those who enter.

### Threads, carried forward and added

- rising to give (→ 13:25);
- the door, knocking, opening (→ 13:24–25; 24:31);
- inside and outside (ἔσωθεν / ἔξωθεν), one maker;
- daily (the cross, the bread);
- letting go and owing (→ 16:5–7);
- temptation (→ 22:40, 22:46);
- midnight; the midst of the night;
- setting before (παρατίθημι);
- seeking: for holding, for a sign, for finding (→ 19:10; 24:5);
- the holy spirit given;
- snakes and scorpions;
- the finger (of God, of the lawyers, → 16:24);
- the strong man: guarding, possessions, trust (→ 18:9), the armour taken, the spoils distributed (→ 18:22);
- gathering and scattering (→ 12:17–18);
- the swept house (→ 15:8);
- guarding the word;
- signs (→ 21:11, 21:25; 23:8–9);
- the lamp and the measure;
- the eye (→ 24:16, 24:31);
- the cup (→ 22:17–20, 22:42);
- alms and mercy;
- the first seat (→ 14:7–11, unprinted);
- tombs (→ 24:2–22);
- building (→ 12:18);
- the fathers who killed the prophets (6:23, 6:26);
- blood poured out (→ 22:20);
- the key and entering (→ 13:24; 18:17; 24:26);
- lying in wait for his mouth (→ 20:20–26; 22:71);
- judgement as one's own measure;
- κρίσις absent from the printed text.

---

## Chapter 10

*12:1–59. Grey: 12:15, 12:17–18, 12:21, 12:25–26, 12:29, 12:50, 12:52, 12:54–55. Not printed: 12:6 (A−), 12:7 (U−), 12:28a (attested omission), 12:33–34 (U?). Adopted Marcionite wordings: 12:5 βαλεῖν; 12:8–9 "before God" without "the angels of"; 12:27 "neither spin nor weave"; 12:31 "the kingdom of God"; 12:32 "the Father"; 12:38 "in the evening watch"; 12:53 singular "will be divided"; 12:56 "heaven and earth"; 12:58 word order.*

### 12:1

*Ἐν οἷς ἐπισυναχθεισῶν τῶν μυριάδων τοῦ ὄχλου, ὥστε καταπατεῖν ἀλλήλους, ἤρξατο λέγειν πρὸς τοὺς μαθητὰς αὐτοῦ πρῶτον· Προσέχετε ἑαυτοῖς ἀπὸ τῆς ζύμης, ἥτις ἐστὶν ὑπόκρισις, τῶν Φαρισαίων.*

- **"so that they trampled (καταπατεῖν) one another"**: treading down.
  - The seed by the way "was trodden down" (κατεπατήθη, 8:5). The seventy tread on snakes (10:19).
  - Here the crowd treads on itself.
- **"first, to his disciples"**: "first" (πρῶτον). This "first" is his, against the own's "first" (9:59, 9:61).
- **"beware for yourselves (ἑαυτοῖς) of the leaven (ζύμης)"**:
  - Leaven works hidden through the whole lump.
  - *Forward: "it is like leaven which a woman took and hid (ἐνέκρυψεν) in three measures of flour until all was leavened" (13:21);* the feast of unleavened bread (22:1, 22:7).
  - Leaven, like many words in this text, works both ways: falsity spreading hidden, or the kingdom hidden until the whole is leavened.
- **"which is hypocrisy"** (ὑπόκρισις): "hypocrite, first cast out the beam" (6:42). The outside that differs from the inside (11:39). *Forward:* 12:56; 13:15; "spies pretending (ὑποκρινομένους) to be righteous" (20:20).

### 12:2–3

*οὐδὲν δὲ συγκεκαλυμμένον ἐστὶν ὃ οὐκ ἀποκαλυφθήσεται, καὶ κρυπτὸν ὃ οὐ γνωσθήσεται. ἀνθ’ ὧν ὅσα ἐν τῇ σκοτίᾳ εἴπατε ἐν τῷ φωτὶ ἀκουσθήσεται, καὶ ὃ πρὸς τὸ οὖς ἐλαλήσατε ἐν τοῖς ταμείοις κηρυχθήσεται ἐπὶ τῶν δωμάτων.*

- **"nothing covered (συγκεκαλυμμένον) that will not be uncovered (ἀποκαλυφθήσεται), nor hidden (κρυπτόν) that will not be known"**:
  - *The law of veiling, the third time:* 8:17 (stated); 9:45 (the veil); 10:21 (thanks for the uncovering); here.
  - *Forward: "on the day the Son of Man is revealed (ἀποκαλύπτεται)" (17:30).*
  - The uncovering of the hidden runs to the uncovering of the Son of Man.
- **"what you said in the dark will be heard in the light"**: the light of the lamp (8:16; 11:33).
- **"what you whispered in the inner rooms (ταμείοις) will be proclaimed on the housetops (δωμάτων)"**:
  - From the innermost room to the highest part of the house.
  - The roof: the friends "went up onto the roof" (ἀναβάντες ἐπὶ τὸ δῶμα) to let the paralysed man down (5:19). *Forward:* "whoever is on the roof… let him not come down (μὴ καταβάτω)" (17:31).
  - The inner room: the ravens "have no inner room (ταμεῖον) nor barn" (12:24).

### 12:4–5

*Λέγω δὲ ὑμῖν τοῖς φίλοις μου, μὴ φοβηθῆτε ἀπὸ τῶν ἀποκτεινόντων τὸ σῶμα καὶ μετὰ ταῦτα μὴ ἐχόντων περισσότερόν τι ποιῆσαι. ὑποδείξω δὲ ὑμῖν τίνα φοβηθῆτε· φοβήθητε τὸν μετὰ τὸ ἀποκτεῖναι ἔχοντα ἐξουσίαν βαλεῖν εἰς γέενναν· ναί, λέγω ὑμῖν, τοῦτον φοβήθητε.*

- **"to you, my friends"**: the friend at midnight (11:5–8). The disciples are now called friends.
- **"do not fear those who kill the body (σῶμα)"**:
  - Fear (5:10; 8:25; 8:35–37; 8:50; 9:34; 9:45).
  - The body: *forward:* "this is my body" (22:19); his body taken down, laid, not found (23:52, 23:55; 24:3, 24:23).
- **"and after that have nothing more (περισσότερον) to do"**: the overflow (περισσεύω, 9:17; 6:45). Killing has no overflow.
- **"fear the one who, after killing, has authority (ἐξουσίαν) to cast (βαλεῖν) into Gehenna"**:
  - Authority (4:36; 5:24; 9:1; 10:19). The fear is moved from what reaches the body to what reaches beyond it.
  - βαλεῖν without the prefix is the adopted Marcionite reading. It joins "I came to cast (βαλεῖν) fire on the earth" (12:49) and "cast (βαλεῖ) you into prison" (12:58). **Neutral.**

### Test: 12:6–7 (12:6 A−; 12:7 U−)

*οὐχὶ πέντε στρουθία πωλοῦνται ἀσσαρίων δύο; καὶ ἓν ἐξ αὐτῶν οὐκ ἔστιν ἐπιλελησμένον ἐνώπιον τοῦ θεοῦ. ἀλλὰ καὶ αἱ τρίχες τῆς κεφαλῆς ὑμῶν πᾶσαι ἠρίθμηνται· μὴ φοβεῖσθε· πολλῶν στρουθίων διαφέρετε.*

- **"five sparrows sold (πωλοῦνται)"**:
  - Birds (8:5; 9:58; 12:24). Five (9:13).
  - Selling: "sell your possessions" (12:33, test); "sell all that you have" (18:22).
- **"not one of them is forgotten before God (ἐνώπιον τοῦ θεοῦ)"**: *"before God" is the phrase of the printed 12:8–9.* Forgetting, against remembering. *Forward:* "do this in remembrance" (22:19); "remember… and they remembered" (24:6, 24:8).
- **"the hairs of your head (αἱ τρίχες τῆς κεφαλῆς) are all numbered"**:
  - *The woman wiped his feet "with the hairs of her head" (ταῖς θριξὶν τῆς κεφαλῆς, 7:38, 7:44).*
  - The head: "the Son of Man has nowhere to lay his head" (9:58).
  - The least and outermost of a person is counted.
- **"do not fear; you are worth more (διαφέρετε) than many sparrows"**:
  - *The words of the printed 12:24: "how much more are you worth (διαφέρετε) than the birds".*
  - "Do not fear": 12:32.

**Verdict: continues.**
- Its words are in the threads of the printed text around it: before God (12:8–9), worth more than the birds (12:24), do not fear (12:32), the hairs of the head (7:38).
- It turns the fear of 12:4–5 into trust before 12:8.
- 12:5 → 12:8 runs (fear the one with authority → acknowledging before God), and the "do not fear" comes at 12:32. So it does not close a gap.

### 12:8–9

*πᾶς ὃς ἂν ὁμολογήσῃ ἐν ἐμοὶ ἔμπροσθεν τῶν ἀνθρώπων, καὶ ὁ υἱὸς τοῦ ἀνθρώπου ὁμολογήσει ἐν αὐτῷ ἔμπροσθεν τοῦ θεοῦ· ὁ δὲ ἀρνησάμενός με ἐνώπιον τῶν ἀνθρώπων ἀπαρνηθήσεται ἐνώπιον τοῦ θεοῦ.*

- **"whoever acknowledges in me… the Son of Man will acknowledge in him"**: the measure (6:38). "In me" and "in him": being in each other.
- **"before men… before God"**: before the natural and before the Divine.
  - "Before God", without "the angels of", is the adopted Marcionite reading.
  - "The angels of God" occurs in the printed text: "joy before the angels of God" (15:10).
  - **Neutral.**
- **"whoever denies (ἀρνησάμενος) me"**: denial.
  - All denied that they had touched (8:45). "Let him deny himself" (9:23).
  - *Forward:* "you will deny three times that you know me" (22:34).
  - Denying the self, or denying him: the choice is between those two.

### 12:10–12

*καὶ πᾶς ὃς ἐρεῖ λόγον εἰς τὸν υἱὸν τοῦ ἀνθρώπου, ἀφεθήσεται αὐτῷ τῷ δὲ εἰς τὸ ἅγιον πνεῦμα βλασφημήσαντι οὐκ ἀφεθήσεται. ὅταν δὲ εἰσφέρωσιν ὑμᾶς ἐπὶ τὰς συναγωγὰς καὶ τὰς ἀρχὰς καὶ τὰς ἐξουσίας, μὴ μεριμνήσητε πῶς ἢ τί ἀπολογήσησθε ἢ τί εἴπητε· τὸ γὰρ ἅγιον πνεῦμα διδάξει ὑμᾶς ἐν αὐτῇ τῇ ὥρᾳ ἃ δεῖ εἰπεῖν.*

- **"a word against the Son of Man will be let go (ἀφεθήσεται)"**: letting go (5:20–24; 7:47–49; 11:4).
- **"blasphemy against the holy spirit will not be let go"**:
  - Blasphemy: "who is this who speaks blasphemies?" (5:21). *Forward:* "blaspheming, they said many other things against him" (22:65).
  - The holy spirit: what the Father gives to those who ask (11:13).
  - What is said against the outward form is let go. What is said against what is being given from within is not, because it refuses the giving.
- **"when they bring you in (εἰσφέρωσιν) before synagogues and rulers (ἀρχάς)"**:
  - "Do not bring us into (εἰσενέγκῃς) temptation" (11:4).
  - *Forward:* "to hand him over to the rule (ἀρχῇ) and authority of the governor" (20:20).
- **"do not be anxious (μεριμνήσητε)"**: anxiety (10:41, test; 12:22–26). *Forward:* "do not prepare beforehand to defend yourselves" (21:14).
- **"the holy spirit will teach you in that very hour what you must (δεῖ) say"**: "must" (9:22). The holy spirit given (11:13) teaches.

### 12:13–14

*Διδάσκαλε, εἰπὲ τῷ ἀδελφῷ μου μερίσασθαι μετ’ ἐμοῦ τὴν κληρονομίαν. ὁ δὲ εἶπεν αὐτῷ· Ἄνθρωπε, τίς με κατέστησεν κριτὴν ἢ μεριστὴν ἐφ’ ὑμᾶς;*

- **"tell my brother to divide (μερίσασθαι) the inheritance with me"**:
  - Inheritance: "what must I do to inherit life?" (10:25). Here the inheritance is property.
  - Division: the kingdom divided against itself (11:17).
- **"who set me as judge (κριτήν) or divider (μεριστήν) over you?"**:
  - He refuses to judge or divide the own's share.
  - *Yet at 12:51 he says he came to bring division (διαμερισμόν).* He does not divide the inheritance between brothers. He divides the house within (12:52–53).

### 12:15 (grey)

*Ὁρᾶτε καὶ φυλάσσεσθε ἀπὸ πάσης πλεονεξίας, ὅτι οὐκ ἐν τῷ περισσεύειν τινὶ ἡ ζωὴ αὐτοῦ ἐστιν ἐκ τῶν ὑπαρχόντων αὐτῷ.*

- **"guard yourselves (φυλάσσεσθε) from all greed"**:
  - Guarding (11:21: the strong man guards his court; 11:28: the hearers guard the word). Here one guards oneself against having more.
  - Greed (πλεονεξία) is "having more".
- **"not in abounding (περισσεύειν) is one's life (ζωή) from one's possessions (ὑπαρχόντων)"**:
  - *The overflow:* the overflow taken up (9:17) is the overflow of what was given. Abounding in possessions is not life.
  - Life (10:25, 10:28): from doing, not from having.
  - Possessions: the strong man's possessions at peace (11:21).

### 12:16–18 (12:17–18 grey)

*Ἀνθρώπου τινὸς πλουσίου εὐφόρησεν ἡ χώρα. καὶ διελογίζετο ἐν ἑαυτῷ λέγων· Τί ποιήσω, ὅτι οὐκ ἔχω ποῦ συνάξω τοὺς καρπούς μου; καὶ εἶπεν· Τοῦτο ποιήσω· καθελῶ μου τὰς ἀποθήκας καὶ μείζονας οἰκοδομήσω, καὶ συνάξω ἐκεῖ πάντα τὸν σῖτον καὶ τὰ ἀγαθά μου,*

- **"a rich (πλουσίου) man's land bore well"**: "woe to you who are rich (πλουσίοις)" (6:24). *Forward:* the rich man (16:19); "he was very rich" (18:23); Zacchaeus, rich (19:2).
- **"he reasoned within himself (ἐν ἑαυτῷ)"**: the reasonings (5:21–22; 9:46–47); "within himself" (7:39; 11:38).
- **"What shall I do (Τί ποιήσω)?"**:
  - The lawyer: "what must I do (τί ποιήσας) to inherit life?" (10:25).
  - *Forward:* the steward, "what shall I do?" (16:3).
- **"where to gather (συνάξω) my fruits"**:
  - "Whoever does not gather (συνάγων) with me scatters" (11:23). He gathers for himself.
  - Fruit: the good earth bore a hundredfold (8:8); a tree is known by its fruit (6:43–44).
- **"I will pull down (καθελῶ) my barns and build greater (μείζονας)"**:
  - *καθαιρέω, "take down": the only other place is 23:53, where Joseph, "taking him down (καθελών)", laid him in a tomb.*
  - Building (6:48–49; 11:47–48). Greater: "who is greater" (9:46).
- **"my fruits… my barns… my grain… my goods"**: "my", four times. The own names what it holds.

### 12:19

*καὶ ἐρῶ τῇ ψυχῇ μου· Ψυχή, ἔχεις πολλὰ ἀγαθὰ κείμενα εἰς ἔτη πολλά· ἀναπαύου, φάγε, πίε, εὐφραίνου.*

- **"I will say to my soul (ψυχῇ)"**: the soul. "Whoever wills to save his soul will lose it" (9:24). He speaks to his soul as a possession: "my soul".
- **"many goods laid up (κείμενα)"**: *κεῖμαι, "lie, be laid": in 23:53 the tomb is "where no one had yet been laid (κείμενος)".* Goods laid up for many years; a body laid where none had lain.
- **"rest (ἀναπαύου), eat, drink, be merry (εὐφραίνου)"**:
  - Rest: peace "will rest" on the son of peace (10:6); the unclean spirit seeks rest and does not find it (11:24, test).
  - *Forward: the rich man "making merry (εὐφραινόμενος) daily, splendidly" (16:19).*

### 12:20–21 (12:21 grey)

*εἶπεν δὲ αὐτῷ ὁ θεός· Ἄφρων, ταύτῃ τῇ νυκτὶ τὴν ψυχήν σου ἀπαιτοῦσιν ἀπὸ σοῦ· ἃ δὲ ἡτοίμασας, τίνι ἔσται; οὕτως ὁ θησαυρίζων ἑαυτῷ καὶ μὴ εἰς θεὸν πλουτῶν.*

- **"Fool (ἄφρων)"**: "fools (ἄφρονες), did not the one who made the outside also make the inside?" (11:40).
- **"this night (νυκτί)"**: the night (11:5; 17:34).
- **"they demand back (ἀπαιτοῦσιν) your soul from you"**:
  - *ἀπαιτέω: "from the one who takes what is yours, do not demand it back (ἀπαίτει)" (6:30).*
  - The one who would demand back his goods has his soul demanded back from him. The measure (6:38).
- **"what you have prepared (ἡτοίμασας), whose will it be?"**:
  - Preparing: the messengers "to prepare for him" (9:52). *Forward:* "be ready (ἕτοιμοι)" (12:40); "did not prepare (ἑτοιμάσας)" (12:47).
  - He prepared for himself.
- **"who treasures (θησαυρίζων) for himself (ἑαυτῷ), and is not rich toward God"**:
  - Treasure: "out of the good treasure of the heart" (6:45). *Forward:* "you will have treasure in heaven" (18:22).
  - "For himself": the self (9:23–25).

### 12:22–23

*Διὰ τοῦτο λέγω ὑμῖν, μὴ μεριμνᾶτε τῇ ψυχῇ τί φάγητε, μηδὲ τῷ σώματι τί ἐνδύσησθε. ἡ γὰρ ψυχὴ πλεῖόν ἐστιν τῆς τροφῆς καὶ τὸ σῶμα τοῦ ἐνδύματος.*

- **"do not be anxious for the soul, what you will eat, nor for the body, what you will put on (ἐνδύσησθε)"**: two needs, eating and clothing. Each gets its answer in what follows.
  - *Clothing:*
    - Legion "had not put on (ἐνεδύσατο) a garment" (8:27), and then sat "clothed" (8:35).
    - *Forward:* the rich man "was clothed in purple" (16:19); Herod "putting bright clothing on him" (23:11); "until you are clothed (ἐνδύσησθε) with power from on high" (24:49, in the unprinted 24:48–53).

### 12:24

*κατανοήσατε τοὺς κόρακας ὅτι οὐ σπείρουσιν οὐδὲ θερίζουσιν, οἷς οὐκ ἔστιν ταμεῖον οὐδὲ ἀποθήκη, καὶ ὁ θεὸς τρέφει αὐτούς· πόσῳ μᾶλλον ὑμεῖς διαφέρετε τῶν πετεινῶν.*

- **"the ravens: they neither sow nor reap"**: the sower (8:5); the harvest (10:2).
- **"they have neither inner room (ταμεῖον) nor barn (ἀποθήκη)"**:
  - *The barns of 12:18.* The rich man pulls his down to build greater. The ravens have none.
  - The inner rooms of 12:3.
- **"and God feeds (τρέφει) them"**: the answer to eating. *τρέφω: Nazareth, "where he had been brought up (τεθραμμένος)" (4:16).* The own claims him as one it fed. God feeds the ravens.
- **"how much more are you worth than the birds"**.

### 12:25–26 (grey)

*τίς δὲ ἐξ ὑμῶν μεριμνῶν δύναται ἐπὶ τὴν ἡλικίαν αὐτοῦ προσθεῖναι πῆχυν; εἰ οὖν οὐδὲ ἐλάχιστον δύνασθε, τί περὶ τῶν λοιπῶν μεριμνᾶτε;*

- **"who by being anxious can add (προσθεῖναι) a cubit to his stature (ἡλικίαν)?"**:
  - *ἡλικία, stature, height: the only other place is Zacchaeus, "small in stature" (τῇ ἡλικίᾳ μικρός), who runs ahead and goes up a tree to see (19:3–4).*
  - No one adds to his own height by anxiety. The one who is small goes up to see.
  - *Adding:* "seek the kingdom, and these things will be added (προστεθήσεται) to you" (12:31). The same verb. What cannot be added by anxiety is added to the one who seeks the kingdom.
- **"if you cannot do even the least (ἐλάχιστον)"**: *forward:* "faithful in the least" (16:10; 19:17).

### 12:27

*κατανοήσατε τὰ κρίνα πῶς οὔτε νήθει οὔτε ὑφαίνει· λέγω δὲ ὑμῖν, οὐδὲ Σολομὼν ἐν πάσῃ τῇ δόξῃ αὐτοῦ περιεβάλετο ὡς ἓν τούτων.*

- **"the lilies: they neither spin nor weave"**: the adopted Marcionite (D) wording; NA27 has "how they grow: they neither toil nor spin". **Neutral.** The lilies do not make their own clothing.
- **"not even Solomon in all his glory (δόξῃ) was clothed (περιεβάλετο) like one of these"**:
  - Glory (9:26, 9:31–32).
  - Clothed: *forward: Herod, "putting (περιβαλών) bright clothing on him", sent him back (23:11).*
  - Solomon clothed himself in glory, and is outdone by what is clothed without making.

### 12:28

*πόσῳ μᾶλλον ὑμᾶς, ὀλιγόπιστοι.*

- **"how much more you (ὑμᾶς), of little faith"**:
  - Faith: "where is your faith?" (8:25); "faithless generation" (9:41).
  - "You" is accusative, the object of a verb, and no verb in the printed text governs it. 12:27's verb is middle ("Solomon clothed himself"), with Solomon as subject.

### Test: 12:28a (attested omission)

*εἰ δὲ ἐν ἀγρῷ τὸν χόρτον ὄντα σήμερον καὶ αὔριον εἰς κλίβανον βαλλόμενον ὁ θεὸς οὕτως ἀμφιέζει,* "if God so clothes the grass in the field, which is today and tomorrow is thrown into the oven…"

- **"God so clothes (ἀμφιέζει)"**: the answer to clothing, with its giver.
  - It matches the answer to eating: "God feeds (τρέφει) them" (12:24).
  - With it, the two needs of 12:22, eating and clothing, are both answered from God. Without it, eating is given by God and clothing is given by no one: the lilies are clothed, and the giver is not named.
- **"the grass (χόρτον)"**: the root of χορτάζω, "be filled": "you will be filled" (6:21); "all were filled" (9:17).
- **"today and tomorrow"**: *forward:* "today salvation has come to this house" (19:9).
- **"thrown (βαλλόμενον) into the oven"**: casting (12:5, 12:49, 12:58).

**Verdict: continues, and the printed text leans on it.**
- The reason is the mechanics of the passage, not its grammar alone. 12:22 sets two needs and 12:24 answers the first with "God feeds". This clause answers the second with "God clothes".
- The printed 12:28 keeps the conclusion of the second answer ("how much more you") without its ground. Its accusative "you" is the object of a clothing that the printed text never states.
- This is the first passage in the reading where the printed text needs words that are not printed. It is an attested omission (Epiphanius states that the text "does not have 'God clothes the grass'"), so restoring it would be against Marcion's text. *Recorded for task 14.*

### 12:29–31 (12:29 grey)

*καὶ ὑμεῖς μὴ ζητεῖτε τί φάγητε καὶ τί πίητε, καὶ μὴ μετεωρίζεσθε, ταῦτα γὰρ πάντα τὰ ἔθνη τοῦ κόσμου ἐπιζητοῦσιν, ὑμῶν δὲ ὁ πατὴρ οἶδεν ὅτι χρῄζετε τούτων. πλὴν ζητεῖτε τὴν βασιλείαν τοῦ θεοῦ, καὶ ταῦτα προστεθήσεται ὑμῖν.*

- **"do not seek (ζητεῖτε) what you will eat"**: seeking (11:9–10, 11:29).
- **"and do not be held aloft (μετεωρίζεσθε)"**:
  - μετεωρίζομαι: from μετέωρος, "raised up in the air, suspended". It is used of anxious suspense.
  - The text sets aside the height of being held up in the air, the anxious height, as it sets aside adding a cubit to one's stature (12:25).
- **"the nations (ἔθνη) of the world seek these things"**: the nations. *Forward:* "to all the nations" (24:47).
- **"your Father knows that you need (χρῄζετε) them"**: need (11:8, "as much as he needs").
- **"seek the kingdom of God, and these things will be added (προστεθήσεται) to you"**: the verb of 12:25.
  - "The kingdom of God" for "his kingdom" is the adopted Marcionite reading. **Neutral.**

### 12:32

*Μὴ φοβοῦ, τὸ μικρὸν ποίμνιον, ὅτι εὐδόκησεν ὁ πατὴρ δοῦναι ὑμῖν τὴν βασιλείαν.*

- **"do not fear, little (μικρόν) flock"**:
  - Little: "the lesser among you is great" (9:48). *Forward: Zacchaeus "small" (μικρός, 19:3).*
  - The flock: lambs (10:3). *Forward:* the lost sheep (15:4–6).
- **"it pleased (εὐδόκησεν) the Father to give you the kingdom"**:
  - The Father's good pleasure (εὐδοκία, 10:21).
  - Giving: the kingdom is given, not taken. "The Father", not "your Father", is the adopted Marcionite reading. **Neutral.**

### Test: 12:33–34 (U?)

*πωλήσατε τὰ ὑπάρχοντα ὑμῶν καὶ δότε ἐλεημοσύνην· ποιήσατε ἑαυτοῖς βαλλάντια μὴ παλαιούμενα, θησαυρὸν ἀνέκλειπτον ἐν τοῖς οὐρανοῖς, ὅπου κλέπτης οὐκ ἐγγίζει οὐδὲ σὴς διαφθείρει· ὅπου γάρ ἐστιν ὁ θησαυρὸς ὑμῶν, ἐκεῖ καὶ ἡ καρδία ὑμῶν ἔσται.*

- **"sell your possessions (ὑπάρχοντα) and give alms (ἐλεημοσύνην)"**:
  - Possessions (11:21; 12:15).
  - Alms: "give as alms the things within" (11:41).
  - *The printed 18:22 gives the same instruction: "sell all that you have and give to the poor, and you will have treasure in heaven."*
- **"purses that do not grow old (μὴ παλαιούμενα)"**:
  - "Carry no purse" (10:4).
  - *Old:* the old garment, the old wine, the old skins (5:36–39).
- **"an unfailing treasure in the heavens"**: against "treasuring for himself" (12:21). Unfailing (ἀνέκλειπτον). *Forward:* "when it fails (ἐκλίπῃ)" (16:9); "that your faith may not fail (ἐκλίπῃ)" (22:32).
- **"where no thief draws near (ἐγγίζει)"**: *the thief of 12:39.* Drawing near (10:9, 10:11).
- **"where your treasure is, there your heart will be"**: the heart (6:45; 8:15, test; 9:47).

**Verdict: continues.**
- It answers 12:16–21: treasure for oneself, then treasure in heaven.
- Its words reach 10:4, 11:41, 5:36–39, 12:39 and 18:22.
- 12:32 → 12:35 runs, so it does not close a gap.

### 12:35–36

*Ἔστωσαν ὑμῶν αἱ ὀσφύες περιεζωσμέναι καὶ οἱ λύχνοι καιόμενοι, καὶ ὑμεῖς ὅμοιοι ἀνθρώποις προσδεχομένοις τὸν κύριον ἑαυτῶν πότε ἀναλύσῃ ἐκ τῶν γάμων, ἵνα ἐλθόντος καὶ κρούσαντος εὐθέως ἀνοίξωσιν αὐτῷ.*

- **"loins girded (περιεζωσμέναι)"**: readiness. *The master will "gird himself" (περιζώσεται) in 12:37.*
- **"lamps burning (καιόμενοι)"**: the lamp (8:16; 11:33). *Forward:* "the woman lights a lamp" (15:8).
- **"men waiting for (προσδεχομένοις) their lord"**:
  - προσ-δέχομαι, the reception thread, as waiting.
  - *Forward: Joseph of Arimathea, "who was waiting for (προσεδέχετο) the kingdom of God" (23:51).*
- **"when he returns from the wedding"**.
- **"so that when he comes and knocks (κρούσαντος) they may open (ἀνοίξωσιν) to him at once"**:
  - *Knocking reversed.* In 11:9–10 we knock and it is opened to us. Here the Lord knocks and we open.
  - *Forward:* in 13:25 the master has risen and shut the door, and we knock outside.

### 12:37–38

*μακάριοι οἱ δοῦλοι ἐκεῖνοι, οὓς ἐλθὼν ὁ κύριος εὑρήσει γρηγοροῦντας· ἀμὴν λέγω ὑμῖν ὅτι περιζώσεται καὶ ἀνακλινεῖ αὐτοὺς καὶ παρελθὼν διακονήσει αὐτοῖς. κἂν ἐν τῇ ἑσπερινῇ φυλακῇ ἔλθῃ καὶ εὕρῃ οὕτως, μακάριοί εἰσιν ἐκεῖνοι.*

- **"whom the lord finds awake (γρηγοροῦντας)"**: "keeping fully awake (διαγρηγορήσαντες), they saw his glory" (9:32).
- **"he will gird himself, and make them recline (ἀνακλινεῖ), and coming by, will serve (διακονήσει) them"**:
  - *The lord serves.*
  - Service: the healed women served (8:3); Martha's service (10:40, test); the left-out 4:39.
  - Here the master takes the servant's place.
  - *The κλίνω family:* the crowd made to recline (9:14–15); "nowhere to lay his head" (9:58). *Forward: he reclines with them at Emmaus (24:30).*
- **"in the evening watch"** (ἑσπερινῇ):
  - The adopted Marcionite reading; NA27 has "in the second or the third watch".
  - *"Evening" joins the evening at Emmaus: "it is toward evening (ἑσπέραν), and the day has already declined" (24:29).* There, too, the guest is pressed to stay, and reclines, and gives.
  - **Neutral, with the thread.**

### 12:39–40

*Τοῦτο δὲ γινώσκετε ὅτι εἰ ᾔδει ὁ οἰκοδεσπότης ποίᾳ ὥρᾳ ὁ κλέπτης ἔρχεται, οὐκ ἂν ἀφῆκεν διορυχθῆναι τὸν οἶκον αὐτοῦ. καὶ ὑμεῖς γίνεσθε ἕτοιμοι, ὅτι ᾗ ὥρᾳ οὐ δοκεῖτε ὁ υἱὸς τοῦ ἀνθρώπου ἔρχεται.*

- **"the master of the house (οἰκοδεσπότης)"**: *forward:* the master who rises and shuts the door (13:25); the master who calls the poor (14:21); the master of the house with the guest room (22:11).
- **"the thief… would not have let his house be dug through (διορυχθῆναι)"**: digging. The wise builder "dug (ἔσκαψεν) and went deep" (6:48). Digging for a foundation, or digging through to rob.
- **"be ready (ἕτοιμοι)"**: preparing (9:52; 12:20). *Forward:* "Lord, I am ready (ἕτοιμος) to go with you to prison and to death" (22:33).
- **"in an hour you do not think (δοκεῖτε)"**: seeming (8:18, "what he seems to have"). *Forward:* "do you think (δοκεῖτε) I came to give peace?" (12:51).

### 12:41–44

*Εἶπεν δὲ ὁ Πέτρος· Κύριε, πρὸς ἡμᾶς τὴν παραβολὴν ταύτην λέγεις ἢ καὶ πρὸς πάντας; καὶ εἶπεν ὁ κύριος· Τίς ἄρα ἐστὶν ὁ πιστὸς οἰκονόμος, ὁ φρόνιμος, ὃν καταστήσει ὁ κύριος ἐπὶ τῆς θεραπείας αὐτοῦ τοῦ διδόναι ἐν καιρῷ τὸ σιτομέτριον; μακάριος ὁ δοῦλος ἐκεῖνος, ὃν ἐλθὼν ὁ κύριος αὐτοῦ εὑρήσει ποιοῦντα οὕτως· ἀληθῶς λέγω ὑμῖν ὅτι ἐπὶ πᾶσιν τοῖς ὑπάρχουσιν αὐτοῦ καταστήσει αὐτόν.*

- **"Peter: to us, or to all?"**: faith asks whether it is set apart.
- **"the faithful (πιστός) and prudent (φρόνιμος) steward (οἰκονόμος)"**: *forward: the steward of 16:1–8, who "acted prudently (φρονίμως)".* Faith.
- **"set over his household service (θεραπείας)"**: the word is "healing". "Those who had need of healing (θεραπείας)" (9:11).
- **"to give in season the measure of grain (σιτομέτριον)"**:
  - The measure (6:38). Grain: the rich man gathered all his grain (σῖτον) for himself (12:18).
  - *Forward:* "a hundred measures of wheat (σίτου)" (16:7); "Satan asked to sift you like wheat (σῖτον)" (22:31).
  - The steward gives the grain out, in measure.
- **"over all his possessions he will set him"**: possessions (11:21; 12:15). *Forward:* "have authority over ten cities" (19:17). What is given out in faithfulness is given back as rule over everything.

### 12:45–46

*ἐὰν δὲ εἴπῃ ὁ δοῦλος ἐκεῖνος ἐν τῇ καρδίᾳ αὐτοῦ· Χρονίζει ὁ κύριός μου ἔρχεσθαι, καὶ ἄρξηται τύπτειν τοὺς παῖδας καὶ τὰς παιδίσκας, ἐσθίειν τε καὶ πίνειν καὶ μεθύσκεσθαι, ἥξει ὁ κύριος τοῦ δούλου ἐκείνου ἐν ἡμέρᾳ ᾗ οὐ προσδοκᾷ καὶ ἐν ὥρᾳ ᾗ οὐ γινώσκει, καὶ διχοτομήσει αὐτὸν καὶ τὸ μέρος αὐτοῦ μετὰ τῶν ἀπίστων θήσει.*

- **"if that servant says in his heart, My lord delays"**:
  - When the Divine seems absent (the sleep in the boat, 8:23), the own says "he is not coming" and takes the house.
  - "In his heart": the heart (12:34, test).
- **"to beat the servant boys and girls (παῖδας), to eat and drink and get drunk"**:
  - The children (παῖς: 8:51, 8:54; 9:42; 9:47). The innocent states are beaten.
  - *Forward:* "lest your hearts be weighed down with dissipation and drunkenness and cares" (21:34).
- **"on a day he does not expect (προσδοκᾷ)"**: expecting (7:19–20; 8:40).
- **"he will cut him in two, and put his part (μέρος) with the unfaithful (ἀπίστων)"**:
  - Division (12:51–53). The part: Mary's good part (μερίς, 10:42).
  - The unfaithful: "faithless generation" (9:41).
  - The servant who divided his heart from his lord is divided.

### 12:47–48

*ἐκεῖνος δὲ ὁ δοῦλος ὁ γνοὺς τὸ θέλημα τοῦ κυρίου αὐτοῦ καὶ μὴ ἑτοιμάσας ἢ ποιήσας πρὸς τὸ θέλημα αὐτοῦ δαρήσεται πολλάς· ὁ δὲ μὴ γνοὺς ποιήσας δὲ ἄξια πληγῶν δαρήσεται ὀλίγας. παντὶ δὲ ᾧ ἐδόθη πολύ, πολὺ ζητηθήσεται παρ’ αὐτοῦ, καὶ ᾧ παρέθεντο πολύ, περισσότερον αἰτήσουσιν αὐτόν.*

- **"knowing the will (θέλημα) of his lord"**: *forward:* "not my will but yours be done" (22:42); "he handed Jesus over to their will" (23:25).
- **"not preparing"**: preparing (12:20, 12:40).
- **"worthy of blows (πληγῶν)"**: the blows laid on the man going down from Jerusalem (πληγάς, 10:30, test).
- **"to whom much was given, much will be sought (ζητηθήσεται)"**: the measure (6:38; 8:18). Seeking from: "it will be sought out (ἐκζητηθήσεται) from this generation" (11:50–51, test).
- **"to whom much was entrusted (παρέθεντο), more will be asked"**: *παρατίθημι, "set before": the bread set before the crowd (9:16); what is set before the seventy (10:8); "I have nothing to set before him" (11:6).* What was set before one is what one is asked for.

### 12:49–50 (12:50 grey)

*Πῦρ ἦλθον βαλεῖν ἐπὶ τὴν γῆν, καὶ τί θέλω εἰ ἤδη ἀνήφθη; βάπτισμα δὲ ἔχω βαπτισθῆναι, καὶ πῶς συνέχομαι ἕως ὅτου τελεσθῇ.*

- **"I came to cast fire on the earth (ἐπὶ τὴν γῆν)"**:
  - *Fire:* James and John wanted fire to come down from heaven on the village that did not receive (9:54), and were rebuked. *Forward:* fire and sulphur rained from heaven on Sodom (17:29).
  - The fire he casts is not the fire of anger. Fire is love.
  - *He casts it "on the earth": the earth again (5:24; 8:8).*
- **"how I wish it were already kindled (ἀνήφθη)"**: lighting, as the lamp is lit (8:16; 11:33; 15:8).
- **"I have a baptism to be baptized with"**:
  - The Pharisee wondered that he had not first washed (ἐβαπτίσθη, 11:38). His washing is his passage.
  - Baptism: John's (7:20; 9:19). *Forward:* "the baptism of John, was it from heaven?" (20:4).
- **"and how I am held fast (συνέχομαι) until it is accomplished"**:
  - Held fast (συνέχω): the left-out 4:38 (fever); 8:37 (fear); 8:45 (the crowds).
  - *Forward: "the men holding him fast (συνέχοντες) mocked him" (22:63).*
  - He is held fast now, inwardly, until he is held fast outwardly.

### 12:51–53 (12:52 grey)

*δοκεῖτε ὅτι εἰρήνην παρεγενόμην δοῦναι ἐν τῇ γῇ; οὐχί, λέγω ὑμῖν, ἀλλ’ ἢ διαμερισμόν. ἔσονται γὰρ ἀπὸ τοῦ νῦν πέντε ἐν ἑνὶ οἴκῳ διαμεμερισμένοι, τρεῖς ἐπὶ δυσὶν καὶ δύο ἐπὶ τρισίν, διαμερισθήσεται πατὴρ ἐπὶ υἱῷ καὶ υἱὸς ἐπὶ πατρί, μήτηρ ἐπὶ θυγατέρα καὶ θυγάτηρ ἐπὶ τὴν μητέρα, πενθερὰ ἐπὶ τὴν νύμφην αὐτῆς καὶ νύμφη ἐπὶ τὴν πενθεράν.*

- **"do you think (δοκεῖτε) I came to give peace on the earth? No, but division (διαμερισμόν)"**:
  - Peace was to be given first to every house (10:5–6). But the peace of the strong man over his possessions (11:21) is the peace he does not give.
  - Division: the kingdom divided against itself falls (11:17–18).
  - The division he brings is inside the one house.
- **"five in one house divided, three against two and two against three"**: the house is the mind (6:48–49; 11:24–26, test).
- **"father against son and son against father, mother against daughter… mother-in-law against daughter-in-law"**:
  - The relations of origin are set against each other: what one comes from, against what comes from it.
  - Father: the natural father left to the dead (9:59–60).
  - *Mother-in-law (πενθερά): the only other place is the left-out 4:38, Simon's mother-in-law, held fast by a fever, who rose and served.*
  - The singular "will be divided" is the adopted Marcionite reading. **Neutral.**

### 12:54–56 (12:54–55 grey)

*Ὅταν ἴδητε τὴν νεφέλην ἀνατέλλουσαν ἐπὶ δυσμῶν… ὑποκριταί, τὸ πρόσωπον τοῦ οὐρανοῦ καὶ τῆς γῆς οἴδατε δοκιμάζειν, τὸν καιρὸν δὲ τοῦτον πῶς οὐκ οἴδατε δοκιμάζειν;*

- **"a cloud rising (ἀνατέλλουσαν)"**: the cloud of the mountain (9:34–35), here read as weather.
- **"the face (πρόσωπον) of heaven and earth you know how to test (δοκιμάζειν)"**:
  - *The face:* his face changed and set (9:29, 9:51–53). They read the face of the sky, and not the face set toward Jerusalem.
  - *Testing (δοκιμάζω): "be rejected (ἀποδοκιμασθῆναι)" (9:22). Forward: "be rejected (ἀποδοκιμασθῆναι) by this generation" (17:25).* Those who test the weather reject him.
  - *Heaven and earth:* printed here together (the Marcionite order, heaven first, is adopted). **Neutral.**
- **"this time (καιρόν)"**: the season of 12:42. *Forward:* 21:8; 21:36.

### 12:57–59

*Τί δὲ καὶ ἀφ’ ἑαυτῶν οὐ κρίνετε τὸ δίκαιον; ὡς γὰρ ὑπάγεις μετὰ τοῦ ἀντιδίκου σου ἐπ’ ἄρχοντα, ἐν τῇ ὁδῷ δὸς ἐργασίαν ἀπηλλάχθαι ἀπ’ αὐτοῦ, μήποτε κατασύρῃ σε πρὸς τὸν κριτήν, καὶ ὁ κριτὴς παραδώσει σε τῷ πράκτορι, καὶ ὁ πράκτωρ σε βαλεῖ εἰς φυλακήν. λέγω σοι, οὐ μὴ ἐξέλθῃς ἐκεῖθεν ἕως καὶ τὸ ἔσχατον λεπτὸν ἀποδῷς.*

- **"why do you not judge for yourselves (ἀφ’ ἑαυτῶν) what is right?"**: judgement as one's own (11:19). Judging from oneself.
- **"as you go with your adversary (ἀντιδίκου) to the ruler"**:
  - *Forward:* the widow, "give me justice against my adversary (ἀντιδίκου)" (18:3).
  - The ruler (3:1; 8:41).
- **"on the way (ἐν τῇ ὁδῷ), make an effort to be released from him"**: the way. On the way, while there is still going, is when release is possible.
- **"lest he drag you down (κατασύρῃ) to the judge"**: down.
- **"and the judge hand you over (παραδώσει)"**: handing over (9:44; 10:22). The word order is the adopted Marcionite one. **Neutral.**
- **"and cast you into prison (φυλακήν)"**:
  - φυλακή, from φυλάσσω: guarding (11:21; 11:28; 12:15). The evening "watch" (12:38).
  - *Forward:* "handing you over to synagogues and prisons" (21:12); "with you I am ready to go to prison" (22:33); Barabbas, cast into prison (23:19, 23:25).
- **"you will not come out (ἐξέλθῃς) from there until you give back (ἀποδῷς) the last coin"**:
  - Coming out (4:35; 8:2). Here, the coming out is held until what is owed is given back.
  - Giving back: the boy given back (9:42). *Forward:* "half my possessions I give, and… I give back (ἀποδίδωμι) fourfold" (19:8).
  - The debt (11:4).

### What came out of the reading of chapter 10

**The law of veiling, the third time.** Nothing covered that will not be uncovered (12:2). What was whispered in the inner rooms is proclaimed from the roofs. It runs to the day the Son of Man is revealed (17:30).

**Fear and its object.** Do not fear those who kill the body. Fear the authority that reaches past it. (In the test passage 12:6–7: not one sparrow is forgotten; the hairs of the head are numbered.) Then: acknowledging or denying before God; and the holy spirit teaching in the hour.

**The own and its possessions, in one parable (12:16–21).**
- The rich man reasons within himself.
- "My fruits, my barns, my grain, my goods, my soul."
- He pulls down to build greater, lays up for many years, and tells his soul to rest, eat, drink and be merry.
- "This night they demand back your soul": the demanding back that 6:30 forbade, turned on him.
- *Two of its words come back only at the burial:* he "pulls down" (καθελῶ) his barns, and Joseph "taking him down" (καθελών) from the cross (23:53); goods "laid up" (κείμενα), and the tomb "where no one had yet been laid" (κείμενος).

**The two needs, and their giver.**
- Do not be anxious for the soul, what to eat, nor for the body, what to put on (12:22).
- God feeds the ravens, which have no barns.
- No one can add a cubit to his height by anxiety. Zacchaeus, small in height, goes up a tree (19:3–4). One is not to be held aloft (μετεωρίζεσθε).
- Seek the kingdom, and these things will be added: the verb of adding to one's height.
- The lilies are clothed better than Solomon. *But the printed text does not say who clothes them. 12:28 keeps "how much more you", with no verb for its "you".* The clause that gives the giver ("God so clothes the grass") is an attested omission, 12:28a.
- **By the passage's own mechanics, the printed text needs it.** This is the first such case in the reading, and it is recorded for task 14.

**The lord serves.**
- Loins girded, lamps burning, waiting (προσδεχόμενοι) for the lord to come and knock. The knocking is reversed from 11:9–10, and reversed again at 13:25.
- He finds them awake, girds himself, makes them recline, and serves them.
- "The evening watch" (adopted) joins the evening at Emmaus (24:29).

**The steward and the own's "my lord delays."**
- The faithful steward gives the measure of grain in season, and is set over everything. The rich man gathered his grain for himself.
- The servant who says in his heart "my lord delays" beats the children and gets drunk, and is cut in two.
- To whom much was set before, much is asked.

**Fire, baptism, division.**
- He came to cast fire on the earth: not the fire charity wanted from heaven (9:54).
- He has a baptism, his washing (11:38), and is held fast until it is done (→ 22:63).
- He does not divide the inheritance between brothers (12:14). He divides the one house: father against son, mother against daughter, and the mother-in-law of the left-out 4:38.

**On the way.** Read the face of heaven and earth, and not this time. Judge for yourselves. On the way, be released from your adversary. Otherwise you are dragged down, handed over and cast into the guarded place, and do not come out until the last coin is given back.

### Threads, carried forward and added

- treading down;
- leaven (→ 13:21; 22:1);
- hypocrisy (→ 20:20);
- covered and uncovered (→ 17:30);
- the inner room and the roof (→ 17:31);
- friends;
- the body (→ 22:19; 23–24);
- fear rightly placed;
- before men and before God;
- denial (→ 22:34);
- the holy spirit teaching;
- anxiety (→ 21:34);
- inheritance;
- division, outward refused, inward brought;
- greed and abounding;
- the rich (→ 16:19; 18:23; 19:2);
- "what shall I do?" (→ 16:3);
- gathering for oneself;
- pulling down and laying (→ 23:53);
- the soul demanded back (ἀπαιτέω, 6:30);
- making merry (→ 16:19);
- treasure (→ 18:22);
- feeding and clothing, and the giver of each (12:28a);
- stature, height (→ 19:3);
- adding;
- being held aloft;
- the nations (→ 24:47);
- the little flock;
- the Father's good pleasure;
- girding and serving: the lord serves (→ 24:30);
- waiting (προσδέχομαι, → 23:51);
- knocking and opening (→ 13:25);
- evening (→ 24:29);
- the master of the house (→ 13:25; 14:21; 22:11);
- the thief and the digging;
- ready (→ 22:33);
- the steward (→ 16:1–8);
- the measure of grain (→ 16:7; 22:31);
- "my lord delays";
- the children beaten;
- the will (→ 22:42; 23:25);
- what is set before, asked for;
- fire on the earth;
- baptism;
- held fast (→ 22:63);
- the face of heaven and earth;
- testing and rejection (→ 17:25);
- judging for oneself;
- the adversary (→ 18:3);
- the prison (→ 21:12; 22:33; 23:19);
- giving back the last coin (→ 19:8).
