## Chapter 11

*13:10–28. Grey: 13:10–13, 13:17–18, 13:23–24. Not printed, and tested at their places: 13:1–9 (A−), before 13:10; 13:22 (U?); 13:29–35 (A−) and 14:1–11 (U?), between 13:28 and 14:12. Adopted Marcionite wordings at 13:28: "all the righteous" for "Abraham and Isaac and Jacob and all the prophets", and "held" for "thrown out".*

### Test: 13:1–9 (A−)

*Παρῆσαν δέ τινες… ἀπαγγέλλοντες αὐτῷ περὶ τῶν Γαλιλαίων ὧν τὸ αἷμα Πιλᾶτος ἔμιξεν μετὰ τῶν θυσιῶν αὐτῶν… Δοκεῖτε ὅτι οἱ Γαλιλαῖοι οὗτοι ἁμαρτωλοὶ… ὅτι ταῦτα πεπόνθασιν; οὐχί, λέγω ὑμῖν, ἀλλ’ ἐὰν μὴ μετανοῆτε πάντες ὁμοίως ἀπολεῖσθε. ἢ ἐκεῖνοι οἱ δεκαοκτὼ ἐφ’ οὓς ἔπεσεν ὁ πύργος ἐν τῷ Σιλωὰμ καὶ ἀπέκτεινεν αὐτούς, δοκεῖτε ὅτι αὐτοὶ ὀφειλέται ἐγένοντο… Συκῆν εἶχέν τις πεφυτευμένην ἐν τῷ ἀμπελῶνι αὐτοῦ, καὶ ἦλθεν ζητῶν καρπὸν ἐν αὐτῇ καὶ οὐχ εὗρεν… Ἰδοὺ τρία ἔτη… ἔκκοψον αὐτήν· ἱνατί καὶ τὴν γῆν καταργεῖ; … Κύριε, ἄφες αὐτὴν καὶ τοῦτο τὸ ἔτος, ἕως ὅτου σκάψω περὶ αὐτὴν καὶ βάλω κόπρια…*

- **"the Galileans whose blood Pilate mixed with their sacrifices"**:
  - Blood (8:43–44; 11:50–51, test; 22:20).
  - Pilate, named in the opening (3:1) and at the end (23:1–24).
- **"do you think they were sinners… because they suffered (πεπόνθασιν) these things?"**:
  - Suffering is not proof of sin.
  - "The Son of Man must suffer (παθεῖν)" (9:22). *Forward:* 22:15; 24:26.
- **"unless you repent (μετανοῆτε), you will all likewise perish"**: repenting (11:32, test). *Forward:* 15:7, 15:10; 17:3–4; 24:47. Perishing (9:24; 8:24).
- **"those eighteen (δεκαοκτώ) on whom the tower fell (ἔπεσεν)"**:
  - *Eighteen: the bent woman of the printed 13:11, 13:16 has been bound "eighteen years". The only other places the number occurs.*
  - The tower fell and killed eighteen. She, bound eighteen years, is straightened up.
  - Falling: the house that fell (6:49); house on house (11:17).
- **"debtors (ὀφειλέται)"**: owing (7:41; 11:4).
- **"a fig tree planted in his vineyard; he came seeking fruit and found none"**:
  - Fruit (6:43–44; 8:8; 12:17).
  - *Forward:* "look at the fig tree and all the trees" (21:29); Zacchaeus' sycamore-fig (συκομορέα, 19:4).
- **"three years"**: three, the full period (9:22).
- **"cut it down; why should it use up the earth?"**: the earth (8:8).
- **"Lord, let it be (ἄφες) this year too, until I dig (σκάψω) around it and put on manure (κόπρια)"**:
  - ἀφίημι: letting go, here letting be.
  - Digging: the builder "dug (ἔσκαψεν) and went deep" (6:48).
  - Manure: *the salt fit "neither for the earth nor for the dunghill (κοπρίαν)" (14:35, test).*

**Verdict: continues.**
- Every word is in a thread.
- Its "eighteen" and "fell" lie directly against the printed 13:11–16: eighteen killed by a fall, and a woman bound eighteen years, made straight.
- 12:59 → 13:10 runs (a new scene, in a synagogue), so it does not close a gap.

### 13:10–11 (grey)

*Ἦν δὲ διδάσκων ἐν μιᾷ τῶν συναγωγῶν ἐν τοῖς σάββασιν. καὶ ἰδοὺ γυνὴ πνεῦμα ἔχουσα ἀσθενείας ἔτη δεκαοκτώ, καὶ ἦν συγκύπτουσα καὶ μὴ δυναμένη ἀνακύψαι εἰς τὸ παντελές.*

- **"teaching in one of the synagogues on the sabbaths"**: the assembly (4:33; 6:6).
- **"a woman having a spirit of weakness eighteen years"**: having (8:18; 8:27).
- **"bent together (συγκύπτουσα), and unable to straighten up (ἀνακύψαι) at all"**:
  - *ἀνακύπτω, "bend up, straighten up": the only other place is 21:28: "straighten up (ἀνακύψατε) and lift up your heads, because your redemption draws near".*
  - The woman who cannot straighten up, and the command at the end to straighten up.
  - Bent toward the earth: the natural turned in on itself, unable to look up (?).

### 13:12–13 (grey)

*ἰδὼν δὲ αὐτὴν ὁ Ἰησοῦς προσεφώνησεν καὶ εἶπεν αὐτῇ· Γύναι, ἀπολέλυσαι τῆς ἀσθενείας σου, καὶ ἐπέθηκεν αὐτῇ τὰς χεῖρας· καὶ παραχρῆμα ἀνωρθώθη, καὶ ἐδόξαζεν τὸν θεόν.*

- **"he called to her"**: calling (6:13; 8:54).
- **"Woman, you are loosed (ἀπολέλυσαι) from your weakness"**:
  - *The λύω family, loosing:*
    - "release (ἀπολύετε), and you will be released" (6:37);
    - the ox loosed (λύει, 13:15); "must she not be loosed (λυθῆναι) from this bond" (13:16).
  - *Forward:* "your redemption (ἀπολύτρωσις) draws near" (21:28); Pilate wills to release (ἀπολῦσαι) him (23:16–22); "we hoped he was the one to redeem (λυτροῦσθαι) Israel" (24:21).
  - The bent woman is the first one "loosed" in this sense. The word runs to the redemption that "draws near" at the end.
- **"he laid his hands on her"**: the hand (5:13; 8:54).
- **"and at once she was made straight (ἀνωρθώθη)"**:
  - ἀν-ορθόω, "set upright again": *"you answered rightly (ὀρθῶς); do this and you will live" (10:28).*
  - Straightness given back. She stands up straight, not by her own power ("unable").
- **"and she glorified God"**: glory (5:25–26; 7:16). *Forward:* the Samaritan who returns "glorifying God" (17:15); the blind man (18:43).

### 13:14–16

*ἀποκριθεὶς δὲ ὁ ἀρχισυνάγωγος, ἀγανακτῶν ὅτι τῷ σαββάτῳ ἐθεράπευσεν ὁ Ἰησοῦς, ἔλεγεν τῷ ὄχλῳ ὅτι Ἓξ ἡμέραι εἰσὶν ἐν αἷς δεῖ ἐργάζεσθαι· ἐν αὐταῖς οὖν ἐρχόμενοι θεραπεύεσθε καὶ μὴ τῇ ἡμέρᾳ τοῦ σαββάτου. ἀπεκρίθη δὲ αὐτῷ ὁ κύριος καὶ εἶπεν· Ὑποκριταί, ἕκαστος ὑμῶν τῷ σαββάτῳ οὐ λύει τὸν βοῦν αὐτοῦ ἢ τὸν ὄνον ἀπὸ τῆς φάτνης καὶ ἀπαγαγὼν ποτίζει; ταύτην δὲ θυγατέρα Ἀβραὰμ οὖσαν, ἣν ἔδησεν ὁ Σατανᾶς ἰδοὺ δέκα καὶ ὀκτὼ ἔτη, οὐκ ἔδει λυθῆναι ἀπὸ τοῦ δεσμοῦ τούτου τῇ ἡμέρᾳ τοῦ σαββάτου;*

- **"the ruler of the synagogue"**: the rulers (3:1). Jairus, a ruler of a synagogue, fell at his feet (8:41). This one is indignant.
- **"six days in which one must (δεῖ) work"**:
  - The ruler's "must", against his "must" (9:22) and against the "must" (ἔδει) of 13:16.
  - The six days are the days of labour; the sabbath is rest (?).
- **"Hypocrites"**: 12:1, 12:56.
- **"does not each loose (λύει) his ox or donkey from the manger and lead it away to water it?"**: loosing, and watering. The animal is led from the manger to water.
- **"this daughter of Abraham"**:
  - Daughter: "Daughter, your faith has saved you" (8:48); Jairus' daughter (8:42).
  - Abraham. *Forward:* "son of Abraham" (19:9); Abraham's bosom (16:22–30).
- **"whom Satan bound (ἔδησεν)… must (ἔδει) she not be loosed (λυθῆναι) from this bond (δεσμοῦ)?"**:
  - *The bond: Legion "was bound with chains… and bursting the bonds (δεσμά)" (8:29). The only other place δεσμός occurs.*
  - Legion burst outward bonds by the daimon's force. She is loosed from Satan's bond by the word.
  - "Must" (ἔδει): *the necessity of the passion (9:22; 24:7, 24:26) is here a necessity of loosing.*

### 13:17–19 (13:17–18 grey)

*καὶ ταῦτα λέγοντος αὐτοῦ κατῃσχύνοντο πάντες οἱ ἀντικείμενοι αὐτῷ, καὶ πᾶς ὁ ὄχλος ἔχαιρεν ἐπὶ πᾶσιν τοῖς ἐνδόξοις τοῖς γινομένοις ὑπ’ αὐτοῦ. Ἔλεγεν οὖν· Τίνι ὁμοία ἐστὶν ἡ βασιλεία τοῦ θεοῦ, καὶ τίνι ὁμοιώσω αὐτήν; ὁμοία ἐστὶν κόκκῳ σινάπεως, ὃν λαβὼν ἄνθρωπος ἔβαλεν εἰς κῆπον ἑαυτοῦ, καὶ ηὔξησεν καὶ ἐγένετο εἰς δένδρον, καὶ τὰ πετεινὰ τοῦ οὐρανοῦ κατεσκήνωσεν ἐν τοῖς κλάδοις αὐτοῦ.*

- **"all who opposed him were put to shame"**: shame (9:26). *Forward:* "with shame (αἰσχύνης) to take the last place" (14:9, test).
- **"the crowd rejoiced at the glorious things"**: joy (10:17, 10:21).
- **"a grain of mustard seed, which a man took and cast (ἔβαλεν) into his own garden"**:
  - The seed (8:5–8). Casting: "I came to cast (βαλεῖν) fire on the earth" (12:49).
  - The garden: a place of growth inside one's own (ἑαυτοῦ) ground.
- **"and it grew and became a tree"**:
  - *Growth upward, from the smallest thing.* The seed in the good earth grew (φυέν) and bore fruit (8:8).
  - The tree: known by its fruit (6:43–44). *Forward:* "the fig tree and all the trees" (21:29).
- **"and the birds of heaven dwelt (κατεσκήνωσεν) in its branches"**:
  - *The birds of heaven ate the seed by the way (8:5).* "The birds of heaven have dwellings (κατασκηνώσεις), but the Son of Man has nowhere to lay his head" (9:58). Peter wanted to make dwellings (σκηνάς) on the mountain (9:33).
  - The birds that took the seed away now dwell in what the seed has become. The thoughts that carried off the word are housed in the tree grown from it.

### 13:20–21

*Τίνι ὁμοιώσω τὴν βασιλείαν τοῦ θεοῦ; ὁμοία ἐστὶν ζύμῃ, ἣν λαβοῦσα γυνὴ ἐνέκρυψεν εἰς ἀλεύρου σάτα τρία ἕως οὗ ἐζυμώθη ὅλον.*

- **"leaven, which a woman took and hid (ἐνέκρυψεν)"**:
  - The leaven of the Pharisees (12:1); here, the kingdom as leaven.
  - Hidden: the law of veiling (8:17; 10:21; 12:2). The kingdom is hidden, and works until the whole is changed.
- **"in three measures of flour, until the whole was leavened"**: three (11:5, three loaves); the measure (6:38). The whole (ὅλον: 10:27 "with the whole heart").

### Test: 13:22 (U?)

*Καὶ διεπορεύετο κατὰ πόλεις καὶ κώμας διδάσκων καὶ πορείαν ποιούμενος εἰς Ἱεροσόλυμα.*

- **"he went through (διεπορεύετο) cities and villages"**: through-going (8:1; 9:6).
- **"teaching, and making his journey (πορείαν) to Jerusalem"**: the road and its goal, restated (9:51).

**Verdict: continues.** It marks the road at its middle. 13:21 → 13:23 runs, so it does not close a gap.

*An observation to carry forward. In the printed text, after 9:51–53, the road is named with Jerusalem as its goal only at 17:11 ("as he was going to Jerusalem") and 19:11 ("because he was near Jerusalem"). The other places where NA27 names Jerusalem on the road are not printed: 13:22, 13:33–34, 18:31, 19:28, and 19:41 (in 19:29–46).*

### 13:23–24 (grey)

*Κύριε, εἰ ὀλίγοι οἱ σῳζόμενοι; … Ἀγωνίζεσθε εἰσελθεῖν διὰ τῆς στενῆς θύρας, ὅτι πολλοί, λέγω ὑμῖν, ζητήσουσιν εἰσελθεῖν καὶ οὐκ ἰσχύσουσιν,*

- **"are those being saved few?"**: saving (6:9; 7:50; 8:48, 8:50; 9:24).
- **"strive (ἀγωνίζεσθε) to enter (εἰσελθεῖν) through the narrow door (θύρας)"**:
  - Entering: "you did not enter, and you hindered those entering" (11:52); "the light for those coming in" (11:33).
  - The door: 11:7 (shut, with the one inside); 12:36 (opened to the lord who knocks).
  - Narrow: what the own cannot pass through with its possessions (?).
- **"many will seek to enter and will not have the strength (ἰσχύσουσιν)"**:
  - Seeking (11:9: "seek, and you will find").
  - *Strength:* the strong man (11:21–22); "love with your whole strength" (10:27); the woman who "had no strength" to be healed (8:43).
  - Many seek with their own strength, and it is not enough.

### 13:25

*ἀφ’ οὗ ἂν ἐγερθῇ ὁ οἰκοδεσπότης καὶ ἀποκλείσῃ τὴν θύραν, καὶ ἄρξησθε ἔξω ἑστάναι καὶ κρούειν τὴν θύραν λέγοντες· Κύριε, ἄνοιξον ἡμῖν· καὶ ἀποκριθεὶς ἐρεῖ ὑμῖν· Οὐκ οἶδα ὑμᾶς πόθεν ἐστέ.*

- **"once the master of the house has risen (ἐγερθῇ) and shut the door"**:
  - *The parable of 11:5–8, reversed.* There, the one inside, behind a shut door, was raised (ἐγερθείς) to give. Here the master rises to shut.
  - The master of the house (12:39).
- **"you begin to stand outside (ἔξω ἑστάναι)"**:
  - The mother and brothers "stand outside" (8:20). Nazareth cast him "outside" (4:29).
  - Now those who claimed him stand outside.
- **"and to knock at the door, saying, Lord, open to us"**:
  - "Knock, and it will be opened" (11:9–10). The lord knocks, and the watchful open (12:36).
  - Here the knocking comes after the door is shut.
- **"I do not know you, where you are from (πόθεν)"**:
  - *Origin.* Nazareth claimed him by origin, "where he had been brought up" (4:16), "your fatherland" (4:23–24).
  - Here origin is not recognized. *Forward:* the chief priests answer that they do not know "where from" (πόθεν) John's baptism was (20:7).

### 13:26–27

*τότε ἄρξεσθε λέγειν· Ἐφάγομεν ἐνώπιόν σου καὶ ἐπίομεν, καὶ ἐν ταῖς πλατείαις ἡμῶν ἐδίδαξας· καὶ ἐρεῖ λέγων ὑμῖν· Οὐκ οἶδα ὑμᾶς πόθεν ἐστέ· ἀπόστητε ἀπ’ ἐμοῦ, πάντες ἐργάται ἀδικίας.*

- **"we ate and drank before you, and you taught in our streets"**:
  - They claim him by nearness: eating with him (5:30), his teaching in "our" streets. "Our" is the own's claim.
  - The streets: the seventy go out into the streets of the city that does not receive (10:10). *Forward: the poor are brought in from the streets (14:21).*
- **"depart (ἀπόστητε) from me"**:
  - ἀφίστημι: those on the rock "stand away (ἀφίστανται) in time of temptation" (8:13, test).
  - Simon said "go out from me" (5:8). Here he says "depart from me".
- **"all workers (ἐργάται) of unrighteousness"**: the workers of the harvest (10:2, 10:7). *Forward:* "the steward of unrighteousness" (16:8); "the mammon of unrighteousness" (16:9).

### 13:28

*ἐκεῖ ἔσται ὁ κλαυθμὸς καὶ ὁ βρυγμὸς τῶν ὀδόντων, ὅταν ὄψησθε πάντας τοὺς δικαίους ἐν τῇ βασιλείᾳ τοῦ θεοῦ, ὑμᾶς δὲ κρατουμένους ἔξω.*

- **"weeping (κλαυθμός)"**: "blessed who weep now" (6:21); "do not weep" (7:13; 8:52). The weeping that comes too late.
- **"when you see all the righteous in the kingdom"**:
  - The adopted Marcionite reading. "All the righteous" joins the printed 14:14, "the resurrection of the righteous".
  - NA27's "Abraham and Isaac and Jacob and all the prophets" would join 16:23, the rich man who "sees Abraham from afar".
  - **Neutral.**
- **"and yourselves held (κρατουμένους) outside"**:
  - The adopted Marcionite reading, for NA27's "thrown out".
  - κρατέω occurs in the printed text only at 8:54 ("taking hold of her hand"), here, and 24:16 ("their eyes were held so that they did not recognize him").
  - "Held outside" joins the held eyes of 24:16. NA27's "thrown out" joins the casting out of 4:29.
  - **Neutral.**

### Test: 13:29–35 (A−)

*καὶ ἥξουσιν ἀπὸ ἀνατολῶν καὶ δυσμῶν καὶ ἀπὸ βορρᾶ καὶ νότου καὶ ἀνακλιθήσονται ἐν τῇ βασιλείᾳ τοῦ θεοῦ. καὶ ἰδοὺ εἰσὶν ἔσχατοι οἳ ἔσονται πρῶτοι, καὶ εἰσὶν πρῶτοι οἳ ἔσονται ἔσχατοι. Ἐν αὐτῇ τῇ ὥρᾳ προσῆλθάν τινες Φαρισαῖοι λέγοντες αὐτῷ· Ἔξελθε καὶ πορεύου ἐντεῦθεν, ὅτι Ἡρῴδης θέλει σε ἀποκτεῖναι. καὶ εἶπεν αὐτοῖς· Πορευθέντες εἴπατε τῇ ἀλώπεκι ταύτῃ· Ἰδοὺ ἐκβάλλω δαιμόνια καὶ ἰάσεις ἀποτελῶ σήμερον καὶ αὔριον, καὶ τῇ τρίτῃ τελειοῦμαι. πλὴν δεῖ με σήμερον καὶ αὔριον καὶ τῇ ἐχομένῃ πορεύεσθαι, ὅτι οὐκ ἐνδέχεται προφήτην ἀπολέσθαι ἔξω Ἰερουσαλήμ. Ἰερουσαλὴμ Ἰερουσαλήμ, ἡ ἀποκτείνουσα τοὺς προφήτας καὶ λιθοβολοῦσα τοὺς ἀπεσταλμένους πρὸς αὐτήν— ποσάκις ἠθέλησα ἐπισυνάξαι τὰ τέκνα σου ὃν τρόπον ὄρνις τὴν ἑαυτῆς νοσσιὰν ὑπὸ τὰς πτέρυγας, καὶ οὐκ ἠθελήσατε. ἰδοὺ ἀφίεται ὑμῖν ὁ οἶκος ὑμῶν. λέγω δὲ ὑμῖν, οὐ μὴ ἴδητέ με ἕως ἥξει ὅτε εἴπητε· Εὐλογημένος ὁ ἐρχόμενος ἐν ὀνόματι κυρίου.*

- **"they will come from east and west… and recline (ἀνακλιθήσονται) in the kingdom"**:
  - *The κλίνω family:* the lord "will make them recline (ἀνακλινεῖ)" (12:37); 9:14–15; 24:30.
  - Against "held outside" (13:28): others come in and recline.
- **"the last (ἔσχατοι) will be first (πρῶτοι)"**:
  - "First": the own's first (9:59–61), the first seat (11:43).
  - *Forward:* "the last place (ἔσχατον τόπον)" (14:9–10, test).
- **"Herod wants to kill you… tell that fox (ἀλώπεκι)"**:
  - Herod (3:1; 9:7–9). *Forward:* 23:6–12.
  - Foxes "have holes" (9:58).
- **"I cast out daimons and perform cures today and tomorrow (σήμερον καὶ αὔριον)"**: today and tomorrow (12:28a, test). *Forward:* "today salvation" (19:9).
- **"and on the third I am perfected (τελειοῦμαι)"**:
  - The third: "after three days" (9:22). *Forward:* "the third day" (24:7, 24:21).
  - Perfected: "until it is accomplished (τελεσθῇ)" (12:50).
  - *The end of the road named as being perfected, made complete.*
- **"I must (δεῖ) go (πορεύεσθαι) today and tomorrow and the next day"**:
  - *The road's own infinitive:* the crowds held him back "from going" (4:42); he set his face "to go" to Jerusalem (9:51).
  - The "must" of 9:22.
- **"for it cannot be that a prophet perishes outside (ἔξω) Jerusalem"**:
  - *Why the road goes to Jerusalem:* it is the place where the prophet perishes.
  - "No prophet is accepted in his fatherland" (4:24); the fathers killed the prophets (11:47–48).
- **"Jerusalem, Jerusalem, killing the prophets and stoning those sent (ἀπεσταλμένους)"**: the sent (9:2; 10:1–3; 11:49, test).
- **"how often I wanted (ἠθέλησα) to gather (ἐπισυνάξαι) your children… and you did not want"**:
  - Gathering: "whoever does not gather with me scatters" (11:23); the rich man gathering for himself (12:17–18).
  - His will against theirs: "the will of his lord" (12:47).
- **"your house is left (ἀφίεται) to you"**: ἀφίημι, letting go. The house left, as the dead are left to the dead (9:60).
- **"you will not see me until you say, Blessed is the one who comes in the name of the Lord"**:
  - "The coming one" (ὁ ἐρχόμενος): "are you the coming one?" (7:19–20).
  - *Forward: in the unprinted 19:29–46, the crowd says "Blessed is the coming king in the name of the Lord" (19:38).*

**Verdict: continues.**
- Its words are in the threads throughout, and it reaches 9:22, 12:50 and the third day of 24:7, 24:21.
- *It is the passage that says why the road goes to Jerusalem: a prophet perishes there, and on the third day he is perfected.* The printed text has the necessity (9:22; 24:7, 24:26), but not the reason the place is Jerusalem.
- It does not close a gap. With 14:1–11 restored (below), 13:28 → 14:1 runs. Its first two verses would lead into 14:7–11: those who come in and recline, the last who will be first.

### Test: 14:1–11 (U?)

*Καὶ ἐγένετο ἐν τῷ ἐλθεῖν αὐτὸν εἰς οἶκόν τινος τῶν ἀρχόντων τῶν Φαρισαίων σαββάτῳ φαγεῖν ἄρτον καὶ αὐτοὶ ἦσαν παρατηρούμενοι αὐτόν. καὶ ἰδοὺ ἄνθρωπός τις ἦν ὑδρωπικὸς ἔμπροσθεν αὐτοῦ… Ἔξεστιν τῷ σαββάτῳ θεραπεῦσαι ἢ οὔ; οἱ δὲ ἡσύχασαν. καὶ ἐπιλαβόμενος ἰάσατο αὐτὸν καὶ ἀπέλυσεν. καὶ πρὸς αὐτοὺς εἶπεν· Τίνος ὑμῶν υἱὸς ἢ βοῦς εἰς φρέαρ πεσεῖται, καὶ οὐκ εὐθέως ἀνασπάσει αὐτὸν ἐν ἡμέρᾳ τοῦ σαββάτου; … Ἔλεγεν δὲ πρὸς τοὺς κεκλημένους παραβολήν, ἐπέχων πῶς τὰς πρωτοκλισίας ἐξελέγοντο… Ὅταν κληθῇς ὑπό τινος εἰς γάμους, μὴ κατακλιθῇς εἰς τὴν πρωτοκλισίαν… καὶ τότε ἄρξῃ μετὰ αἰσχύνης τὸν ἔσχατον τόπον κατέχειν. ἀλλ’ ὅταν κληθῇς πορευθεὶς ἀνάπεσε εἰς τὸν ἔσχατον τόπον, ἵνα ὅταν ἔλθῃ ὁ κεκληκώς σε ἐρεῖ σοι· Φίλε, προσανάβηθι ἀνώτερον· τότε ἔσται σοι δόξα ἐνώπιον πάντων τῶν συνανακειμένων σοι. ὅτι πᾶς ὁ ὑψῶν ἑαυτὸν ταπεινωθήσεται καὶ ὁ ταπεινῶν ἑαυτὸν ὑψωθήσεται.*

- **"into the house of a ruler of the Pharisees, on a sabbath, to eat bread"**: the ruler (13:14); the meal (7:36; 11:37).
- **"they were watching (παρατηρούμενοι) him"**: *forward: "watching (παρατηρήσαντες), they sent spies" (20:20).*
- **"a man with dropsy"**: swollen with water that does not satisfy (?). *The ox is led to water (13:15); the son or ox fallen into a well is drawn up (14:5).*
- **"they were silent (ἡσύχασαν)"**: *forward: "on the sabbath they rested (ἡσύχασαν)" (23:56).*
- **"taking hold (ἐπιλαβόμενος) of him, he healed him and released (ἀπέλυσεν) him"**: taking hold of the child (9:47); loosing (13:12).
- **"a son or an ox fallen (πεσεῖται) into a well, will he not at once draw him up (ἀνασπάσει)?"**:
  - Falling (6:39: the blind "fall into a pit"). Drawing up: ἀνα-σπάω, up.
  - *13:15 (printed) leads the ox to water; 14:5 draws the son up out of the well.*
- **"the invited (κεκλημένους)… choosing (ἐξελέγοντο) the first places (πρωτοκλισίας)"**:
  - Calling (5:32). *The printed great supper uses the same participle: "to say to the invited (κεκλημένοις)" (14:17, 14:24).*
  - Choosing: Mary "chose the good part" (10:42, test). The first places: the first seat (11:43). πρωτο-κλισία is of the κλίνω family: the first reclining place.
- **"do not recline (κατακλιθῇς) in the first place… then with shame you begin to hold (κατέχειν) the last place (ἔσχατον τόπον)"**:
  - Shame (13:17).
  - Holding (κατέχω): the crowds held him back (4:42); the good heart holds the word fast (8:15, test). Here one is reduced to holding the last place.
- **"but when you are invited, go and recline (ἀνάπεσε) in the last place"**: *the posture of 11:37 and 22:14 (ἀνέπεσεν).*
- **"so that when the one who invited you (ὁ κεκληκώς σε) comes, he will say to you, Friend, go up higher (προσανάβηθι ἀνώτερον)"**:
  - *Here the text says how one goes up.* One goes up by being called up by the host, from the last place. It is not taken by oneself.
  - Friend (11:5–8; 12:4).
  - ἀναβαίνω: he "went up" to the mountain to pray (9:28).
- **"then you will have glory (δόξα) before all who recline with you (συνανακειμένων)"**: glory (9:26, 9:31–32). *Forward:* "to enter into his glory" (24:26).
- **"everyone who lifts himself up (ὑψῶν) will be brought low, and whoever brings himself low will be lifted up (ὑψωθήσεται)"**:
  - *The same words in the printed 18:14.*
  - Capernaum, lifted to heaven and going down (10:15, test).

**Verdict: continues, and the printed text needs it.**
- *By its words.* The printed 14:12 begins "he said also to the one who had invited him (τῷ κεκληκότι αὐτόν)". That is the participle of 14:10 ("the one who invited you", ὁ κεκληκώς σε). The printed text has no host and no meal since 11:37.
  - The printed 14:15, "one of those reclining with him (συνανακειμένων)", takes up 14:10's word.
  - Its "eat bread in the kingdom" (φάγεται ἄρτον) takes up 14:1's "to eat bread" (φαγεῖν ἄρτον).
  - Three of the printed words point back to this passage.
- *By its mechanics.* 14:7–14 is one teaching about the meal, in two halves:
  - the guest's half (14:7–11): take the last place, and be called up higher, to glory;
  - the host's half (14:12–14): call those who cannot repay, and be repaid "at the resurrection of the righteous".
  - The printed text has the host's half without the guest's. That is: it has the giving, but not the way one is raised.
- *It is the one place where the text says "go up" of a person, as an invitation from above to the lowest place.* For the question of what ascends and how, this is the text's own answer in one sentence.
- U?, not A−: restoring it is not against a witness of absence. *Recorded for task 14.*

### What came out of the reading of chapter 11

**Straightened up, and loosed.**
- A woman bound eighteen years, unable to straighten up (ἀνακύψαι), is loosed (ἀπολέλυσαι) and made straight (ἀνωρθώθη). Then she glorifies God.
- *ἀνακύπτω returns only at 21:28: "straighten up and lift up your heads, because your redemption (ἀπολύτρωσις) draws near".* The loosing (λύω) runs to redemption (21:28; 24:21) and to Pilate's will to release (23:16–22).
- She is bound by Satan with a bond (δεσμός). The only other bonds are Legion's (8:29), which he burst by force.
- (In the test passage 13:1–9, eighteen are killed by a falling tower.)

**The kingdom grows up and is hidden.**
- A seed cast into one's own garden grows into a tree. The birds of heaven, which took the seed (8:5) and have dwellings (9:58), dwell in its branches.
- Leaven hidden in three measures works until all is leavened.

**The door shut.**
- The master of the house rises (ἐγερθῇ) to shut, the reverse of 11:8.
- Those outside knock, and are not known "where they are from".
- They claim him by nearness: "we ate before you, you taught in our streets".
- They are held outside (κρατουμένους). That is the word of the eyes "held" at 24:16, so that they did not recognize him.

**The road is named little in the printed text.** After 9:51–53, the printed text names Jerusalem on the road only at 17:11 and 19:11. The unprinted 13:22, 13:33–34, 18:31, 19:28 and 19:41 carry the rest. The one that gives the road its reason — "I must go… a prophet cannot perish outside Jerusalem… on the third I am perfected" — is the unprinted 13:31–35.

**The second passage the printed text needs: 14:1–11.**
- Its words are pointed to by the printed 14:12 ("the one who had invited him") and 14:15 ("those reclining with him"; "eat bread").
- Its mechanics are the guest's half of the teaching whose host's half is printed.
- It is the text's own sentence on ascent: "Friend, go up higher", said by the host to the one who took the last place. Then glory. Then the law: whoever brings himself low will be lifted up (= the printed 18:14).

### Threads, carried forward and added

- straightening up (ἀνακύπτω, → 21:28);
- loosing and redemption (λύω, → 21:28; 23:16–25; 24:21);
- bonds (8:29 / 13:16);
- eighteen (13:4 / 13:11, 13:16);
- the ox, and water, and the well;
- the sabbath and its rest (→ 23:56);
- daughter of Abraham (→ 16:22–30; 19:9);
- the mustard seed and the tree;
- the birds that dwell;
- leaven hidden;
- the road to Jerusalem: printed at 9:51–53, 17:11, 19:11; the rest unprinted;
- the narrow door, entering, strength;
- the master who rises to shut;
- knocking after;
- "where from" (πόθεν, → 20:7);
- "depart from me";
- workers of unrighteousness (→ 16:8–9);
- held outside (κρατέω, → 24:16);
- first and last, and the last place;
- "go up higher";
- glory (→ 24:26);
- lifting up and bringing low (→ 18:14);
- "perfected on the third day";
- "a prophet cannot perish outside Jerusalem";
- the house left;
- "Blessed is the one who comes" (→ 19:38, unprinted).

## Chapter 12

*14:12–24. Grey: 14:13, 14:15. Not printed: 14:25–35 (U?), after 14:24. The text runs on from 14:1–11 (tested at the end of chapter 11).*

### 14:12

*Ἔλεγεν δὲ καὶ τῷ κεκληκότι αὐτόν· Ὅταν ποιῇς ἄριστον ἢ δεῖπνον, μὴ φώνει τοὺς φίλους σου μηδὲ τοὺς ἀδελφούς σου μηδὲ τοὺς συγγενεῖς σου μηδὲ γείτονας πλουσίους, μήποτε καὶ αὐτοὶ ἀντικαλέσωσίν σε καὶ γένηται ἀνταπόδομά σοι.*

- **"he said also to the one who had invited him"**: "also" (καί) and "the one who had invited" (τῷ κεκληκότι). Both look back to a guest's teaching and a host (14:7–10, test).
- **"a lunch or a dinner (δεῖπνον)"**: *the dinner. Forward: "the cup, after the dinner (δειπνῆσαι)" (22:20).*
- **"do not call your friends, your brothers, your relatives, or rich neighbours"**:
  - The natural ties: "my mother and brothers are those who hear" (8:21).
  - The rich (6:24; 12:16).
- **"lest they invite you back and a repayment (ἀνταπόδομα) be made to you"**:
  - The exchange of the own: "if you lend to those from whom you hope to receive, what grace is that to you?" (6:34).
  - Giving where it comes back is not giving.

### 14:13–14 (14:13 grey)

*ἀλλ’ ὅταν δοχὴν ποιῇς, κάλει πτωχούς, ἀναπείρους, χωλούς, τυφλούς· καὶ μακάριος ἔσῃ, ὅτι οὐκ ἔχουσιν ἀνταποδοῦναί σοι, ἀνταποδοθήσεται γάρ σοι ἐν τῇ ἀναστάσει τῶν δικαίων.*

- **"when you make a reception (δοχήν)"**: *δοχή: the only other place is Levi's "great reception" (5:29), where he ate with tax collectors and sinners.* The reception thread (δέχομαι).
- **"call the poor, the crippled, the lame, the blind"**:
  - The poor: "blessed are the poor" (6:20); "the poor have good news" (7:22).
  - The lame walk, the blind look up (7:21–22).
  - Those whom he healed are those to be called.
- **"blessed will you be, because they have nothing to repay you"**: "lend, expecting nothing back" (6:35).
- **"for you will be repaid at the resurrection (ἀναστάσει) of the righteous"**:
  - *ἀνάστασις, the noun of rising, for the first time in the printed text.* *Forward:* "the resurrection from the dead… sons of the resurrection" (20:27–36).
  - The repayment for giving without return is rising.
  - "The righteous": "all the righteous in the kingdom" (13:28, adopted).

### 14:15 (grey)

*Ἀκούσας δέ τις τῶν συνανακειμένων ταῦτα εἶπεν αὐτῷ· Μακάριος ὅστις φάγεται ἄρτον ἐν τῇ βασιλείᾳ τοῦ θεοῦ.*

- **"one of those reclining with him"** (συνανακειμένων): the fellow guests of 14:10 (test).
- **"blessed is whoever eats bread in the kingdom of God"**:
  - Eating bread (14:1, test; 9:13–17). The bread asked for daily (11:3).
  - *Forward:* "I will not drink… until the kingdom of God comes" (22:18).

### 14:16–17

*Ἄνθρωπός τις ἐποίει δεῖπνον μέγα, καὶ ἐκάλεσεν πολλούς, καὶ ἀπέστειλεν τὸν δοῦλον αὐτοῦ τῇ ὥρᾳ τοῦ δείπνου εἰπεῖν τοῖς κεκλημένοις· Ἔρχεσθε, ὅτι ἤδη ἕτοιμά ἐστιν.*

- **"a great dinner; he called (ἐκάλεσεν) many"**:
  - Calling: "I came to call (καλέσαι) sinners" (5:32).
  - "The calling (κλῆσιν) and the love of God" (11:42, adopted).
- **"he sent his servant at the hour of the dinner"**: sending (9:2; 10:1). The hour. *Forward:* "when the hour came, he reclined" (22:14).
- **"Come, for it is already ready (ἕτοιμα)"**:
  - Preparing: the messengers sent "to prepare for him" (9:52); "what you have prepared, whose will it be?" (12:20); "be ready" (12:40). *Forward:* "prepare the Passover" (22:8–12).
  - Here everything is ready from the host's side. Only coming is asked.

### 14:18–20

*καὶ ἤρξαντο ἀπὸ μιᾶς πάντες παραιτεῖσθαι. ὁ πρῶτος εἶπεν αὐτῷ· Ἀγρὸν ἠγόρασα καὶ ἔχω ἀνάγκην ἐξελθὼν ἰδεῖν αὐτόν· ἐρωτῶ σε, ἔχε με παρῃτημένον. καὶ ἕτερος εἶπεν· Ζεύγη βοῶν ἠγόρασα πέντε καὶ πορεύομαι δοκιμάσαι αὐτά· ἐρωτῶ σε, ἔχε με παρῃτημένον. καὶ ἕτερος εἶπεν· Γυναῖκα ἔγημα καὶ διὰ τοῦτο οὐ δύναμαι ἐλθεῖν.*

- **"all as one began to excuse themselves (παραιτεῖσθαι)"**: αἰτέω, asking: "ask, and it will be given" (11:9); "they demand back (ἀπαιτοῦσιν) your soul" (12:20). Here they ask to be let off.
- **"the first"**: the own's first (9:59, 9:61).
- **"I bought (ἠγόρασα) a field, and I have necessity (ἀνάγκην) to go out and see it"**:
  - Buying: the disciples' "unless we go and buy" (9:13). *Forward:* "they bought, they sold" in the days of Lot (17:28).
  - The own's necessity, against "compel (ἀνάγκασον) them to come in" (14:23).
- **"five yoke of oxen I bought, and I am going (πορεύομαι) to test (δοκιμάσαι) them"**:
  - The road verb, used of going to test what one has bought.
  - Testing: they test the face of heaven and earth, and not this time (12:56).
  - The ox: loosed and led to water (13:15).
- **"I married (ἔγημα) a wife, and therefore I cannot (οὐ δύναμαι) come"**:
  - *The one inside: "I cannot rise and give" (οὐ δύναμαι, 11:7).*
  - *Forward:* "they ate, they drank, they married (ἐγάμουν)… until the day Noah entered the ark" (17:27); "the sons of the resurrection neither marry" (20:35–36).
- The three excuses — buying a field, testing oxen, marrying — are the occupations of the days of Noah and Lot in the printed 17:27–28 ("ate, drank, married… bought, sold, planted, built").

### 14:21–22

*τότε ὀργισθεὶς ὁ οἰκοδεσπότης εἶπεν τῷ δούλῳ αὐτοῦ· Ἔξελθε ταχέως εἰς τὰς πλατείας καὶ ῥύμας τῆς πόλεως, καὶ τοὺς πτωχοὺς καὶ ἀναπείρους καὶ τυφλοὺς καὶ χωλοὺς εἰσάγαγε ὧδε. καὶ εἶπεν ὁ δοῦλος· Κύριε, γέγονεν ὃ ἐπέταξας, καὶ ἔτι τόπος ἐστίν.*

- **"the master of the house"** (12:39; 13:25).
- **"go out quickly into the streets (πλατείας) and lanes of the city"**:
  - *The streets:* the seventy go out into the streets of a city that does not receive (10:10). "You taught in our streets" was the claim of those left outside (13:26).
  - Into the same streets the servant goes to bring in others.
- **"the poor, the crippled, the blind and the lame"**:
  - The list of 14:13. The host does what Jesus told the host to do.
  - The healed of 7:21–22, now brought in.
- **"bring them in (εἰσάγαγε)"**: they do not enter by their own strength (13:24). They are brought in.
- **"what you commanded (ἐπέταξας) has been done"**: command (4:36; 8:25; 8:31).
- **"and there is still place (τόπος)"**: place. "Give place" and "the last place" (14:9–10, test). There is place.

### 14:23–24

*Ἔξελθε εἰς τὰς ὁδοὺς καὶ φραγμοὺς καὶ ἀνάγκασον εἰσελθεῖν, ἵνα γεμισθῇ μου ὁ οἶκος· λέγω γὰρ ὑμῖν ὅτι οὐδεὶς τῶν ἀνδρῶν ἐκείνων τῶν κεκλημένων γεύσεταί μου τοῦ δείπνου.*

- **"go out into the roads (ὁδούς) and hedges"**:
  - The way: those beside the way (8:5); those on the way (9:57); the man going down on the way (10:31, test).
  - Those found on the roads are brought in.
- **"compel (ἀνάγκασον) them to come in (εἰσελθεῖν)"**:
  - The own's necessity (14:18) kept the invited out. The host's necessity brings in those who were not invited.
  - Entering (11:52; 13:24).
- **"so that my house may be filled (γεμισθῇ)"**:
  - *Filling:* the inside "full (γέμει) of plunder" (11:39). Here the house is filled with the poor.
  - The boats filled (5:7); the days filled (9:51).
- **"none of those men who were invited will taste (γεύσεται) my dinner"**:
  - *Tasting:* "some… will not taste (γεύσωνται) death until they see the kingdom" (9:27). The only other place.
  - Those who refused do not taste the dinner.

### Test: 14:25–35 (U?)

*Συνεπορεύοντο δὲ αὐτῷ ὄχλοι πολλοί, καὶ στραφεὶς εἶπεν πρὸς αὐτούς· Εἴ τις ἔρχεται πρός με καὶ οὐ μισεῖ τὸν πατέρα ἑαυτοῦ καὶ τὴν μητέρα καὶ τὴν γυναῖκα καὶ τὰ τέκνα καὶ τοὺς ἀδελφοὺς καὶ τὰς ἀδελφάς, ἔτι τε καὶ τὴν ψυχὴν ἑαυτοῦ, οὐ δύναται εἶναί μου μαθητής. ὅστις οὐ βαστάζει τὸν σταυρὸν ἑαυτοῦ καὶ ἔρχεται ὀπίσω μου, οὐ δύναται εἶναί μου μαθητής. τίς γὰρ ἐξ ὑμῶν θέλων πύργον οἰκοδομῆσαι οὐχὶ πρῶτον καθίσας ψηφίζει τὴν δαπάνην… ἵνα μήποτε θέντος αὐτοῦ θεμέλιον καὶ μὴ ἰσχύοντος ἐκτελέσαι πάντες οἱ θεωροῦντες ἄρξωνται αὐτῷ ἐμπαίζειν… οὕτως οὖν πᾶς ἐξ ὑμῶν ὃς οὐκ ἀποτάσσεται πᾶσιν τοῖς ἑαυτοῦ ὑπάρχουσιν οὐ δύναται εἶναί μου μαθητής. Καλὸν οὖν τὸ ἅλας· ἐὰν δὲ καὶ τὸ ἅλας μωρανθῇ, ἐν τίνι ἀρτυθήσεται; οὔτε εἰς γῆν οὔτε εἰς κοπρίαν εὔθετόν ἐστιν· ἔξω βάλλουσιν αὐτό. ὁ ἔχων ὦτα ἀκούειν ἀκουέτω.*

- **"many crowds were going along with (συνεπορεύοντο) him"**: the road.
- **"whoever does not hate his own father and mother and wife and children and brothers and sisters, and even his own soul (ψυχὴν ἑαυτοῦ)"**:
  - The relations of 12:52–53, divided.
  - The wife of 14:20 ("I married a wife, and cannot come").
  - "Let the dead bury their dead" (9:60). "His own soul": "whoever wills to save his soul will lose it" (9:24).
- **"whoever does not carry (βαστάζει) his own cross and come behind me (ὀπίσω μου)"**: *9:23, the cross and "behind me".*
- **"who, wishing to build (οἰκοδομῆσαι) a tower, does not first sit and count the cost… lest, having laid a foundation (θεμέλιον) and not having the strength (ἰσχύοντος) to finish"**:
  - *Building and the foundation: the house on the rock with a foundation, the house on the earth without one (6:48–49).*
  - The tower: the tower in Siloam that fell (13:4, test).
  - Strength (13:24).
- **"all who watch begin to mock (ἐμπαίζειν) him"**: *forward: "the men holding him mocked (ἐνέπαιζον) him" (22:63); Herod mocking (23:11).*
- **"whoever does not say farewell to (ἀποτάσσεται) all his own possessions (ὑπάρχουσιν)"**:
  - *ἀποτάσσομαι: "first let me say farewell (ἀποτάξασθαι) to those at home" (9:61).*
  - Possessions (11:21; 12:15; 12:33, test).
- **"salt… if it becomes foolish (μωρανθῇ)"**: foolish ("fool", 12:20; 11:40).
- **"fit (εὔθετον) neither for the earth nor for the dunghill (κοπρίαν)"**: *"fit (εὔθετος) for the kingdom" (9:62); the manure (κόπρια) of the fig tree (13:8, test).*
- **"they throw it outside (ἔξω)"**: outside (13:25, 13:28).
- **"whoever has ears to hear, let him hear"**: *8:8.*

**Verdict: continues.**
- Every phrase is in a thread. It sums up 9:23, 9:57–62 and 6:48–49 as conditions of the road.
- 14:24 → 15:1 runs: "none will taste my dinner" → "the tax collectors and sinners drew near to hear him… he receives sinners and eats with them". With the passage, 14:35's "let him hear" leads into 15:1's "to hear him". So it does not close a gap.

### What came out of the reading of chapter 12

**The host's half of the meal teaching.**
- Do not call those who can call you back.
- Make a reception (δοχή, the word of Levi's feast, 5:29) for the poor, the crippled, the lame, the blind. Those are the ones he healed.
- The repayment is "the resurrection (ἀνάστασις) of the righteous". This is the first time the noun of rising appears in the printed text, and it is promised for giving without return.
- The guest's half, "go up higher" (14:10), is in the unprinted 14:1–11.

**The great dinner.**
- Everything is ready from the host's side.
- The invited excuse themselves with the own's necessities:
  - a field bought, which must be seen;
  - oxen bought, which must be tested — "I am going" (πορεύομαι), the road verb, used for going to one's purchase;
  - a wife married: "I cannot come", the words of the one inside who "cannot rise and give" (11:7).
- These are the occupations of the days of Noah and Lot (17:27–28).
- The master sends into the streets — the streets of 10:10 and 13:26 — and the roads. He brings in the poor, crippled, blind and lame, and compels the rest, so that his house is filled. There is still place.
- The invited do not taste the dinner. "Taste" is the word of 9:27, "will not taste death".

**Entering in this chapter is being brought in.** In chapter 11 the door was narrow and many had not the strength (13:24), and the master rose to shut it (13:25). Here the master sends out and brings in those who have nothing. "Bring in", "compel to come in": they do not enter; they are brought.

### Threads, carried forward and added

- the dinner (→ 22:20);
- calling and being called;
- repayment (ἀνταποδίδωμι);
- reception (δοχή, 5:29 / 14:13);
- the poor, crippled, lame, blind (→ 16:20–22; 18:35; 19:8);
- resurrection (ἀνάστασις, → 20:27–36);
- eating bread in the kingdom (→ 22:16–18);
- ready (→ 22:8–12, 22:33);
- the hour (→ 22:14);
- excusing;
- buying, testing, marrying (→ 17:27–28; 20:34–36);
- "I cannot";
- the streets;
- bringing in;
- compelling;
- place;
- the house filled;
- tasting (9:27 / 14:24).

## Chapter 13

*15:1–10. Grey: 15:1–2. Not printed: 15:11–32 (A−), after 15:10.*

### 15:1–2 (grey)

*Ἦσαν δὲ αὐτῷ ἐγγίζοντες πάντες οἱ τελῶναι καὶ οἱ ἁμαρτωλοὶ ἀκούειν αὐτοῦ. καὶ διεγόγγυζον οἵ τε Φαρισαῖοι καὶ οἱ γραμματεῖς λέγοντες ὅτι Οὗτος ἁμαρτωλοὺς προσδέχεται καὶ συνεσθίει αὐτοῖς.*

- **"all the tax collectors and sinners were drawing near (ἐγγίζοντες) to him to hear him"**:
  - Drawing near: the kingdom "has come near" (10:9, 10:11). Now the sinners draw near.
  - To hear (8:8; 9:35; 14:35, test).
- **"the Pharisees and scribes grumbled (διεγόγγυζον)"**:
  - *Grumbling (γογγύζω): at Levi's feast (5:30); here; and at Zacchaeus' house, "he has gone in to lodge with a sinful man" (19:7). The only places.*
  - The three meals with sinners are joined by this one verb.
- **"this man receives (προσδέχεται) sinners and eats with them"**:
  - προσδέχομαι: "men waiting for (προσδεχομένοις) their lord" (12:36).
  - What the watchful do for their lord, he does for sinners. Reception.

### 15:3–4

*Τίς ἄνθρωπος ἐξ ὑμῶν ἔχων ἑκατὸν πρόβατα καὶ ἀπολέσας ἐξ αὐτῶν ἓν οὐ καταλείπει τὰ ἐνενήκοντα ἐννέα ἐν τῇ ἐρήμῳ καὶ πορεύεται ἐπὶ τὸ ἀπολωλὸς ἕως εὕρῃ αὐτό;*

- **"a hundred sheep"**: a hundred, the full (8:8; 7:2). The sheep: the "little flock" (12:32); lambs (10:3).
- **"having lost (ἀπολέσας) one"**:
  - Losing, ἀπόλλυμι: perishing (4:34; 8:24; 9:24; 13:3, test). The same verb is "destroy", "perish" and "lose".
  - *Forward: "the Son of Man came to seek and to save the lost (τὸ ἀπολωλός)" (19:10).*
- **"does he not leave (καταλείπει) the ninety-nine in the desert (ἐρήμῳ)"**:
  - Leaving: *Levi, "leaving (καταλιπών) everything, rising, followed him" (5:28).* What Levi did to follow him, the shepherd does to go after the one.
  - The desert (4:42; 8:29; 9:12).
- **"and go (πορεύεται) after the lost one until he finds it?"**: the road, walked toward the lost.

### 15:5–6

*καὶ εὑρὼν ἐπιτίθησιν ἐπὶ τοὺς ὤμους αὐτοῦ χαίρων, καὶ ἐλθὼν εἰς τὸν οἶκον συγκαλεῖ τοὺς φίλους καὶ τοὺς γείτονας, λέγων αὐτοῖς· Συγχάρητέ μοι ὅτι εὗρον τὸ πρόβατόν μου τὸ ἀπολωλός.*

- **"finding it, he lays it on his shoulders, rejoicing"**:
  - The lost one does not walk back; it is carried.
  - "Lays on" (ἐπιτίθησιν): the verb of the hands laid on the bent woman (13:13).
- **"coming into the house, he calls together the friends and neighbours"**:
  - Friends (11:5–8; 12:4).
  - "Neighbours": "do not call… rich neighbours" (14:12). Here the neighbours are called to share joy, not to repay.
- **"rejoice with me"**: joy (10:17, 10:20–21; 13:17).

### 15:7

*λέγω ὑμῖν ὅτι οὕτως χαρὰ ἐν τῷ οὐρανῷ ἔσται ἐπὶ ἑνὶ ἁμαρτωλῷ μετανοοῦντι ἢ ἐπὶ ἐνενήκοντα ἐννέα δικαίοις οἵτινες οὐ χρείαν ἔχουσιν μετανοίας.*

- **"joy in heaven over one sinner who repents (μετανοοῦντι)"**:
  - Repenting: "I came to call sinners to repentance (μετάνοιαν)" (5:32). *Forward:* "if someone goes to them from the dead, they will repent" (16:30); "repentance… proclaimed to all the nations" (24:47).
  - The finding is called repenting. In the parable, the sheep does nothing and is found; the text calls that repentance.
- **"than over ninety-nine righteous (δικαίοις) who have no need (χρείαν) of repentance"**:
  - *"Those who are well have no need (χρείαν) of a physician" (5:31).*
  - The righteous: "I did not come to call the righteous" (5:32). *Forward:* "some who trusted in themselves that they were righteous" (18:9).

### 15:8–10

*Ἢ τίς γυνὴ δραχμὰς ἔχουσα δέκα, ἐὰν ἀπολέσῃ δραχμὴν μίαν, οὐχὶ ἅπτει λύχνον καὶ σαροῖ τὴν οἰκίαν καὶ ζητεῖ ἐπιμελῶς ἕως οὗ εὕρῃ; καὶ εὑροῦσα συγκαλεῖ τὰς φίλας καὶ γείτονας λέγουσα· Συγχάρητέ μοι ὅτι εὗρον τὴν δραχμὴν ἣν ἀπώλεσα. οὕτως, λέγω ὑμῖν, γίνεται χαρὰ ἐνώπιον τῶν ἀγγέλων τοῦ θεοῦ ἐπὶ ἑνὶ ἁμαρτωλῷ μετανοοῦντι.*

- **"ten drachmas… if she loses one"**: a silver coin, a truth (?). Ten: what is kept, the remains (?).
- **"does she not light a lamp (ἅπτει λύχνον)"**: the lamp (8:16; 11:33; 12:35). The lamp is lit to find.
- **"and sweep (σαροῖ) the house"**:
  - *The house "swept (σεσαρωμένον) and adorned", empty, and reoccupied by seven worse spirits (11:25, test).* The same act, sweeping the house, either leaves it empty or finds what was lost.
  - The lamp makes the difference.
- **"and seek (ζητεῖ) carefully until she finds"**: "seek, and you will find" (11:9). Here the seeker is the woman, and what she seeks is what she lost.
- **"joy before the angels of God over one sinner who repents"**: the angels of God, named here in the printed text (cf. 12:8–9).

### Test: 15:11–32 (A−)

*Ἄνθρωπός τις εἶχεν δύο υἱούς. καὶ εἶπεν ὁ νεώτερος αὐτῶν τῷ πατρί· Πάτερ, δός μοι τὸ ἐπιβάλλον μέρος τῆς οὐσίας· ὁ δὲ διεῖλεν αὐτοῖς τὸν βίον. καὶ μετ’ οὐ πολλὰς ἡμέρας συναγαγὼν πάντα ὁ νεώτερος υἱὸς ἀπεδήμησεν εἰς χώραν μακράν, καὶ ἐκεῖ διεσκόρπισεν τὴν οὐσίαν αὐτοῦ ζῶν ἀσώτως… καὶ πορευθεὶς ἐκολλήθη ἑνὶ τῶν πολιτῶν τῆς χώρας ἐκείνης, καὶ ἔπεμψεν αὐτὸν εἰς τοὺς ἀγροὺς αὐτοῦ βόσκειν χοίρους· καὶ ἐπεθύμει γεμίσαι τὴν κοιλίαν αὐτοῦ ἀπὸ τῶν κερατίων ὧν ἤσθιον οἱ χοῖροι, καὶ οὐδεὶς ἐδίδου αὐτῷ. εἰς ἑαυτὸν δὲ ἐλθὼν ἔφη· Πόσοι μίσθιοι τοῦ πατρός μου περισσεύονται ἄρτων, ἐγὼ δὲ λιμῷ ὧδε ἀπόλλυμαι· ἀναστὰς πορεύσομαι πρὸς τὸν πατέρα μου… καὶ ἀναστὰς ἦλθεν πρὸς τὸν πατέρα ἑαυτοῦ. ἔτι δὲ αὐτοῦ μακρὰν ἀπέχοντος εἶδεν αὐτὸν ὁ πατὴρ αὐτοῦ καὶ ἐσπλαγχνίσθη καὶ δραμὼν ἐπέπεσεν ἐπὶ τὸν τράχηλον αὐτοῦ καὶ κατεφίλησεν αὐτόν… Ταχὺ ἐξενέγκατε στολὴν τὴν πρώτην καὶ ἐνδύσατε αὐτόν, καὶ δότε δακτύλιον εἰς τὴν χεῖρα αὐτοῦ καὶ ὑποδήματα εἰς τοὺς πόδας… ὅτι οὗτος ὁ υἱός μου νεκρὸς ἦν καὶ ἀνέζησεν, ἦν ἀπολωλὼς καὶ εὑρέθη… ὠργίσθη δὲ καὶ οὐκ ἤθελεν εἰσελθεῖν. ὁ δὲ πατὴρ αὐτοῦ ἐξελθὼν παρεκάλει αὐτόν… Τέκνον, σὺ πάντοτε μετ’ ἐμοῦ εἶ, καὶ πάντα τὰ ἐμὰ σά ἐστιν· εὐφρανθῆναι δὲ καὶ χαρῆναι ἔδει, ὅτι ὁ ἀδελφός σου οὗτος νεκρὸς ἦν καὶ ἔζησεν, καὶ ἀπολωλὼς καὶ εὑρέθη.*

- **"give me the portion (μέρος) of the substance that falls to me"**:
  - The own asks for its part. Mary's "good part" (10:42, test); the servant's "part with the unfaithful" (12:46).
  - The father divides (διεῖλεν) his living (βίον). He would not divide the inheritance between brothers (12:13–14); here the father divides.
  - βίος: the woman spent her whole living (βίον) on physicians (8:43).
- **"gathering (συναγαγών) everything, he went away into a far country (χώραν μακράν)"**:
  - Gathering for oneself (12:17–18).
  - *"A far country" (χώραν μακράν): the printed 19:12, "a nobleman went into a far country (χώραν μακράν) to receive a kingdom and return".*
- **"and there he scattered (διεσκόρπισεν) his substance"**:
  - *"Whoever does not gather with me scatters (σκορπίζει)" (11:23).*
  - *The steward "accused of scattering (διασκορπίζων) his possessions" (16:1), the next printed verse.*
- **"going (πορευθείς), he joined himself (ἐκολλήθη) to one of the citizens… who sent him into his fields to feed swine (βόσκειν χοίρους)"**:
  - The road, going down.
  - Joining: *"the dust that clings (κολληθέντα) to our feet" (10:11).*
  - *The swine: the herd feeding (βοσκομένη) on the mountain, and the feeders (βόσκοντες) who fled (8:32–34).* He becomes a feeder of swine.
- **"he longed (ἐπεθύμει) to fill (γεμίσαι) his belly… and no one gave to him"**:
  - *Longing: Lazarus "longing (ἐπιθυμῶν) to be filled from what fell from the rich man's table" (16:21).*
  - Filling: "that my house may be filled (γεμισθῇ)" (14:23).
- **"coming into himself (εἰς ἑαυτὸν ἐλθών)"**:
  - *The self.* "Let him deny himself" (9:23); "what does it profit… losing himself" (9:25).
  - Here the turn is to come into oneself. It is the inside (ἔσωθεν, 11:39–40), reached.
- **"my father's hired servants have bread in overflow (περισσεύονται)"**: the overflow (9:17; 12:15). Bread (11:3).
- **"and I perish (ἀπόλλυμαι) here"**: perishing (8:24: "we are perishing").
- **"rising (ἀναστάς), I will go (πορεύσομαι) to my father"**:
  - *Rising and going, joined, toward the father.* Both are threads that the text has carried since 4:29–30 and 4:42.
  - "Rising" again in 15:20: "and rising, he came".
- **"I sinned against heaven and before you; I am no longer worthy to be called your son"**: the centurion, "I am not worthy" (7:6–7). Being called (14:7–24).
- **"while he was still far off (μακρὰν ἀπέχοντος)"**: *the centurion's "when he was not far off (οὐ μακρὰν ἀπέχοντος)" (7:6), the same words.*
- **"his father saw him and had compassion (ἐσπλαγχνίσθη)"**: the Lord at Nain (7:13); the Samaritan (10:33, test).
- **"running, he fell on his neck and kissed him (κατεφίλησεν)"**:
  - *κατεφίλησεν: the woman "kissed (κατεφίλει)" his feet (7:38, 7:45).* *Forward: Judas draws near "to kiss (φιλῆσαι) him" (22:47–48).*
  - Running: *Zacchaeus "running ahead" (προδραμών, 19:4).*
- **"bring out the first (πρώτην) robe and clothe (ἐνδύσατε) him"**:
  - The first: the own sought the first places (11:43; 14:7–10). Here the first robe is given to the one who asked to be a hired servant.
  - *Clothing:* Legion clothed (8:35); "what you will put on (ἐνδύσησθε)" (12:22); "God so clothes" (12:28a, test). *Forward:* "until you are clothed (ἐνδύσησθε) with power from on high" (24:49, unprinted).
- **"a ring for his hand, and sandals for his feet"**:
  - The hand (8:54; 13:13).
  - *Sandals: "carry no… sandals (ὑποδήματα)" (10:4).* On the road one goes without. At the return they are given.
- **"this my son was dead and lives again (ἀνέζησεν)"**:
  - *ἀνα-ζάω, "live up again".* Living: "do this and you will live (ζήσῃ)" (10:28). *Forward:* "why do you seek the Living One (τὸν ζῶντα) among the dead?" (24:5).
  - Dead: "let the dead bury their dead" (9:60); "she is not dead but sleeps" (8:52).
- **"he was lost (ἀπολωλώς) and is found (εὑρέθη)"**: the words of 15:4–6 and 19:10.
- **"the elder son… drew near (ἤγγισεν) the house… he was angry (ὠργίσθη) and would not go in (εἰσελθεῖν)"**:
  - *Angry: the master of the house, "angry" (ὀργισθείς), brought in the poor (14:21).* Here the son is angry because the poor one is brought in.
  - Not wishing to enter (11:52; 13:24; 14:23). The one who will not go in stands outside (13:25).
  - The healthy (ὑγιαίνοντα, 15:27), against 5:31.
- **"so many years I serve (δουλεύω) you, and never passed by (παρῆλθον) your command"**:
  - Serving: *"no one can serve (δουλεύειν) two masters" (16:13).*
  - Passing by: "you pass by (παρέρχεσθε) the calling and the love of God" (11:42).
  - The ninety-nine righteous "who have no need of repentance" (15:7), in person.
- **"the father came out and urged him"**: the father goes out to the one who will not come in, as he ran to the one who came.
- **"Child, you are always with me, and all that is mine is yours"**: *"all things were handed over to me by my Father" (10:22).*
- **"it was necessary (ἔδει) to make merry and rejoice"**:
  - The "must" of the passion (9:22) and of the loosing (13:16), here the must of joy.
  - Making merry (εὐφραίνω): the rich man told his soul to make merry (12:19); *the rich man of 16:19 "made merry (εὐφραινόμενος) daily".* The merriment of the own, and the merriment of the found.

**Verdict: continues.**
- Every phrase is in a thread of the printed text, and the threads reach 5:28, 5:31, 7:6, 7:13, 7:38, 8:32–34, 10:4, 10:11, 10:22, 11:23, 12:13–19, 12:22, 13:16, 13:25, 14:21–23, 16:1, 16:13, 16:21, 19:4, 19:10, 19:12 and 24:5.
- Its mechanic is the one the printed 15:7 and 15:10 name and do not show. The printed parables show the seeking from the Divine's side: the shepherd goes, the woman lights a lamp; the lost is found and carried. They call that "a sinner who repents". 15:11–32 shows the turn from the other side:
  - the own asks for its part, gathers, goes far, scatters;
  - it joins itself to another, feeds swine, longs to be filled, and no one gives;
  - it comes into itself, rises and goes to the father;
  - it is seen while far off, met running, clothed, given back hand and feet;
  - it was dead and lives again.
- The elder son is the ninety-nine righteous of 15:7, outside, angry, unwilling to enter, and urged to.
- It is the fullest statement in the text of rising (ἀναστάς, twice) joined to going (πορεύσομαι), toward the father, and of living again (ἀνέζησεν).
- 15:10 → 16:1 runs ("he said also to the disciples…"). With the passage, 16:1's "scattering" would follow 15:13's "scattered". So it does not close a gap.
- A−: restoring it would be against Marcion's text.

### What came out of the reading of chapter 13

**The Divine seeks.**
- Sinners draw near, and he receives them (προσδέχεται) and eats with them. That is grumbled at, as at Levi's (5:30) and at Zacchaeus' (19:7).
- The shepherd leaves the ninety-nine, as Levi left everything (5:28). He goes after the lost one, finds it, and carries it on his shoulders. The lost one does not walk back; it is carried.
- The woman lights a lamp and sweeps the house until she finds. It is the sweeping that, in the test passage 11:25, left a house empty for worse spirits.
- Joy in heaven, before the angels of God, over one sinner who repents. More than over ninety-nine righteous who have no need of it: the healthy who need no physician (5:31).

**What repenting is, in the printed text and in the test passage.**
- In the printed parables, repenting is being found and carried.
- In 15:11–32 it is the turn inside the one who is lost:
  - coming into oneself;
  - rising and going to the father;
  - being met while far off;
  - being clothed with the first robe;
  - living again.
- The printed text names this mechanic twice (15:7, 15:10). The test passage shows it. By its words and by its mechanic it continues the line. It does not close a gap in the printed text. A−.

**Being carried, being clothed.** The found sheep is carried on the shoulders. The returning son is clothed with the first robe, by others. In these passages the rising of the lost is not by their own strength. "He rose and came" is the one act of the son. After it, everything is done to him.

### Threads, carried forward and added

- drawing near (sinners);
- grumbling (5:30 / 15:2 / 19:7);
- receiving (προσδέχομαι);
- the lost (→ 19:10);
- leaving (5:28 / 15:4);
- the desert;
- carried on the shoulders;
- the lamp and the sweeping (11:25 / 15:8);
- seeking until finding;
- joy in heaven, before the angels;
- repenting (→ 16:30; 17:3–4; 24:47);
- the righteous who need nothing;
- and, from the test passage:
  - the part asked for;
  - the far country (→ 19:12);
  - scattering (→ 16:1);
  - the swine;
  - longing to be filled (→ 16:21);
  - coming into oneself;
  - rising and going to the father;
  - "far off" (7:6);
  - the kiss (7:38, 7:45 → 22:47–48);
  - the first robe;
  - the ring and the sandals (10:4);
  - "lives again" (→ 24:5);
  - the elder son outside;
  - "all that is mine is yours" (10:22);
  - the must of joy.

## Chapter 14

*16:1–31. Every verse is printed. Grey: 16:1, 16:3, 16:8, 16:10. Adopted Marcionite wordings: 16:12 "what is mine"; 16:17 "of my words" for "of the law"; 16:23 "in his bosom" (singular).*

### 16:1–2 (16:1 grey)

*Ἔλεγεν δὲ καὶ πρὸς τοὺς μαθητάς· Ἄνθρωπός τις ἦν πλούσιος ὃς εἶχεν οἰκονόμον, καὶ οὗτος διεβλήθη αὐτῷ ὡς διασκορπίζων τὰ ὑπάρχοντα αὐτοῦ. καὶ φωνήσας αὐτὸν εἶπεν αὐτῷ· Τί τοῦτο ἀκούω περὶ σοῦ; ἀπόδος τὸν λόγον τῆς οἰκονομίας σου, οὐ γὰρ δύνῃ ἔτι οἰκονομεῖν.*

- **"a steward (οἰκονόμον)"**: *the faithful and prudent steward, set over the household to give the measure of grain (12:42).*
- **"he was accused (διεβλήθη)"**: διαβάλλω, "throw across". It is the root of διάβολος: "the devil (διάβολος) comes and takes the word" (8:12, test).
- **"of scattering (διασκορπίζων) his possessions"**:
  - Scattering: "whoever does not gather with me scatters" (11:23). *The younger son "scattered (διεσκόρπισεν) his substance" (15:13, test).*
  - Possessions (11:21; 12:15, 12:44).
- **"give back (ἀπόδος) the account (λόγον) of your stewardship"**:
  - Giving back: "until you give back (ἀποδῷς) the last coin" (12:59).
  - λόγος: the account is a word.
- **"you can no longer be steward"**: what was entrusted (12:48) is taken back.

### 16:3–4 (16:3 grey)

*εἶπεν δὲ ἐν ἑαυτῷ ὁ οἰκονόμος· Τί ποιήσω ὅτι ὁ κύριός μου ἀφαιρεῖται τὴν οἰκονομίαν ἀπ’ ἐμοῦ; σκάπτειν οὐκ ἰσχύω, ἐπαιτεῖν αἰσχύνομαι· ἔγνων τί ποιήσω, ἵνα ὅταν μετασταθῶ ἐκ τῆς οἰκονομίας δέξωνταί με εἰς τοὺς οἴκους ἑαυτῶν.*

- **"he said within himself (ἐν ἑαυτῷ): What shall I do (Τί ποιήσω)?"**:
  - *The rich man: "he reasoned within himself (ἐν ἑαυτῷ), saying, What shall I do?" (12:17).* The same words.
  - The rich man answered: build greater barns. The steward will answer: let go what is owed.
  - The younger son "came into himself" (15:17, test).
- **"my lord is taking away (ἀφαιρεῖται) the stewardship from me"**: taking away. *Mary's good part "will not be taken away (ἀφαιρεθήσεται)" (10:42, test).* What is held as a stewardship is taken away.
- **"to dig (σκάπτειν) I have not the strength (οὐκ ἰσχύω)"**:
  - Digging: the wise builder "dug (ἔσκαψεν) and went deep" (6:48); the vinedresser "dig around it" (13:8, test).
  - Strength: "many… will not have the strength (ἰσχύσουσιν)" (13:24).
- **"to beg (ἐπαιτεῖν) I am ashamed (αἰσχύνομαι)"**:
  - Begging: *forward: the blind man "sat beside the way begging (ἐπαιτῶν)" (18:35).*
  - Shame (9:26; 13:17; 14:9, test).
- **"so that when I am removed… they will receive (δέξωνται) me into their houses"**: reception (9:5; 9:53; 10:8–10; 15:2).

### 16:5–7

*καὶ προσκαλεσάμενος ἕνα ἕκαστον τῶν χρεοφειλετῶν τοῦ κυρίου ἑαυτοῦ ἔλεγεν τῷ πρώτῳ· Πόσον ὀφείλεις τῷ κυρίῳ μου; ὁ δὲ εἶπεν· Ἑκατὸν βάτους ἐλαίου· ὁ δὲ εἶπεν αὐτῷ· Δέξαι σου τὰ γράμματα καὶ καθίσας ταχέως γράψον πεντήκοντα. ἔπειτα ἑτέρῳ εἶπεν· Σὺ δὲ πόσον ὀφείλεις; ὁ δὲ εἶπεν· Ἑκατὸν κόρους σίτου· λέγει αὐτῷ· Δέξαι σου τὰ γράμματα καὶ γράψον ὀγδοήκοντα.*

- **"his lord's debtors (χρεοφειλετῶν)"**:
  - *χρεοφειλέτης: the only other place is the two debtors of 7:41, one owing five hundred and one fifty, both forgiven ("he graced them both").*
  - The steward does what the creditor of 7:41–42 did, with his lord's goods.
- **"How much do you owe (ὀφείλεις)?"**: "for we ourselves let go everyone who owes (ὀφείλοντι) us" (11:4).
- **"a hundred baths of oil"**:
  - Oil: "you did not anoint my head with oil" (7:46). The Samaritan's oil (10:34, test).
  - A hundred: the full (8:8; 15:4).
- **"take your bill (δέξαι) and… write fifty"**: "receive" (δέξαι) is the reception verb. The debtor receives the letting go.
- **"a hundred kors of wheat (σίτου)… write eighty"**: *the grain:* the rich man's grain (12:18); the steward's "measure of grain" (12:42). The grain owed is let go in part.
- What is owed to the lord is reduced. The steward gives what is not his, as release of debts.

### 16:8–9 (16:8 grey)

*καὶ ἐπῄνεσεν ὁ κύριος τὸν οἰκονόμον τῆς ἀδικίας ὅτι φρονίμως ἐποίησεν· ὅτι οἱ υἱοὶ τοῦ αἰῶνος τούτου φρονιμώτεροι ὑπὲρ τοὺς υἱοὺς τοῦ φωτὸς εἰς τὴν γενεὰν τὴν ἑαυτῶν εἰσιν. καὶ ἐγὼ ὑμῖν λέγω, ἑαυτοῖς ποιήσατε φίλους ἐκ τοῦ μαμωνᾶ τῆς ἀδικίας, ἵνα ὅταν ἐκλίπῃ δέξωνται ὑμᾶς εἰς τὰς αἰωνίους σκηνάς.*

- **"the lord praised the steward of unrighteousness (ἀδικίας)"**: "workers of unrighteousness" (13:27).
- **"because he acted prudently (φρονίμως)"**: *"the faithful and prudent (φρόνιμος) steward" (12:42).*
- **"the sons of this age (αἰῶνος)… the sons of light (φωτός)"**:
  - The age: *forward:* "the sons of this age marry… those counted worthy of that age… sons of the resurrection" (20:34–36).
  - Light (8:16; 11:33; 12:3).
- **"make friends for yourselves from the mammon of unrighteousness"**: friends (11:5–8; 12:4; 15:6, 15:9). Mammon is what is relied on (?).
- **"so that when it fails (ἐκλίπῃ)"**: failing. "An unfailing (ἀνέκλειπτον) treasure" (12:33, test). *Forward:* "that your faith may not fail (ἐκλίπῃ)" (22:32).
- **"they may receive (δέξωνται) you into the eternal tents (σκηνάς)"**:
  - *The tents:*
    - Peter wanted to make three tents on the mountain (9:33);
    - the birds of heaven have dwellings (κατασκηνώσεις), but the Son of Man has nowhere to lay his head (9:58);
    - the birds dwell (κατεσκήνωσεν) in the tree of the kingdom (13:19).
  - Here the eternal tents are where one is received, through what was given away.
  - A dwelling is not built on the height. It is received at the end of what was let go.

### 16:10–12 (16:10 grey)

*Ὁ πιστὸς ἐν ἐλαχίστῳ καὶ ἐν πολλῷ πιστός ἐστιν… εἰ οὖν ἐν τῷ ἀδίκῳ μαμωνᾷ πιστοὶ οὐκ ἐγένεσθε, τὸ ἀληθινὸν τίς ὑμῖν πιστεύσει; καὶ εἰ ἐν τῷ ἀλλοτρίῳ πιστοὶ οὐκ ἐγένεσθε, τὸ ἐμὸν τίς ὑμῖν δώσει;*

- **"faithful in the least (ἐλαχίστῳ)"**: "if you cannot do even the least" (12:26). *Forward:* "because you were faithful in a very little (ἐλαχίστῳ), have authority over ten cities" (19:17).
- **"who will entrust to you the true (ἀληθινόν)?"**: faith; the true.
- **"if in what is another's (ἀλλοτρίῳ) you were not faithful, who will give you what is mine (τὸ ἐμόν)?"**:
  - What one holds is another's: the stewardship is the lord's (16:2–3).
  - "What is mine" is the adopted Marcionite reading. NA27 has "what is your own" (τὸ ὑμέτερον).
  - By the threads, the two readings meet in one sentence of the test passage: *"all that is mine (τὰ ἐμά) is yours (σά)" (15:31).* Also "all things were handed over to me" (10:22).
  - **Neutral.** What is given is his, and it becomes yours.

### 16:13

*οὐδεὶς οἰκέτης δύναται δυσὶ κυρίοις δουλεύειν· ἢ γὰρ τὸν ἕνα μισήσει καὶ τὸν ἕτερον ἀγαπήσει, ἢ ἑνὸς ἀνθέξεται καὶ τοῦ ἑτέρου καταφρονήσει. οὐ δύνασθε θεῷ δουλεύειν καὶ μαμωνᾷ.*

- **"no household servant can serve (δουλεύειν) two lords"**: serving. *The elder son, "so many years I serve (δουλεύω) you" (15:29, test).*
- **"he will hate the one and love the other"**: hate and love. "Love your enemies, do good to those who hate you" (6:27). *Forward:* "his citizens hated (ἐμίσουν) him" (19:14); "you will be hated by all" (21:17).
- **"or hold to (ἀνθέξεται) the one"**: holding (4:42; 8:15, test).
- **"you cannot serve God and mammon"**: the house divided (11:17). There is no middle (11:23).

### 16:14–15

*Ἤκουον δὲ ταῦτα πάντα οἱ Φαρισαῖοι φιλάργυροι ὑπάρχοντες, καὶ ἐξεμυκτήριζον αὐτόν. καὶ εἶπεν αὐτοῖς· Ὑμεῖς ἐστε οἱ δικαιοῦντες ἑαυτοὺς ἐνώπιον τῶν ἀνθρώπων, ὁ δὲ θεὸς γινώσκει τὰς καρδίας ὑμῶν· ὅτι τὸ ἐν ἀνθρώποις ὑψηλὸν βδέλυγμα ἐνώπιον τοῦ θεοῦ.*

- **"lovers of silver"**: love turned to silver (11:43: "you love the first seat").
- **"they sneered at (ἐξεμυκτήριζον) him"**: *forward: at the cross, "the rulers sneered (ἐξεμυκτήριζον)" (23:35, in the unprinted part of chapter 23).*
- **"you are those who justify yourselves (ἑαυτούς) before men"**: *"wishing to justify himself" (10:29, test). Forward:* "who trusted in themselves (ἐφ’ ἑαυτοῖς) that they were righteous" (18:9).
- **"God knows your hearts"**: he knows the reasonings of the heart (9:47).
- **"what is high (ὑψηλόν) among men is an abomination before God"**:
  - *The height of the own.* What men hold to be high is abhorrent before God.
  - Capernaum lifted to heaven goes down (10:15, test). *Forward: "whoever lifts himself up (ὑψῶν) will be brought low" (18:14 = 14:11, test).*
  - No one adds a cubit to his stature (12:25).
  - *Here the printed text says outright that the height men give themselves is not the height before God.*

### 16:16–17

*Ὁ νόμος καὶ οἱ προφῆται μέχρι Ἰωάννου· ἀπὸ τότε ἡ βασιλεία τοῦ θεοῦ εὐαγγελίζεται καὶ πᾶς εἰς αὐτὴν βιάζεται. Εὐκοπώτερον δέ ἐστιν τὸν οὐρανὸν καὶ τὴν γῆν παρελθεῖν ἢ τῶν λόγων μου μίαν κεραίαν πεσεῖν.*

- **"the law and the prophets until John"**: Moses and Elijah on the mountain (9:30). John, who prepares the way (7:27).
- **"from then the kingdom of God is proclaimed as good news, and everyone forces (βιάζεται) his way into it"**:
  - Forcing. *Forward: at Emmaus, "they constrained (παρεβιάσαντο) him, saying, Stay with us" (24:29).*
  - The force of entering: "strive to enter" (13:24); "compel them to come in" (14:23).
- **"easier for heaven and earth to pass away (παρελθεῖν) than for one stroke of my words to fall (πεσεῖν)"**:
  - Heaven and earth, paired (12:56; 21:33).
  - "Of my words" is the adopted Marcionite reading. The apparatus records that it was adopted against Roth's grade. NA27 has "of the law".
  - By the threads:
    - *"my words" joins 21:33: "heaven and earth will pass away, but my words will not pass away";*
    - *"the law" joins 16:16 ("the law and the prophets until John") and 16:29–31 ("they have Moses and the prophets; let them hear them").*
  - **Neutral:** each continues.
  - Falling (6:49; 8:5–8).

### 16:18

*Πᾶς ὁ ἀπολύων τὴν γυναῖκα αὐτοῦ καὶ γαμῶν ἑτέραν μοιχεύει, καὶ ὁ ἀπολελυμένην ἀπὸ ἀνδρὸς γαμῶν μοιχεύει.*

- **"whoever releases (ἀπολύων) his wife and marries another commits adultery"**:
  - *The λύω family:*
    - the bent woman "loosed" from Satan's bond (13:12, 13:16);
    - "release, and you will be released" (6:37).
  - Here the loosing is the wrong one: the loosing of a marriage.
  - Marriage is the joining of good and truth; adultery is the falsifying of it (?).
  - Loosing from a bond of evil frees. Loosing a marriage to join another adulterates.
- **"marries (γαμῶν)"**: "I married a wife, and I cannot come" (14:20). *Forward:* 17:27; "the sons of the resurrection neither marry" (20:35).

### 16:19–21

*Ἄνθρωπος δέ τις ἦν πλούσιος, καὶ ἐνεδιδύσκετο πορφύραν καὶ βύσσον εὐφραινόμενος καθ’ ἡμέραν λαμπρῶς. πτωχὸς δέ τις ὀνόματι Λάζαρος ἐβέβλητο πρὸς τὸν πυλῶνα αὐτοῦ εἱλκωμένος καὶ ἐπιθυμῶν χορτασθῆναι ἀπὸ τῶν πιπτόντων ἀπὸ τῆς τραπέζης τοῦ πλουσίου· ἀλλὰ καὶ οἱ κύνες ἐρχόμενοι ἐπέλειχον τὰ ἕλκη αὐτοῦ.*

- **"a rich man, clothed (ἐνεδιδύσκετο) in purple and fine linen"**:
  - *Clothing:* "do not be anxious… what you will put on" (12:22); Solomon in his glory (12:27).
  - *Forward: Herod, putting "bright (λαμπράν) clothing" on him (23:11).*
- **"making merry (εὐφραινόμενος) daily (καθ’ ἡμέραν), brightly (λαμπρῶς)"**:
  - *The rich man of 12:19: "soul… eat, drink, be merry (εὐφραίνου)".*
  - Daily: the cross daily (9:23), the bread daily (11:3). Here the merriment is daily.
- **"a poor man named Lazarus was thrown (ἐβέβλητο) at his gate (πυλῶνα)"**:
  - The poor (6:20; 14:13, 14:21).
  - Thrown: casting (12:5, 12:49, 12:58).
  - The gate: the gate of Nain (πύλη, 7:12), where the dead son was carried out.
- **"longing (ἐπιθυμῶν) to be filled (χορτασθῆναι) from what fell (πιπτόντων) from the rich man's table (τραπέζης)"**:
  - *Longing: the younger son "longed (ἐπεθύμει) to fill his belly… and no one gave to him" (15:16, test).*
  - *To be filled: "blessed who hunger now, for you will be filled (χορτασθήσεσθε)" (6:21); "all were filled (ἐχορτάσθησαν)" (9:17).*
  - What falls from the table. *Forward:* "the hand of the one who hands me over is with me on the table (τραπέζης)" (22:21).
- **"even the dogs came and licked his sores"**.

### 16:22

*ἐγένετο δὲ ἀποθανεῖν τὸν πτωχὸν καὶ ἀπενεχθῆναι αὐτὸν ὑπὸ τῶν ἀγγέλων εἰς τὸν κόλπον Ἀβραάμ· ἀπέθανεν δὲ καὶ ὁ πλούσιος καὶ ἐτάφη.*

- **"the poor man died, and was carried away (ἀπενεχθῆναι) by the angels"**:
  - *He is carried.* The lost sheep is carried on the shoulders (15:5). The days of his "being taken up" (9:51).
  - *Forward: at the end, in the unprinted 24:51, "he was carried up (ἀνεφέρετο) into heaven": the same verb, φέρω, with "up" for "away".*
- **"into the bosom (κόλπον) of Abraham"**:
  - *κόλπος: the only other place is 6:38: "a good measure, pressed down, shaken, running over, they will give into your bosom (εἰς τὸν κόλπον ὑμῶν)".*
  - The bosom is where the measure is given. Lazarus, who received nothing, is carried into the bosom.
  - Abraham: "daughter of Abraham" (13:16). *Forward:* "son of Abraham" (19:9).
- **"the rich man also died and was buried (ἐτάφη)"**:
  - *"Let the dead bury (θάψαι) their own dead" (9:60).*
  - The poor man is carried. The rich man is buried.

### 16:23–24

*καὶ ἐν τῷ ᾅδῃ ἐπάρας τοὺς ὀφθαλμοὺς αὐτοῦ, ὑπάρχων ἐν βασάνοις, ὁρᾷ Ἀβραὰμ ἀπὸ μακρόθεν καὶ Λάζαρον ἐν τῷ κόλπῳ αὐτοῦ. καὶ αὐτὸς φωνήσας εἶπεν· Πάτερ Ἀβραάμ, ἐλέησόν με καὶ πέμψον Λάζαρον ἵνα βάψῃ τὸ ἄκρον τοῦ δακτύλου αὐτοῦ ὕδατος καὶ καταψύξῃ τὴν γλῶσσάν μου, ὅτι ὀδυνῶμαι ἐν τῇ φλογὶ ταύτῃ.*

- **"in Hades (ᾅδῃ)"**: Capernaum "down to Hades" (10:15, test).
- **"lifting up his eyes (ἐπάρας τοὺς ὀφθαλμούς)"**:
  - He lifted up his eyes on his disciples (6:20).
  - *Forward: the tax collector, "standing far off (μακρόθεν), would not even lift up (ἐπᾶραι) his eyes to heaven" (18:13).*
  - The rich man lifts up his eyes from Hades. The tax collector will not lift his to heaven, and goes down justified (18:14).
- **"being in torments (βασάνοις)"**: *Legion: "I beg you, do not torment (βασανίσῃς) me" (8:28).*
- **"he sees Abraham from far off (μακρόθεν)"**:
  - Far off: the tax collector (18:13). "While he was still far off (μακράν)" (15:20, test).
  - The singular "in his bosom" is the adopted Marcionite reading. **Neutral.**
- **"Father Abraham, have mercy (ἐλέησον) on me"**:
  - *Mercy: the blind man, "Jesus, son of David, have mercy (ἐλέησον) on me" (18:38–39).*
  - "Give as alms (ἐλεημοσύνην) the things within" (11:41): what the rich man did not do.
- **"send Lazarus to dip the tip of his finger (δακτύλου) in water"**:
  - *The finger:* the finger of God casts out (11:20); the lawyers do not touch the burdens "with one of your fingers" (11:46).
  - Here the least part of the poor man is asked for.
- **"and cool my tongue, for I am in anguish in this flame"**: fire.

### 16:25–26

*εἶπεν δὲ Ἀβραάμ· Τέκνον, μνήσθητι ὅτι ἀπέλαβες τὰ ἀγαθά σου ἐν τῇ ζωῇ σου, καὶ Λάζαρος ὁμοίως τὰ κακά· νῦν δὲ ὧδε παρακαλεῖται σὺ δὲ ὀδυνᾶσαι. καὶ ἐν πᾶσι τούτοις μεταξὺ ἡμῶν καὶ ὑμῶν χάσμα μέγα ἐστήρικται, ὅπως οἱ θέλοντες διαβῆναι ἔνθεν πρὸς ὑμᾶς μὴ δύνωνται, μηδὲ ἐκεῖθεν πρὸς ἡμᾶς διαπερῶσιν.*

- **"Child (Τέκνον)"**: *the father to the elder son, "Child (Τέκνον), you are always with me" (15:31, test).*
- **"remember (μνήσθητι)"**: *forward: "remember (μνήσθητε) how he told you… and they remembered" (24:6, 24:8).*
- **"you received (ἀπέλαβες) your good things (ἀγαθά) in your life (ζωῇ)"**:
  - Receiving back: "if you lend to those from whom you hope to receive (ἀπολαβεῖν)" (6:34).
  - *"My goods (ἀγαθά)" (12:18–19).* "Life is not from one's possessions" (12:15).
- **"now he is comforted (παρακαλεῖται)"**: *"woe to you who are rich, for you have received your comfort (παράκλησιν)" (6:24).* The woe of 6:24, completed.
- **"a great chasm has been fixed (ἐστήρικται)"**:
  - *στηρίζω: "he set firm (ἐστήρισεν) his face to go to Jerusalem" (9:51).* The face set firm toward the goal; the chasm set firm between the two states.
  - *Forward:* "strengthen (στήρισον) your brothers" (22:32).
- **"so that those who wish to cross (διαβῆναι)… cannot"**: crossing (8:22, "let us go across"). In this life the crossing is made (8:22–26). After it, it cannot be made.

### 16:27–31

*Ἐρωτῶ σε οὖν, πάτερ, ἵνα πέμψῃς αὐτὸν εἰς τὸν οἶκον τοῦ πατρός μου, ἔχω γὰρ πέντε ἀδελφούς, ὅπως διαμαρτύρηται αὐτοῖς… λέγει δὲ Ἀβραάμ· Ἔχουσι Μωϋσέα καὶ τοὺς προφήτας· ἀκουσάτωσαν αὐτῶν. ὁ δὲ εἶπεν· Οὐχί, πάτερ Ἀβραάμ, ἀλλ’ ἐάν τις ἀπὸ νεκρῶν πορευθῇ πρὸς αὐτοὺς μετανοήσουσιν. εἶπεν δὲ αὐτῷ· Εἰ Μωϋσέως καὶ τῶν προφητῶν οὐκ ἀκούουσιν, οὐδ’ ἐάν τις ἐκ νεκρῶν ἀναστῇ πεισθήσονται.*

- **"to my father's house… five brothers"**: the natural house. "My father's house" (15:17, test).
- **"that he may testify to them"**: testimony (5:14; 9:5).
- **"They have Moses and the prophets; let them hear (ἀκουσάτωσαν) them"**:
  - Moses and Elijah on the mountain (9:30). Hearing: "hear him" (9:35).
  - *Forward: in the unprinted 24:27, "beginning from Moses and all the prophets, he interpreted to them in all the scriptures the things about himself", and in the unprinted 24:44–46, "everything written about me in the law of Moses and the prophets… that the Anointed One should suffer and rise from the dead (ἀναστῆναι ἐκ νεκρῶν) on the third day".*
- **"if someone goes (πορευθῇ) to them from the dead, they will repent"**:
  - The road verb, said of someone going from the dead.
  - Repenting (15:7, 15:10).
- **"if they do not hear Moses and the prophets, neither will they be persuaded if someone rises (ἀναστῇ) from the dead (ἐκ νεκρῶν)"**:
  - *Rising from the dead, said of someone, as a hypothesis.* "Raised from the dead" was Herod's rumour of John (9:7).
  - The condition: rising from the dead persuades no one who does not hear Moses and the prophets.
  - *The printed text sets the condition here. Whether it shows the condition being met at the end — the opening of Moses and the prophets about the rising (24:27, 24:44–46) — is a question for chapter 22. Those verses are not printed.*

### What came out of the reading of chapter 14

**The steward lets go what is owed.**
- His stewardship is taken away.
- He asks within himself "What shall I do?", the rich man's question of 12:17.
- He cannot dig, and is ashamed to beg.
- He calls the debtors — the word of the two debtors forgiven in 7:41 — and lets go part of what they owe the lord: oil and wheat.
- He is praised, and the teaching follows: make friends from what fails, so that you are received into the eternal tents.
- *The tents that Peter wanted on the mountain (9:33), that the Son of Man had none of on the way (9:58), are received at the end of letting go.*

**The height of men is abomination before God (16:15).** Here the printed text says outright what 12:25, 14:11 (test) and 18:14 say in their ways: the height one gives oneself is not the height before God.

**What is another's and what is his.** One holds what is another's. What is given at the end is "mine" (adopted) or "your own" (NA27). In the test passage 15:31 the two meet: "all that is mine is yours".

**Loosing in two directions.** The wife released to marry another (16:18) against the woman loosed from Satan's bond (13:16). One loosing frees, the other adulterates.

**The rich man and Lazarus.**
- The rich man is clothed, and makes merry daily.
- The poor man is thrown at the gate, longing to be filled from what falls — longing as the younger son longed (15:16, test).
- The poor man dies and is carried by the angels into the bosom. *The bosom is the word of the measure given "into your bosom" (6:38).*
- The rich man dies and is buried.
- In Hades he lifts up his eyes, sees from far off, and asks for the tip of a finger in water.
- "You received your good things in your life; now he is comforted": the woe of 6:24 completed.
- A chasm is set firm (the verb of the face set toward Jerusalem, 9:51); no one crosses.
- Moses and the prophets are to be heard. One rising from the dead will not persuade those who do not hear them.

**Movement.**
- *Lazarus is carried away by angels. This is the second time in the printed text that one who is lowest is carried, after the lost sheep on the shoulders (15:5). Its verb (ἀποφέρω) is the verb of the unprinted 24:51 (ἀναφέρω).*
- The rich man lifts up his eyes from below, and sees the height from far off.
- "Rising from the dead" is spoken of, as a condition, for the first time not as rumour.

### Threads, carried forward and added

- the steward (12:42 / 16:1–8);
- scattering (11:23; 15:13 → 16:1);
- "What shall I do?" (12:17 / 16:3);
- taking away;
- digging; begging (→ 18:35); shame;
- the debtors let go (7:41 / 16:5);
- oil and wheat;
- receiving into houses and into the eternal tents (9:33; 9:58; 13:19 / 16:9);
- failing (→ 22:32);
- faithful in the least (→ 19:17);
- another's and mine (10:22; 15:31);
- two lords;
- hate and love (→ 19:14; 21:17);
- sneering (→ 23:35, unprinted);
- justifying oneself (→ 18:9–14);
- the height of men;
- the law and prophets until John;
- forcing into the kingdom (→ 24:29);
- heaven and earth pass (→ 21:33);
- loosing in two directions;
- marrying (→ 17:27; 20:34–36);
- the rich man clothed and merry daily (12:19 / 16:19 → 23:11);
- the gate;
- longing to be filled;
- the table (→ 22:21);
- carried by angels (→ 24:51, unprinted);
- the bosom (6:38 / 16:22–23);
- buried;
- Hades;
- lifting the eyes (→ 18:13);
- far off (→ 18:13);
- torment (8:28);
- mercy (→ 18:38–39);
- the finger;
- "Child";
- remember (→ 24:6–8);
- receiving one's good things;
- comfort (6:24);
- the chasm set firm (9:51);
- crossing;
- Moses and the prophets, heard (→ 24:27, 24:44, unprinted);
- rising from the dead as a condition (→ 24:46, unprinted).
