## Chapter 15

*17:1–37, with 4:27 printed between 17:12 and 17:14, where both witnesses place it. Grey: 17:23–24, 17:27, 17:29–31, 17:33–35, 17:37. Not printed: 17:5–10 (U?; 17:10b A−); 17:12b–13 (A−). Adopted Marcionite wordings: 4:27 at this place, with "in the days of"; 17:21 "Look… or, Look…".*

### 17:1–2

*Ἀνένδεκτόν ἐστιν τοῦ τὰ σκάνδαλα μὴ ἐλθεῖν, πλὴν οὐαὶ δι’ οὗ ἔρχεται· λυσιτελεῖ αὐτῷ εἰ λίθος μυλικὸς περίκειται περὶ τὸν τράχηλον αὐτοῦ καὶ ἔρριπται εἰς τὴν θάλασσαν ἢ ἵνα σκανδαλίσῃ τῶν μικρῶν τούτων ἕνα.*

- **"stumbling blocks (σκάνδαλα) must come"**: "blessed is whoever is not made to stumble (σκανδαλισθῇ) in me" (7:23).
- **"a millstone around his neck, thrown into the sea"**:
  - Downward, into the deep: the herd ran down into the lake and was choked (8:33).
  - The millstone: *"two women grinding (ἀλήθουσαι) together" (17:35).*
  - The neck: *the father "fell on his neck" (15:20, test).*
- **"one of these little ones (μικρῶν)"**: "the least among you is great" (9:48); "little flock" (12:32). *Forward: Zacchaeus, "small" (19:3).*

### 17:3–4

*προσέχετε ἑαυτοῖς. ἐὰν ἁμάρτῃ ὁ ἀδελφός σου ἐπιτίμησον αὐτῷ, καὶ ἐὰν μετανοήσῃ ἄφες αὐτῷ· καὶ ἐὰν ἑπτάκις τῆς ἡμέρας ἁμαρτήσῃ εἰς σὲ καὶ ἑπτάκις ἐπιστρέψῃ πρὸς σὲ λέγων· Μετανοῶ, ἀφήσεις αὐτῷ.*

- **"take heed to yourselves (προσέχετε ἑαυτοῖς)"**: *12:1, the leaven. Forward: 21:34, "take heed to yourselves, lest your hearts be weighed down".*
- **"rebuke (ἐπιτίμησον) him"**: the rebuke is now given to the disciples. It was his against the daimons (4:35, 4:41), the wind (8:24), faith's naming (9:21), and the anger of charity (9:55).
- **"and if he repents (μετανοήσῃ), let go (ἄφες) to him"**: repenting (15:7, 15:10); letting go (11:4).
- **"seven times a day… and seven times turns back (ἐπιστρέψῃ) to you"**:
  - Seven: the whole (8:2).
  - Turning back (ἐπιστρέφω): the girl's spirit "returned" (8:55). *Forward:* "let him not turn back to the things behind" (17:31); "when you have turned back (ἐπιστρέψας), strengthen your brothers" (22:32).
  - The turning back is met each time with letting go.

### Test: 17:5–10 (U?; 17:10b A−)

*Καὶ εἶπαν οἱ ἀπόστολοι τῷ κυρίῳ· Πρόσθες ἡμῖν πίστιν. εἶπεν δὲ ὁ κύριος· Εἰ ἔχετε πίστιν ὡς κόκκον σινάπεως, ἐλέγετε ἂν τῇ συκαμίνῳ ταύτῃ· Ἐκριζώθητι καὶ φυτεύθητι ἐν τῇ θαλάσσῃ· καὶ ὑπήκουσεν ἂν ὑμῖν. Τίς δὲ ἐξ ὑμῶν δοῦλον ἔχων ἀροτριῶντα ἢ ποιμαίνοντα, ὃς εἰσελθόντι ἐκ τοῦ ἀγροῦ ἐρεῖ αὐτῷ· Εὐθέως παρελθὼν ἀνάπεσε, ἀλλ’ οὐχὶ ἐρεῖ αὐτῷ· Ἑτοίμασον τί δειπνήσω καὶ περιζωσάμενος διακόνει μοι ἕως φάγω καὶ πίω… οὕτως καὶ ὑμεῖς, ὅταν ποιήσητε πάντα τὰ διαταχθέντα ὑμῖν, λέγετε ὅτι Δοῦλοι ἀχρεῖοί ἐσμεν, ὃ ὠφείλομεν ποιῆσαι πεποιήκαμεν.*

- **"add (πρόσθες) faith to us"**:
  - *Adding:* no one can "add" a cubit to his stature (12:25); "these things will be added (προστεθήσεται) to you" (12:31). *Forward:* "adding (προσθείς), he told a parable" (19:11).
  - Faith is asked to be added.
- **"faith like a grain of mustard seed"**: *the mustard seed that grew into a tree for the birds (13:19).*
- **"you would say to this mulberry tree, Be uprooted and planted in the sea, and it would obey (ὑπήκουσεν) you"**:
  - "Hear under": the winds and water "obey" him (8:25).
  - The sea: the millstone (17:2).
- **"a servant ploughing (ἀροτριῶντα) or shepherding (ποιμαίνοντα)"**: *the plough (9:62); the shepherd (15:4–6).*
- **"Come at once and recline (ἀνάπεσε)"**: *the posture of 14:10 (test, "recline in the last place") and 11:37.*
- **"Prepare (ἑτοίμασον) what I shall dine (δειπνήσω), and girding yourself (περιζωσάμενος), serve (διακόνει) me"**:
  - *The reverse of 12:37 (printed), where the lord "will gird himself (περιζώσεται), make them recline, and… serve (διακονήσει) them".*
  - There the lord serves the watchful. Here the servant serves the lord.
  - Preparing (9:52; 12:20; 14:17). Dining (14:12–24).
- **"say: We are servants without need (ἀχρεῖοι); we have done what we owed (ὠφείλομεν) to do"** (17:10b, A−):
  - *ἀ-χρεῖος, "of no need":*
    - "those who are well have no need (χρείαν) of a physician" (5:31);
    - "ninety-nine righteous who have no need (χρείαν) of repentance" (15:7).
  - Owing (11:4; 16:5–7).
  - The servant claims nothing for what he did. *Against it, the elder son: "so many years I serve you, and never… and you never gave me" (15:29, test). Forward: the Pharisee who thanks God that he is not like other men (18:11–12).*

**Verdict: continues.**
- Its words are in the threads. It is the other side of 12:37: the lord serves those who watch, and the servant who serves claims no merit.
- *It joins the line at the point the text keeps returning to: receiving, against claiming as one's own.*
- 17:4 → 17:11 runs, so it does not close a gap.

### 17:11

*Καὶ ἐγένετο ἐν τῷ πορεύεσθαι εἰς Ἰερουσαλὴμ καὶ αὐτὸς διήρχετο διὰ μέσον Σαμαρείας καὶ Γαλιλαίας.*

- **"as he was going (πορεύεσθαι) to Jerusalem"**:
  - The road's infinitive (4:42; 9:51).
  - The first time since 9:53 that the printed text names Jerusalem as the goal.
- **"he himself was passing through (διήρχετο) through the midst (διὰ μέσον)"**:
  - *The words of 4:30: "but he himself, passing through (διελθών) through their midst (διὰ μέσου αὐτῶν), went on (ἐπορεύετο)".* The same four elements: he himself, passing through, through the midst, going.
  - At Nazareth he passed through the midst of those who would cast him down. Here he passes through the midst of Samaria and Galilee.
  - *The road is marked by the same sentence at its first step and here.*
- **"Samaria and Galilee"**:
  - Samaria: it did not receive him because his face was going to Jerusalem (9:52–53). The Samaritan (10:33, test).
  - Galilee: where the word came down (4:31).

### 17:12 and 4:27

*καὶ εἰσερχομένου αὐτοῦ εἴς τινα κώμην ἀπήντησαν αὐτῷ δέκα λεπροὶ ἄνδρες. πολλοὶ λεπροὶ ἦσαν ἐν τῷ Ἰσραὴλ ἐν ἡμέραις Ἐλισαίου τοῦ προφήτου, καὶ οὐδεὶς αὐτῶν ἐκαθαρίσθη εἰ μὴ Νεεμὰν ὁ Σύρος.*

- **"ten leprous men met him"**:
  - Ten (15:8). *Forward:* "ten servants… ten minas… ten cities" (19:13–17).
  - Leprosy is truth profaned, the uncleanness that spreads over the whole (?). The leper "full of leprosy" fell on his face (5:12).
- **(4:27) "many lepers were in Israel in the days of Elisha the prophet, and none of them was cleansed (ἐκαθαρίσθη) except Naaman the Syrian"**:
  - Cleansing: the leper (5:12–14); "lepers are cleansed" (7:22); "you cleanse the outside of the cup" (11:39).
  - *Elisha:* the prophet who followed Elijah. Elijah is named on the mountain (9:30–33) and in the rumours (9:8, 9:19).
  - *Naaman the Syrian: one foreigner among many. Forward: "this foreigner" (17:18).*
  - Read in this place, the verse states ahead of time what 17:15–18 will show: of many unclean, the one cleansed and turned is the foreigner.
  - **Neutral, with the thread.** The placement is the one both witnesses attest. *(At Nazareth, the place NA27 gives it, the printed text runs 4:24 → 4:28. That was read in chapter 1.)*

### Test: 17:12b–13 (A−)

*οἳ ἔστησαν πόρρωθεν, καὶ αὐτοὶ ἦραν φωνὴν λέγοντες· Ἰησοῦ ἐπιστάτα, ἐλέησον ἡμᾶς.*

- **"who stood far off (πόρρωθεν)"**:
  - "While he was still far off" (15:20, test); the rich man sees "from far off" (16:23).
  - *Forward: the tax collector "standing far off (μακρόθεν)" (18:13).*
- **"they lifted up (ἦραν) their voice"**: αἴρω, lifting up.
- **"Jesus, master (ἐπιστάτα)"**: 5:5; 8:24, 8:45; 9:33.
- **"have mercy (ἐλέησον) on us"**: the rich man, "Father Abraham, have mercy on me" (16:24). *Forward: the blind man, "son of David, have mercy on me" (18:38–39).*

**Verdict: continues.**
- Standing far off and crying for mercy is the state the text shows in the printed 16:23–24, 18:13 and 18:38–39.
- 17:12 → 17:14 runs: "seeing (ἰδών), he said to them". The healing comes unasked, as at Nain (7:13–14) and for the bent woman (13:12). So it does not close a gap.

### 17:14

*καὶ ἰδὼν εἶπεν αὐτοῖς· Πορευθέντες ἐπιδείξατε ἑαυτοὺς τοῖς ἱερεῦσιν. καὶ ἐγένετο ἐν τῷ ὑπάγειν αὐτοὺς ἐκαθαρίσθησαν.*

- **"seeing, he said: Go (πορευθέντες) and show yourselves to the priests"**: *the word to the first leper: "go, show yourself (δεῖξον σεαυτόν) to the priest" (5:14).*
- **"and as they went (ἐν τῷ ὑπάγειν αὐτούς), they were cleansed"**:
  - *Cleansed on the way.* The going is where the cleansing takes place.
  - The road heals as it is walked.

### 17:15–16

*εἷς δὲ ἐξ αὐτῶν, ἰδὼν ὅτι ἰάθη, ὑπέστρεψεν μετὰ φωνῆς μεγάλης δοξάζων τὸν θεόν, καὶ ἔπεσεν ἐπὶ πρόσωπον παρὰ τοὺς πόδας αὐτοῦ εὐχαριστῶν αὐτῷ· καὶ αὐτὸς ἦν Σαμαρίτης.*

- **"one of them, seeing that he was healed, turned back (ὑπέστρεψεν)"**: *returning (8:37–40; 9:10; 10:17).* The others go on to the priests. One turns back to him.
- **"with a loud voice glorifying God"**: *the bent woman "glorified God" (13:13). Forward: the blind man (18:43).*
- **"and fell on his face (ἐπὶ πρόσωπον) at his feet"**:
  - *The first leper, "falling on his face (πεσὼν ἐπὶ πρόσωπον)" (5:12).* The leper of the first stretch and the leper of the road fall the same way.
  - The face (9:29, 9:51–53). At his feet (7:38; 8:35; 8:41).
- **"giving thanks (εὐχαριστῶν) to him"**:
  - Thanksgiving: his own (10:21, adopted).
  - *Forward: the Pharisee's "I thank (εὐχαριστῶ) you that I am not like other men" (18:11); "having given thanks (εὐχαριστήσας)" over the cup and the bread (22:17, 22:19).*
- **"and he was a Samaritan"**: *the village of the Samaritans did not receive him (9:52–53). The Samaritan of 10:33 (test) received the half-dead man.* Here the Samaritan turns back and gives thanks.

### 17:17–19

*Οὐχὶ οἱ δέκα ἐκαθαρίσθησαν; οἱ δὲ ἐννέα ποῦ; οὐχ εὑρέθησαν ὑποστρέψαντες δοῦναι δόξαν τῷ θεῷ εἰ μὴ ὁ ἀλλογενὴς οὗτος; καὶ εἶπεν αὐτῷ· Ἀναστὰς πορεύου· ἡ πίστις σου σέσωκέν σε.*

- **"where are the nine?"**: "the ninety-nine" (15:4, 15:7).
- **"were none found (εὑρέθησαν) returning to give glory to God, except this foreigner (ἀλλογενής)?"**:
  - Found: the lost sheep, the coin (15:5–9); "was lost and is found" (15:24, 15:32, test).
  - *"Other-born", a foreigner by origin:*
    - Nazareth claimed him by origin (4:23–24);
    - the master does not know those outside "where they are from" (13:25);
    - Naaman the Syrian (4:27).
  - The one who turns back is the one not of the origin.
- **"Rising, go (Ἀναστὰς πορεύου)"**:
  - *Rising and going, joined, said by him to the one who turned back.*
  - *The paralysed man: "rise, take up your bed, and go (πορεύου) to your house" (5:24). The younger son: "rising, I will go (ἀναστὰς πορεύσομαι) to my father" (15:18, test).*
  - In the printed text the one who turned back is told to rise and go.
  - The order: cleansed on the way; turning back; falling on the face at the feet; thanks; then "rise, go".
- **"your faith has saved you"**: *to the sinful woman (7:50) and to the woman with the flow (8:48). Forward: to the blind man (18:42).*

### 17:20–21

*Ἐπερωτηθεὶς δὲ ὑπὸ τῶν Φαρισαίων πότε ἔρχεται ἡ βασιλεία τοῦ θεοῦ ἀπεκρίθη αὐτοῖς καὶ εἶπεν· Οὐκ ἔρχεται ἡ βασιλεία τοῦ θεοῦ μετὰ παρατηρήσεως, οὐδὲ ἐροῦσιν· Ἰδοὺ ὧδε ἤ· Ἰδοὺ ἐκεῖ· ἰδοὺ γὰρ ἡ βασιλεία τοῦ θεοῦ ἐντὸς ὑμῶν ἐστιν.*

- **"when does the kingdom of God come?"**: coming (11:2: "let your kingdom come").
- **"the kingdom of God does not come with watching (παρατηρήσεως)"**:
  - *Watching: the scribes and Pharisees "watched (παρετηροῦντο) him" to accuse him (6:7). Forward: "watching (παρατηρήσαντες), they sent spies" (20:20).*
  - The kingdom does not come to the watching of those who watch in order to catch.
- **"nor will they say: Look, here! or, Look, there!"**: the double "look" is the adopted Marcionite reading. **Neutral.**
- **"for behold, the kingdom of God is within (ἐντός) you"**:
  - *Within.* The inside (ἔσωθεν): "did not the one who made the outside make the inside also?" (11:40); "give as alms the things within" (11:41). "The one inside" who rises to give (11:7–8). "Coming into himself" (15:17, test).
  - *Here the text says where the kingdom is: inside.*

### 17:22–24 (17:23–24 grey)

*Ἐλεύσονται ἡμέραι ὅτε ἐπιθυμήσετε μίαν τῶν ἡμερῶν τοῦ υἱοῦ τοῦ ἀνθρώπου ἰδεῖν καὶ οὐκ ὄψεσθε. καὶ ἐροῦσιν ὑμῖν· Ἰδοὺ ἐκεῖ, ἤ· Ἰδοὺ ὧδε· μὴ ἀπέλθητε μηδὲ διώξητε. ὥσπερ γὰρ ἡ ἀστραπὴ ἀστράπτουσα ἐκ τῆς ὑπὸ τὸν οὐρανὸν εἰς τὴν ὑπ’ οὐρανὸν λάμπει, οὕτως ἔσται ὁ υἱὸς τοῦ ἀνθρώπου ἐν τῇ ἡμέρᾳ αὐτοῦ.*

- **"you will long (ἐπιθυμήσετε) to see one of the days of the Son of Man"**: longing (16:21; 15:16, test). *Forward:* "with longing I have longed (ἐπιθυμίᾳ ἐπεθύμησα)" (22:15).
- **"do not go away, nor pursue"**: no road "here" or "there". The road is not to an outward place.
- **"as the lightning (ἀστραπή) flashing (ἀστράπτουσα)… shines (λάμπει)"**:
  - *Flashing:*
    - his garment "flashing" (ἐξαστράπτων) on the mountain (9:29);
    - Satan falling "like lightning" (10:18, test);
    - the lamp "with its flash" (11:36, test).
  - *Forward:* "two men in flashing (ἀστραπτούσῃ) clothing" (24:4).
  - Shining: "brightly" (λαμπρῶς) for the rich man's feasting (16:19).
- **"so will the Son of Man be in his day"**.

### 17:25

*πρῶτον δὲ δεῖ αὐτὸν πολλὰ παθεῖν καὶ ἀποδοκιμασθῆναι ἀπὸ τῆς γενεᾶς ταύτης.*

- **"but first (πρῶτον) he must suffer many things and be rejected"**:
  - *The words of 9:22: δεῖ… πολλὰ παθεῖν καὶ ἀποδοκιμασθῆναι.*
  - "First": *his "first", against the own's "first" (9:59–61; 14:18).* Before the day like lightning, suffering.
- **"by this generation"**: the generation that seeks a sign (11:29), that tests the face of the sky (12:56).

### 17:26–29 (17:27, 17:29 grey)

*καὶ καθὼς ἐγένετο ἐν ταῖς ἡμέραις Νῶε… ἤσθιον, ἔπινον, ἐγάμουν, ἐγαμίζοντο, ἄχρι ἧς ἡμέρας εἰσῆλθεν Νῶε εἰς τὴν κιβωτόν, καὶ ἦλθεν ὁ κατακλυσμὸς καὶ ἀπώλεσεν πάντας. ὁμοίως καθὼς ἐγένετο ἐν ταῖς ἡμέραις Λώτ· ἤσθιον, ἔπινον, ἠγόραζον, ἐπώλουν, ἐφύτευον, ᾠκοδόμουν· ᾗ δὲ ἡμέρᾳ ἐξῆλθεν Λὼτ ἀπὸ Σοδόμων, ἔβρεξεν πῦρ καὶ θεῖον ἀπ’ οὐρανοῦ καὶ ἀπώλεσεν πάντας.*

- **"they ate, they drank, they married… they bought, they sold, they planted, they built"**:
  - *The own's occupations:*
    - the rich man's "eat, drink, be merry" and "I will build" (12:18–19);
    - the excuses, "I bought a field… I married a wife" (14:18–20).
  - The days pass in these, without turning.
- **"until the day Noah entered (εἰσῆλθεν) the ark"**: entering (13:24; 14:23). The ark is a vessel that holds (?).
- **"the flood came down (κατακλυσμός) and destroyed all"**: *the flood (πλημμύρα) burst on the house built without a foundation (6:48–49).*
- **"the day Lot went out (ἐξῆλθεν) from Sodom"**: coming out. Sodom (10:12, test).
- **"it rained fire and sulphur from heaven"**:
  - *Fire from heaven: what James and John wanted to call down on the village (9:54), and were rebuked.*
  - Here it comes on the day of going out. It is the end of the own's days, not a punishment called down by charity.

### 17:30–32 (17:30–31 grey)

*κατὰ τὰ αὐτὰ ἔσται ᾗ ἡμέρᾳ ὁ υἱὸς τοῦ ἀνθρώπου ἀποκαλύπτεται. ἐν ἐκείνῃ τῇ ἡμέρᾳ ὃς ἔσται ἐπὶ τοῦ δώματος καὶ τὰ σκεύη αὐτοῦ ἐν τῇ οἰκίᾳ, μὴ καταβάτω ἆραι αὐτά, καὶ ὁ ἐν ἀγρῷ ὁμοίως μὴ ἐπιστρεψάτω εἰς τὰ ὀπίσω. μνημονεύετε τῆς γυναικὸς Λώτ.*

- **"the day the Son of Man is revealed (ἀποκαλύπτεται)"**: *the law of veiling, at its end:* 8:17; 9:45; 10:21–22; 12:2. What was covered is uncovered, and what is uncovered is the Son of Man.
- **"whoever is on the roof (δώματος), with his vessels (σκεύη) in the house, let him not go down (μὴ καταβάτω) to take (ἆραι) them"**:
  - *The roof:* the friends "went up onto the roof" (ἀναβάντες ἐπὶ τὸ δῶμα) and let the paralysed man down (5:19); what was whispered is proclaimed "on the roofs" (12:3).
  - *Not going down to take:* the possessions (the vessels) are below. The one who is up does not go down to take them.
  - "Take nothing for the way" (9:3); "take up (ἆρον) your bed and go" (5:24).
  - *Here staying up is not going down to take what one has.*
- **"whoever is in the field, let him not turn back (ἐπιστρεψάτω) to the things behind (εἰς τὰ ὀπίσω)"**:
  - *"No one who puts his hand to the plough and looks at the things behind (τὰ ὀπίσω)" (9:62).*
  - The field: the excuse of the field (14:18).
  - Turning back is good toward him (17:4, 17:15). It is not good toward what is behind.
- **"remember Lot's wife"**: who looked back. Remembering (16:25). *Forward:* 24:6–8.

### 17:33 (grey)

*ὃς ἐὰν ζητήσῃ τὴν ψυχὴν αὐτοῦ περιποιήσασθαι ἀπολέσει αὐτήν, ὃς δ’ ἂν ἀπολέσῃ ζῳογονήσει αὐτήν.*

- **"whoever seeks to keep his soul will lose it"**: *9:24 again.*
- **"whoever loses it will make it live (ζῳογονήσει)"**:
  - *ζῳο-γονέω, "beget alive".* The life thread:
    - "do this and you will live" (10:28);
    - "he was dead and lives again" (15:24, test);
    - "the Living One" (24:5).
  - At 9:24 the soul lost is "saved". Here it is made alive.

### 17:34–35 (grey)

*ταύτῃ τῇ νυκτὶ ἔσονται δύο ἐπὶ κλίνης μιᾶς, ὁ εἷς παραλημφθήσεται καὶ ὁ ἕτερος ἀφεθήσεται· ἔσονται δύο ἀλήθουσαι ἐπὶ τὸ αὐτό, ἡ μία παραλημφθήσεται ἡ δὲ ἑτέρα ἀφεθήσεται.*

- **"this night (νυκτί)"**: *"this night your soul is demanded back" (12:20).*
- **"two on one bed (κλίνης)"**: the bed (5:18; 8:16). The κλίνω family.
- **"one will be taken along (παραλημφθήσεται)"**:
  - *παραλαμβάνω, "take along": the only other places are 9:10 (taking the apostles, he withdrew) and 9:28, "taking along (παραλαβών) Peter and John and James, he went up (ἀνέβη) into the mountain".*
  - The word of being taken along with him in the going up.
  - The λαμβάνω family: being taken up (ἀνάλημψις, 9:51); the spirit that "takes" the boy (9:39); "everyone who asks receives (λαμβάνει)" (11:10).
- **"and the other will be left (ἀφεθήσεται)"**: ἀφίημι, let go, left. "Let the dead bury their dead" (9:60); "your house is left to you" (13:35, test).
- **"two grinding together"**: the millstone (17:2).

### 17:37 (grey)

*καὶ ἀποκριθέντες λέγουσιν αὐτῷ· Ποῦ, κύριε; ὁ δὲ εἶπεν αὐτοῖς· Ὅπου τὸ σῶμα, ἐκεῖ καὶ οἱ ἀετοὶ ἐπισυναχθήσονται.*

- **"Where, Lord?"**: the question of place, after "not here or there" (17:21, 17:23).
- **"where the body (σῶμα) is, there the eagles will be gathered"**:
  - *The body:* "do not fear those who kill the body" (12:4). *Forward:* "this is my body" (22:19); his body laid (23:52, 23:55); not found (24:3, 24:23).
  - The eagles fly high. They are the understanding raised up (?).
  - Gathering (ἐπισυνάγω): the crowds gathered (12:1); "how often I wanted to gather your children" (13:34, test).
  - The answer to "where?" is not a place but the body.

### What came out of the reading of chapter 15

**The road is marked with the sentence of 4:30.** "As he was going to Jerusalem, he himself was passing through the midst" (17:11). These are the words of his passing "through the midst" of Nazareth and going on (4:30). The road began there, and the text marks it there again.

**Cleansed on the way; then turning back; then rising and going.**
- Ten lepers are sent to the priests, and are cleansed "as they went".
- One turns back, glorifying God, falls on his face at his feet, and gives thanks. He is the foreigner, the Samaritan.
- To him alone: "Rising, go; your faith has saved you."
- *Rising and going are joined in the printed text (17:19; 5:24) as they are in the test passage of the younger son (15:18).* In this printed passage the rising comes after the turning back and the falling down.
- (4:27, here: of many lepers, the foreigner alone cleansed. Neutral, with the thread.)

**The kingdom within (17:21).** It does not come to those who watch to catch him. It is not here or there. It is "within you". Inside is where the kingdom is.

**The day.**
- Like lightning, the flashing of his garment on the mountain (9:29).
- But "first he must suffer many things and be rejected": 9:22, with his own "first".
- The own's days (eating, drinking, marrying, buying, selling, planting, building) go on until the going out. Then fire from heaven, which charity was rebuked for wanting (9:54).

**Staying up and not turning back.**
- On the roof, do not go down to take your vessels.
- In the field, do not turn back to the things behind (9:62).
- Remember Lot's wife.
- Losing the soul makes it live (ζῳογονήσει).

**Taken along, or left.** "One will be taken along (παραλημφθήσεται), the other left." *The verb is the one of 9:28: "taking along Peter and John and James, he went up into the mountain."* Where the body is, the eagles gather.

**The servant's side (test passage 17:5–10).** The servant who has done all that was commanded says "we are servants of no need; we have done what we owed". It is the other side of 12:37, where the lord serves those who watch. And it is the claim that the elder son (15:29, test) and the Pharisee (18:11) make.

### Threads, carried forward and added

- stumbling;
- the millstone and the sea;
- the little ones;
- rebuke given to the disciples;
- turning back (ἐπιστρέφω, → 22:32), against turning back to what is behind;
- seven times;
- adding faith;
- the servant who claims nothing (17:10b, A−; → 18:11–12);
- "through the midst" (4:30 / 17:11);
- ten;
- lepers and cleansing (5:12–14 / 17:12–17);
- Naaman, the foreigner;
- cleansed on the way;
- turning back to give glory;
- falling on the face (5:12 / 17:16 → 24:5);
- thanksgiving (→ 18:11; 22:17–19);
- the Samaritan;
- "Rising, go" (5:24 / 17:19; 15:18, test);
- faith that saves (→ 18:42);
- the kingdom within;
- watching to catch (6:7 → 20:20);
- longing to see (→ 22:15);
- lightning (→ 24:4);
- "first he must suffer" (9:22 / 17:25);
- Noah and Lot;
- fire from heaven (9:54 / 17:29);
- the day of revealing;
- the roof (5:19 / 12:3 / 17:31);
- not going down to take;
- the things behind (9:62 / 17:31);
- Lot's wife;
- making the soul live;
- taken along (9:28 / 17:34–35);
- left;
- the body and the eagles (→ 22:19; 23:52–24:23).

## Chapter 16

*18:1–43. Grey: 18:4, 18:6, 18:8–9, 18:15, 18:17. Not printed: 18:24–30 (U?), 18:31–33 (A−), 18:34 (U−), between 18:23 and 18:35. Adopted Marcionite wordings: 18:19 "God the Father" (Epiphanius: Marcion "added 'the Father'"); 18:20 the order of the commandments; 18:22 no "still", "give" for "distribute", "in heaven" (singular).*

### 18:1–5 (18:4 grey)

*Ἔλεγεν δὲ παραβολὴν αὐτοῖς πρὸς τὸ δεῖν πάντοτε προσεύχεσθαι αὐτοὺς καὶ μὴ ἐγκακεῖν, λέγων· Κριτής τις ἦν ἔν τινι πόλει τὸν θεὸν μὴ φοβούμενος καὶ ἄνθρωπον μὴ ἐντρεπόμενος. χήρα δὲ ἦν ἐν τῇ πόλει ἐκείνῃ καὶ ἤρχετο πρὸς αὐτὸν λέγουσα· Ἐκδίκησόν με ἀπὸ τοῦ ἀντιδίκου μου. καὶ οὐκ ἤθελεν ἐπὶ χρόνον, μετὰ ταῦτα δὲ εἶπεν ἐν ἑαυτῷ· Εἰ καὶ τὸν θεὸν οὐ φοβοῦμαι οὐδὲ ἄνθρωπον ἐντρέπομαι, διά γε τὸ παρέχειν μοι κόπον τὴν χήραν ταύτην ἐκδικήσω αὐτήν ἵνα μὴ εἰς τέλος ἐρχομένη ὑπωπιάζῃ με.*

- **"the need (δεῖν) to pray always and not lose heart"**:
  - "Must" (δεῖ): of the passion (9:22; 17:25), of the loosing (13:16), and here of prayer.
  - Prayer (11:1–13).
- **"a judge (κριτής)… a widow… Vindicate me against my adversary (ἀντιδίκου)"**:
  - The judge (12:14, 12:58).
  - *The adversary on the way (12:58).*
  - The widow: the widow of Nain (7:12).
- **"he said within himself (ἐν ἑαυτῷ)"**: the rich man (12:17), the steward (16:3).
- **"because this widow gives me trouble (κόπον)"**:
  - *The friend at midnight: "do not give me trouble (κόπους)… because of his shamelessness, being raised, he will give" (11:7–8).*
  - The same mechanic: the one inside is moved by persistence.

### 18:6–8 (18:6, 18:8 grey)

*Ἀκούσατε τί ὁ κριτὴς τῆς ἀδικίας λέγει· ὁ δὲ θεὸς οὐ μὴ ποιήσῃ τὴν ἐκδίκησιν τῶν ἐκλεκτῶν αὐτοῦ τῶν βοώντων αὐτῷ ἡμέρας καὶ νυκτός, καὶ μακροθυμεῖ ἐπ’ αὐτοῖς; λέγω ὑμῖν ὅτι ποιήσει τὴν ἐκδίκησιν αὐτῶν ἐν τάχει. πλὴν ὁ υἱὸς τοῦ ἀνθρώπου ἐλθὼν ἆρα εὑρήσει τὴν πίστιν ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς;*

- **"the judge of unrighteousness"**: the steward of unrighteousness (16:8).
- **"his chosen (ἐκλεκτῶν), who cry (βοώντων) to him day and night"**:
  - The chosen: he chose (ἐκλεξάμενος) the twelve (6:13).
  - *Crying: the blind man "cried out (ἐβόησεν)" (18:38).*
- **"will the Son of Man, when he comes, find faith on the earth (ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς)?"**: faith (17:19). *The earth:* authority "on the earth" (5:24); fire "on the earth" (12:49).

### 18:9–10 (18:9 grey)

*Εἶπεν δὲ καὶ πρός τινας τοὺς πεποιθότας ἐφ’ ἑαυτοῖς ὅτι εἰσὶν δίκαιοι καὶ ἐξουθενοῦντας τοὺς λοιποὺς τὴν παραβολὴν ταύτην· Ἄνθρωποι δύο ἀνέβησαν εἰς τὸ ἱερὸν προσεύξασθαι, ὁ εἷς Φαρισαῖος καὶ ὁ ἕτερος τελώνης.*

- **"those who trusted (πεποιθότας) in themselves (ἐφ’ ἑαυτοῖς) that they were righteous"**:
  - *The strong man's armour, "in which he trusted (ἐπεποίθει)" (11:22).*
  - "You are those who justify yourselves" (16:15).
- **"and despised (ἐξουθενοῦντας) the rest"**: *forward: "Herod, despising (ἐξουθενήσας) him" (23:11).*
- **"two men went up (ἀνέβησαν) into the temple to pray (προσεύξασθαι)"**:
  - *The words of 9:28: "he went up (ἀνέβη) into the mountain to pray (προσεύξασθαι)".*
  - Going up to pray, now said of two men. The temple, the first time in the printed text.

### 18:11–12

*ὁ Φαρισαῖος σταθεὶς πρὸς ἑαυτὸν ταῦτα προσηύχετο· Ὁ θεός, εὐχαριστῶ σοι ὅτι οὐκ εἰμὶ ὥσπερ οἱ λοιποὶ τῶν ἀνθρώπων, ἅρπαγες, ἄδικοι, μοιχοί, ἢ καὶ ὡς οὗτος ὁ τελώνης· νηστεύω δὶς τοῦ σαββάτου, ἀποδεκατῶ πάντα ὅσα κτῶμαι.*

- **"standing, he prayed these things to himself (πρὸς ἑαυτόν)"**: the prayer is turned to himself.
- **"God, I thank (εὐχαριστῶ) you that I am not like the rest"**:
  - *Thanksgiving:* the Samaritan at his feet, "giving thanks" (17:16).
  - Here the thanks is for oneself.
- **"robbers (ἅρπαγες)"**: *"your inside is full of plunder (ἁρπαγῆς)" (11:39).* The Pharisee names in others what was named in the Pharisees.
- **"adulterers"**: 16:18.
- **"I fast twice a week; I tithe (ἀποδεκατῶ) all that I acquire"**:
  - Fasting: John's disciples and the Pharisees fast (5:33). "When the bridegroom is taken away, then they will fast" (5:35).
  - *Tithing: "you tithe (ἀποδεκατοῦτε) mint and rue, and pass by the calling and the love of God" (11:42).*
  - "All that I acquire": what is held as one's own.
  - *The claim of merit: the elder son (15:29, test). Against the servant who says "we have done what we owed" (17:10, test).*

### 18:13

*ὁ δὲ τελώνης μακρόθεν ἑστὼς οὐκ ἤθελεν οὐδὲ τοὺς ὀφθαλμοὺς ἐπᾶραι εἰς τὸν οὐρανόν, ἀλλ’ ἔτυπτεν τὸ στῆθος αὐτοῦ λέγων· Ὁ θεός, ἱλάσθητί μοι τῷ ἁμαρτωλῷ.*

- **"standing far off (μακρόθεν)"**:
  - *The rich man "sees Abraham from far off (μακρόθεν)" (16:23).* The lepers "stood far off" (17:12b, test). "While he was still far off" (15:20, test).
  - Distance, acknowledged.
- **"would not even lift up his eyes (ἐπᾶραι) to heaven"**:
  - *The rich man in Hades "lifted up (ἐπάρας) his eyes" (16:23).*
  - He looked up into heaven over the bread (9:16).
  - The tax collector does not lift his eyes.
- **"but beat (ἔτυπτεν) his breast"**: beating. The servant who "began to beat (τύπτειν)" the children (12:45). Here the beating is turned on one's own breast.
- **"God, be merciful to me, the sinner"**: mercy (16:24; 18:38). "The sinner": the sinner who repents (15:7, 15:10).

### 18:14

*λέγω ὑμῖν, κατέβη οὗτος δεδικαιωμένος εἰς τὸν οἶκον αὐτοῦ παρ’ ἐκεῖνον· ὅτι πᾶς ὁ ὑψῶν ἑαυτὸν ταπεινωθήσεται, ὁ δὲ ταπεινῶν ἑαυτὸν ὑψωθήσεται.*

- **"this man went down (κατέβη) to his house justified"**:
  - *Two went up (18:10). The one who did not lift his eyes goes down justified.*
  - Going down: he "came down" from the mountain (καταβάς, 6:17). "He came down" (κατῆλθεν, 4:31).
  - *Forward: "Zacchaeus, hurry and come down (κατάβηθι)… and he hurried and came down (κατέβη)… Today salvation has come to this house" (19:5–9).* A tax collector goes down to his house justified in the parable. A chief tax collector comes down, and salvation comes to his house, in the event.
- **"justified (δεδικαιωμένος)"**: *against "you who justify yourselves" (16:15).* He does not justify himself; he goes down justified.
- **"everyone who lifts himself up (ὑψῶν) will be brought low, and whoever brings himself low (ταπεινῶν) will be lifted up (ὑψωθήσεται)"**:
  - *The law, printed. It is the same as the unprinted 14:11. Capernaum (10:15, test); "what is high among men" (16:15).*
  - "Will be lifted up" is passive: one does not lift oneself up. One is lifted.
  - *In this parable the printed text puts the ascent in its own terms:*
    - two go up to pray;
    - the one who lifts himself remains with himself ("to himself");
    - the one who does not lift his eyes goes down justified;
    - and the law: brought low, lifted up.

### 18:15–17 (18:15, 18:17 grey)

*Προσέφερον δὲ αὐτῷ καὶ τὰ βρέφη ἵνα αὐτῶν ἅπτηται· ἰδόντες δὲ οἱ μαθηταὶ ἐπετίμων αὐτοῖς. ὁ δὲ Ἰησοῦς προσεκαλέσατο αὐτὰ λέγων· Ἄφετε τὰ παιδία ἔρχεσθαι πρός με καὶ μὴ κωλύετε αὐτά, τῶν γὰρ τοιούτων ἐστὶν ἡ βασιλεία τοῦ θεοῦ. ἀμὴν λέγω ὑμῖν, ὃς ἂν μὴ δέξηται τὴν βασιλείαν τοῦ θεοῦ ὡς παιδίον, οὐ μὴ εἰσέλθῃ εἰς αὐτήν.*

- **"they were bringing even the infants, that he might touch them"**: touching (5:13; 8:44–47).
- **"the disciples rebuked (ἐπετίμων) them"**: the rebuke misused, like "we forbade him" (9:49, test).
- **"let the children come to me, and do not hinder (κωλύετε) them"**: *"do not hinder (μὴ κωλύετε)" (9:50, test); "you hindered those entering" (11:52).*
- **"whoever does not receive (δέξηται) the kingdom as a child will not enter (εἰσέλθῃ) it"**:
  - *The child received in his name (9:47–48).*
  - Receiving, then entering. The kingdom is received, not taken.

### 18:18–19

*Καὶ ἐπηρώτησέν τις αὐτὸν ἄρχων λέγων· Διδάσκαλε ἀγαθέ, τί ποιήσας ζωὴν αἰώνιον κληρονομήσω; εἶπεν δὲ αὐτῷ ὁ Ἰησοῦς· Τί με λέγεις ἀγαθόν; οὐδεὶς ἀγαθὸς εἰ μὴ εἷς ὁ θεὸς ὁ πατήρ.*

- **"a ruler (ἄρχων)"**: the rulers (3:1; 8:41; 13:14).
- **"Good teacher, what must I do to inherit eternal life?"**:
  - *The lawyer's question (10:25).*
  - Good: "the good treasure" (6:45); "you, being evil, know how to give good gifts" (11:13).
- **"Why do you call me good? No one is good except one (εἷς), God the Father"**:
  - *The reading has to weigh "the Father" here. It is Marcion's attested addition; NA27 reads "except one, God" (εἷς ὁ θεός).*
  - *By the threads:*
    - **One (εἷς).** "No one is good except one" is followed by "one thing (ἕν) you lack" (18:22), and the one thing is "follow me". *"Of one thing (ἑνός) there is need" (10:42, test).* The One good, the one thing lacking, and following him are joined in four verses.
    - **The identity of the good.** "Why do you call me good?" puts back to the asker the question the text keeps open from 5:21 to 9:20: "who is this?" The printed text answers it only by revealing: "no one knows who the Father is except the son… and to whom the son wills to reveal" (10:22).
    - **"God the Father" as a title** occurs nowhere else in the text. Everywhere else "Father" is a relation:
      - the son's address in prayer (10:21; 11:2; 22:42; 23:46);
      - the giver to the children (11:13; 12:30, 12:32);
      - the source of glory and of all things handed over (9:26; 10:22).
    - **The next verse.** It gives the commandment "honour your father and mother". In Swedenborg's exposition of that commandment (*Arcana Coelestia* on Exod 20:12), the father is, inwardly, the Lord as to the Divine good.
  - *What the addition does.* "No one is good except one, God" leaves open where the one good is, and so leaves open who is speaking. The text keeps that open until it is revealed.
    - "God the Father" closes it in the letter. It names the One as "the Father", as a title, set against "me".
    - It states outright, as a separate name, what the next verse holds inwardly ("your father") and what 10:22 holds as a revealed relation.
  - **Strain.** The added words turn an inner relation into an outward title, and set it against the speaker, at the point where the text keeps the identity open. NA27's "one, God" continues the line: the one good, the one thing lacking, "follow me".
  - *This is the second wording the reading marks as a strain (after 10:21). Both are separations: of the Lord from the earth, and of the good from the one speaking. Recorded for task 14.*

### 18:20–21

*τὰς ἐντολὰς οἶδας· Μὴ φονεύσῃς, Μὴ μοιχεύσῃς, Μὴ κλέψῃς, Μηδὲ ψευδομαρτυρήσῃς, Τίμα τὸν πατέρα σου καὶ τὴν μητέρα. ὁ δὲ εἶπεν· Ταῦτα πάντα ἐφύλαξα ἐκ νεότητος.*

- **"you know the commandments"**: the order (kill, adultery) is the adopted Marcionite one. **Neutral.**
- **"honour your father and mother"**:
  - The natural father, left to bury the dead (9:59–60).
  - Father against son (12:53). "Whoever does not hate his father and mother" (14:26, test).
  - The commandment is kept, and its inner object is the One of 18:19.
- **"all these I have kept (ἐφύλαξα) from my youth"**:
  - *Keeping, guarding:* the strong man "guards" his court (11:21); "blessed are those who hear the word and keep (φυλάσσοντες) it" (11:28); "guard yourselves from all greed" (12:15).
  - He has kept the commandments, as the elder son "never passed by your command" (15:29, test).

### 18:22–23

*ἀκούσας δὲ ὁ Ἰησοῦς εἶπεν αὐτῷ· Ἕν σοι λείπει· πάντα ὅσα ἔχεις πώλησον καὶ δὸς πτωχοῖς, καὶ ἕξεις θησαυρὸν ἐν οὐρανῷ, καὶ δεῦρο ἀκολούθει μοι. ὁ δὲ ἀκούσας ταῦτα περίλυπος ἐγενήθη, ἦν γὰρ πλούσιος σφόδρα.*

- **"one thing (ἕν) you lack"**: the One of 18:19. The adopted wording (no "still", "give", singular "heaven") is **neutral**.
- **"sell all that you have and give to the poor"**:
  - *"Sell your possessions and give alms" (12:33, test).* "Give as alms the things within" (11:41).
  - The strong man's spoils given out (11:22).
  - Having (8:18: "whoever has… even what he seems to have").
- **"and you will have treasure in heaven"**: against "treasuring for himself" (12:21).
- **"and come, follow me (ἀκολούθει μοι)"**: *the word to Levi (5:27), who "leaving everything, rising, followed" (5:28). "Let him… follow me" (9:23).*
- **"he became very sorrowful, for he was very rich"**: the rich (6:24; 12:16; 16:19). Sorrow (λύπη). *Forward:* "he found them sleeping from sorrow (λύπης)" (22:45).

### Test: 18:24–30 (U?)

*Πῶς δυσκόλως οἱ τὰ χρήματα ἔχοντες εἰς τὴν βασιλείαν τοῦ θεοῦ εἰσπορεύονται· εὐκοπώτερον γάρ ἐστιν κάμηλον διὰ τρήματος βελόνης εἰσελθεῖν ἢ πλούσιον εἰς τὴν βασιλείαν τοῦ θεοῦ εἰσελθεῖν. Εἶπαν δὲ οἱ ἀκούσαντες· Καὶ τίς δύναται σωθῆναι; ὁ δὲ εἶπεν· Τὰ ἀδύνατα παρὰ ἀνθρώποις δυνατὰ παρὰ τῷ θεῷ ἐστιν. Εἶπεν δὲ ὁ Πέτρος· Ἰδοὺ ἡμεῖς ἀφέντες τὰ ἴδια ἠκολουθήσαμέν σοι. ὁ δὲ εἶπεν αὐτοῖς· Ἀμὴν λέγω ὑμῖν ὅτι οὐδείς ἐστιν ὃς ἀφῆκεν οἰκίαν ἢ γυναῖκα ἢ ἀδελφοὺς ἢ γονεῖς ἢ τέκνα ἕνεκεν τῆς βασιλείας τοῦ θεοῦ, ὃς οὐχὶ μὴ ἀπολάβῃ πολλαπλασίονα ἐν τῷ καιρῷ τούτῳ καὶ ἐν τῷ αἰῶνι τῷ ἐρχομένῳ ζωὴν αἰώνιον.*

- **"how hard for those who have riches to enter (εἰσπορεύονται)"**: entering (11:33, 11:52; 13:24).
- **"a camel through the eye of a needle"**: *the narrow (στενῆς) door, which many will not have the strength to enter (13:24).*
- **"who can be saved?… What is impossible with men is possible (δυνατά) with God"**:
  - *Possibility:* "I cannot rise and give" (11:7); "I cannot come" (14:20); "you cannot serve God and mammon" (16:13); "if you cannot do even the least" (12:26); "no one can add a cubit" (12:25).
  - What a person cannot do, God can.
- **"we have left our own (ἀφέντες τὰ ἴδια) and followed you"**:
  - *τὰ ἴδια, "one's own":* "the beam in your own (ἰδίῳ) eye" (6:41).
  - Leaving: "leaving (ἀφέντες) everything, they followed him" (5:11); Levi (5:28).
- **"no one who has left house or wife or brothers or parents or children"**: the house left (9:61); the wife (14:20); the relations divided (12:52–53); "hate father and mother" (14:26, test).
- **"who will not receive back (ἀπολάβῃ) many times more… and in the age to come eternal life"**:
  - *Receiving back: the rich man "received back (ἀπέλαβες) your good things in your life" (16:25).*
  - *Eternal life: the ruler's question (18:18), answered in its own words.*

**Verdict: continues.**
- It answers the ruler's question with the ruler's words ("eternal life").
- It names the own (τὰ ἴδια) as what is left.
- It says the passage through the narrow is possible with God and not with men.
- 18:23 → 18:35 runs, so it does not close a gap.

### Test: 18:31–34 (18:31–33 A−; 18:34 U−)

*Παραλαβὼν δὲ τοὺς δώδεκα εἶπεν πρὸς αὐτούς· Ἰδοὺ ἀναβαίνομεν εἰς Ἰερουσαλήμ, καὶ τελεσθήσεται πάντα τὰ γεγραμμένα διὰ τῶν προφητῶν τῷ υἱῷ τοῦ ἀνθρώπου· παραδοθήσεται γὰρ τοῖς ἔθνεσιν καὶ ἐμπαιχθήσεται καὶ ὑβρισθήσεται καὶ ἐμπτυσθήσεται, καὶ μαστιγώσαντες ἀποκτενοῦσιν αὐτόν, καὶ τῇ ἡμέρᾳ τῇ τρίτῃ ἀναστήσεται. καὶ αὐτοὶ οὐδὲν τούτων συνῆκαν, καὶ ἦν τὸ ῥῆμα τοῦτο κεκρυμμένον ἀπ’ αὐτῶν, καὶ οὐκ ἐγίνωσκον τὰ λεγόμενα.*

- **"taking along (παραλαβών) the twelve, he said: Behold, we go up (ἀναβαίνομεν) to Jerusalem"**:
  - *The structure of 9:28: "taking along (παραλαβών) Peter and John and James, he went up (ἀνέβη) into the mountain".* "Taken along" (17:34–35).
  - *"We go up to Jerusalem."* Of the passages that put the verb "go up" (ἀναβαίνω) together with Jerusalem — this one and 19:28 — neither is printed. *The printed text never says "go up to Jerusalem".* It says "go to Jerusalem" (9:51, 9:53; 17:11) and names the movement "taking up" (9:51).
- **"all that is written by the prophets about the Son of Man will be accomplished (τελεσθήσεται)"**:
  - Accomplished: "until it is accomplished (τελεσθῇ)" (12:50); "on the third day I am perfected" (13:32, test).
  - The prophets: "they have Moses and the prophets" (16:29–31).
- **"he will be handed over (παραδοθήσεται) to the nations… mocked, insulted, spat on… they will kill him, and on the third day he will rise (ἀναστήσεται)"**:
  - Handing over (9:44). The nations (12:30). *Forward: mocking (22:63; 23:11).*
  - Rising on the third day: *the printed 24:7, "on the third day rise (ἀναστῆναι)".*
- **"this saying was hidden (κεκρυμμένον) from them"**: *the veil of 9:45.*

**Verdict: continues.**
- Its words take up 9:22, 9:28, 9:44–45, 12:50, 16:29–31 and 17:34, and they reach 24:7.
- The printed text has the necessity of the passion three times (9:22, 9:44, 17:25) and recalls it at 24:6–7 as said "while still in Galilee". That recall is answered by 9:22 and 9:44, both said in Galilee. So the passage does not close a gap: 18:23 → 18:35 runs.
- *It is one of the two places that would give the road its verb "go up". Recorded for task 15.*

### 18:35–37

*Ἐγένετο δὲ ἐν τῷ ἐγγίζειν αὐτὸν εἰς Ἰεριχὼ τυφλός τις ἐκάθητο παρὰ τὴν ὁδὸν ἐπαιτῶν. ἀκούσας δὲ ὄχλου διαπορευομένου ἐπυνθάνετο τί εἴη τοῦτο· ἀπήγγειλαν δὲ αὐτῷ ὅτι Ἰησοῦς ὁ Ναζωραῖος παρέρχεται.*

- **"as he drew near (ἐγγίζειν) to Jericho"**:
  - Drawing near (10:9; 15:1). *Forward:* 19:11; 22:47; 24:15, 24:28.
  - *Jericho: the man "going down from Jerusalem to Jericho" (10:30, test).* The road comes to the place that, in the Samaritan parable, is the end of the going down.
- **"a blind man sat beside the way (παρὰ τὴν ὁδόν)"**:
  - *The seed that fell "beside the way (παρὰ τὴν ὁδόν)" was trodden down, and the birds ate it (8:5). The same words.*
  - The blind man is where the seed was lost.
- **"begging (ἐπαιτῶν)"**: *the steward, "to beg (ἐπαιτεῖν) I am ashamed" (16:3).* The blind man is not ashamed.
- **"a crowd going through (διαπορευομένου)"**: the road, passing through.
- **"Jesus the Nazorean is passing by (παρέρχεται)"**:
  - *Nazareth:* the origin (4:16; 4:34).
  - *Passing by:* the priest and the Levite "passed by" (10:31–32, test); "you pass by the calling" (11:42); the lord "coming by" (παρελθών) will serve those who watch (12:37).
  - He is passing by; the blind man does not let him pass.

### 18:38–39

*καὶ ἐβόησεν λέγων· Ἰησοῦ υἱὲ Δαυίδ, ἐλέησόν με. καὶ οἱ προάγοντες ἐπετίμων αὐτῷ ἵνα σιγήσῃ· αὐτὸς δὲ πολλῷ μᾶλλον ἔκραζεν· Υἱὲ Δαυίδ, ἐλέησόν με.*

- **"he cried out (ἐβόησεν)"**: *"his chosen who cry (βοώντων) to him day and night" (18:7).* The father of the only son "cried out" (9:38).
- **"Jesus, son of David"**:
  - David (6:3). *Forward: "how do they say that the Anointed One is son of David?… David calls him Lord" (20:41–44).*
  - The blind man names him by the line of the kingdom.
- **"have mercy on me (ἐλέησόν με)"**: *the rich man in Hades (16:24); the tax collector, "be merciful to me" (18:13); the lepers (17:13, test).*
- **"those going ahead rebuked (ἐπετίμων) him, to make him silent (σιγήσῃ)"**:
  - The rebuke misused (18:15).
  - Silence: what he ordered after the naming (9:21, 9:36). *Here those in front impose silence on someone asking for mercy.*
- **"but he cried out (ἔκραζεν) all the more"**: κράζω, the cry of falsity in 4:33, 8:28 and 9:39, here the cry for mercy. The same cry, from the other side.

### 18:40–41

*σταθεὶς δὲ ὁ Ἰησοῦς ἐκέλευσεν αὐτὸν ἀχθῆναι πρὸς αὐτόν. ἐγγίσαντος δὲ αὐτοῦ ἐπηρώτησεν αὐτόν· Τί σοι θέλεις ποιήσω; ὁ δὲ εἶπεν· Κύριε, ἵνα ἀναβλέψω.*

- **"Jesus stood (σταθείς)"**: *the Pharisee "standing (σταθείς), prayed to himself" (18:11).* The Pharisee stands to himself; Jesus stands for the blind man. The one passing by stops.
- **"he ordered him to be led (ἀχθῆναι) to him"**: brought in (14:21).
- **"when he drew near (ἐγγίσαντος)"**: the blind man draws near, as the sinners drew near (15:1).
- **"What do you want me to do for you?"**: what the asker wants. "What must I do?" was the ruler's question (18:18). Here he asks what he should do.
- **"Lord, that I may look up (ἀναβλέψω)"**:
  - *ἀνα-βλέπω, "look up, see again":*
    - "the blind look up (ἀναβλέπουσιν)" (7:22);
    - "looking up (ἀναβλέψας) into heaven, he blessed" (9:16).
  - *Forward: "looking up (ἀναβλέψας), Jesus saw" Zacchaeus in the tree (19:5).*

### 18:42–43

*καὶ ὁ Ἰησοῦς εἶπεν αὐτῷ· Ἀνάβλεψον· ἡ πίστις σου σέσωκέν σε. καὶ παραχρῆμα ἀνέβλεψεν, καὶ ἠκολούθει αὐτῷ δοξάζων τὸν θεόν. καὶ πᾶς ὁ λαὸς ἰδὼν ἔδωκεν αἶνον τῷ θεῷ.*

- **"Look up (Ἀνάβλεψον); your faith has saved you"**: the word to the women (7:50; 8:48) and to the Samaritan (17:19).
- **"at once he looked up (ἀνέβλεψεν)"**: the looking up is given.
- **"and followed (ἠκολούθει) him"**:
  - *The ruler was told "come, follow me" (18:22), and went away sorrowful.*
  - The blind beggar beside the way follows.
  - The one beside the way joins the way.
- **"glorifying God"**: the bent woman (13:13), the Samaritan (17:15).
- **"all the people gave praise (αἶνον)"**: *forward: the disciples "began to praise (αἰνεῖν) God" in the unprinted 19:37.*

### What came out of the reading of chapter 16

**Going up, going down, being lifted up (18:9–14).**
- Two men go up to the temple to pray, in the words of his going up the mountain to pray (9:28).
- One stands and prays to himself. He thanks God that he is not like others, and lists his fasting and tithing: the claim of merit.
- The other stands far off, does not lift his eyes to heaven, beats his own breast, and asks for mercy as the sinner.
- He goes down to his house justified.
- "Whoever lifts himself up will be brought low; whoever brings himself low will be lifted up."
- *This is the printed text's own statement of how one ascends:*
  - not by going up, which both did;
  - by being brought low;
  - and the lifting up is done to one ("will be lifted up").
- *Going down to his house justified is what Zacchaeus will do in the event (19:5–9).*

**Receiving the kingdom as a child (18:15–17).** Do not hinder the children. Whoever does not receive the kingdom as a child will not enter it.

**The One good, and what it lacks (18:18–23).**
- "No one is good except one".
- "One thing you lack: sell all, give, follow me".
- *The addition "the Father" (18:19) is the second wording the reading marks as a strain.* It names the One as a title set against "me". The text keeps the identity open there, and the next verse holds it inwardly ("honour your father").

**The two who are asked to follow.**
- The rich ruler has kept everything from his youth, lacks one thing, and goes away sorrowful.
- The blind man sits beside the way, where the seed was lost (8:5), begging, unashamed (16:3).
- He hears that Jesus is passing by, and cries for mercy. When rebuked he cries all the more.
- Jesus stops. The man asks to look up. He looks up, follows, and glorifies God.
- *The verb of his healing is "look up" (ἀναβλέπω), the verb of 9:16 and 19:5.*

**The road's verb "go up".** The printed text never says "go up to Jerusalem". The two passages that say it — 18:31 ("we go up to Jerusalem", built like 9:28) and 19:28 — are not printed. The printed text's own word for the movement is "taking up" (9:51). Its own going up is to pray: to the mountain (9:28) and to the temple (18:10).

### Threads, carried forward and added

- praying always;
- the judge and the widow;
- persistence moving the one inside (11:7–8 / 18:5);
- crying day and night (→ 18:38);
- faith on the earth;
- trusting in oneself (11:22 / 18:9);
- despising (→ 23:11);
- going up to pray (9:28 / 18:10);
- the temple (→ 20:1; 21:37–38);
- praying to oneself;
- thanksgiving for oneself (17:16 / 18:11);
- plunder (11:39 / 18:11);
- fasting (5:33–35);
- tithing (11:42 / 18:12);
- far off;
- not lifting the eyes;
- beating the breast;
- going down justified (→ 19:5–9);
- lifting up and bringing low (18:14 = 14:11, unprinted);
- the children, receiving as a child;
- the One good;
- "God the Father" (strain);
- honouring father and mother;
- keeping the commandments;
- one thing lacking;
- selling and giving (12:33, test);
- following (5:27–28 / 18:22 / 18:43);
- sorrow (→ 22:45);
- (test) the own (τὰ ἴδια), possible with God, receiving back, eternal life;
- (test) "we go up to Jerusalem", the third day, hidden;
- Jericho (10:30, test / 18:35 / 19:1);
- beside the way (8:5 / 18:35);
- begging (16:3);
- passing by;
- son of David (→ 20:41–44);
- mercy;
- silencing;
- the cry (κράζω) turned;
- Jesus stops;
- being led to him;
- looking up (7:22; 9:16 / 18:41–43 → 19:5);
- praise (→ 19:37, unprinted).

## Chapter 17

*19:1–26. Grey: 19:1, 19:3–5, 19:7, 19:12, 19:14–21, 19:24–25. Not printed, after 19:26: 19:27 (U−), 19:28 (U?), 19:29–46 (A−), 19:47–48 (U?).*

### 19:1–2 (19:1 grey)

*Καὶ εἰσελθὼν διήρχετο τὴν Ἰεριχώ. καὶ ἰδοὺ ἀνὴρ ὀνόματι καλούμενος Ζακχαῖος, καὶ αὐτὸς ἦν ἀρχιτελώνης καὶ αὐτὸς πλούσιος·*

- **"entering, he was passing through (διήρχετο) Jericho"**: *passing through: 4:30; 17:11.*
- **"a chief tax collector (ἀρχιτελώνης), and he was rich (πλούσιος)"**:
  - Tax collectors: Levi (5:27–30); those who drew near to hear (15:1); the tax collector who went down justified (18:13–14).
  - A "chief" (ἀρχι-), as the rich man of 18:18 was a "ruler" (ἄρχων). *The rich ruler went away sorrowful (18:23).* Here a second rich man with a ruler's title.

### 19:3–4 (grey)

*καὶ ἐζήτει ἰδεῖν τὸν Ἰησοῦν τίς ἐστιν, καὶ οὐκ ἠδύνατο ἀπὸ τοῦ ὄχλου ὅτι τῇ ἡλικίᾳ μικρὸς ἦν. καὶ προδραμὼν εἰς τὸ ἔμπροσθεν ἀνέβη ἐπὶ συκομορέαν ἵνα ἴδῃ αὐτόν, ὅτι ἐκείνης ἤμελλεν διέρχεσθαι.*

- **"he sought to see (ἐζήτει ἰδεῖν) Jesus, who he is (τίς ἐστιν)"**:
  - *Herod "sought to see (ἐζήτει ἰδεῖν) him" and asked "who is this?" (9:9). Forward: Herod "had long wanted to see him" (23:8).*
  - The question "who is this?" (5:21; 7:49; 8:25; 9:9; 9:18–20).
- **"and could not (οὐκ ἠδύνατο), because of the crowd"**:
  - *The friends of the paralysed man, "not finding how to bring him in because of the crowd (διὰ τὸν ὄχλον), went up (ἀναβάντες) onto the roof" (5:19).*
  - The crowd that presses (8:42, 8:45).
- **"because he was small in stature (τῇ ἡλικίᾳ μικρός)"**:
  - *Stature: "who by being anxious can add a cubit to his stature (ἡλικίαν)?" (12:25), the only other place.*
  - Small: "the least among you is great" (9:48); "little flock" (12:32); "these little ones" (17:2).
  - The one who cannot add to his height.
- **"running ahead (προδραμών)"**: *the father "running" (δραμών) to the son (15:20, test).*
- **"he went up (ἀνέβη) onto a sycamore-fig, to see him"**:
  - *Going up.* He went up the mountain to pray (9:28). Two men went up to the temple to pray (18:10). The friends went up onto the roof because of the crowd (5:19).
  - Here the small man goes up a tree to see.
  - The tree: the mustard seed became a tree, and the birds dwelt in it (13:19). The fig tree that bore no fruit (13:6–9, test). "Look at the fig tree" (21:29).
- **"because he was about to pass through (διέρχεσθαι) that way"**: passing through. He goes up where the way passes.

### 19:5–6 (19:5 grey)

*καὶ ὡς ἦλθεν ἐπὶ τὸν τόπον, ἀναβλέψας ὁ Ἰησοῦς εἶπεν πρὸς αὐτόν· Ζακχαῖε, σπεύσας κατάβηθι, σήμερον γὰρ ἐν τῷ οἴκῳ σου δεῖ με μεῖναι. καὶ σπεύσας κατέβη, καὶ ὑπεδέξατο αὐτὸν χαίρων.*

- **"looking up (ἀναβλέψας), Jesus said"**:
  - *Looking up:* the blind man "looked up" (18:41–43), the verb of the healing just before. "Looking up into heaven, he blessed" (9:16).
  - *He looks up at the one who went up.*
- **"Zacchaeus, hurry and come down (κατάβηθι)"**:
  - *Coming down:* the tax collector "went down (κατέβη) to his house justified" (18:14). He came down from the mountain (6:17). "Let him not go down (μὴ καταβάτω) to take" (17:31).
  - The paralysed man was let down (καθῆκαν) to him from the roof (5:19).
  - The one who went up to see is called down.
- **"for today (σήμερον) I must (δεῖ) remain (μεῖναι) in your house"**:
  - *"Must" (δεῖ), the necessity of the passion (9:22; 17:25), here the necessity of remaining in a sinner's house.*
  - Remaining (μένω):
    - Legion "did not remain in a house" (8:27);
    - "remain in that house" (9:4; 10:7).
  - *Forward: at Emmaus, "Remain (μεῖνον) with us, for it is toward evening… and he went in to remain (μεῖναι) with them" (24:29).*
  - Today: today and tomorrow (13:32–33, test). *Forward:* "today salvation" (19:9).
- **"and hurrying, he came down (κατέβη), and received (ὑπεδέξατο) him rejoicing"**:
  - *Receiving: Martha "received (ὑπεδέξατο) him" (10:38, test). The reception of Levi (δοχή, 5:29); of the poor (14:13).*
  - Rejoicing: the shepherd "rejoicing" (15:5).

### 19:7 (grey)

*καὶ ἰδόντες πάντες διεγόγγυζον λέγοντες ὅτι Παρὰ ἁμαρτωλῷ ἀνδρὶ εἰσῆλθεν καταλῦσαι.*

- **"all grumbled (διεγόγγυζον)"**: *the third of the three: 5:30; 15:2; 19:7.*
- **"he has gone in to lodge (καταλῦσαι) with a sinful man"**:
  - *Lodging: "that they may lodge (καταλύσωσιν) and find provisions" (9:12). Forward: "where is the guest room (κατάλυμα) where I may eat the Passover?" (22:11).*
  - The Son of Man "has nowhere to lay his head" (9:58). He lodges with a sinner.

### 19:8

*σταθεὶς δὲ Ζακχαῖος εἶπεν πρὸς τὸν κύριον· Ἰδοὺ τὰ ἡμίσιά μου τῶν ὑπαρχόντων, κύριε, τοῖς πτωχοῖς δίδωμι, καὶ εἴ τινός τι ἐσυκοφάντησα ἀποδίδωμι τετραπλοῦν.*

- **"standing (σταθείς)"**:
  - *The Pharisee "standing (σταθείς), prayed to himself", listing his fasting and tithing (18:11–12).*
  - Jesus "standing (σταθείς)" for the blind man (18:40).
  - Zacchaeus stands and speaks "to the Lord", not to himself.
- **"half of my possessions (ὑπαρχόντων), Lord, I give (δίδωμι) to the poor"**:
  - *The ruler was told "sell all that you have and give (δός) to the poor" (18:22), and did not.* The chief tax collector gives, now.
  - *The strong man's spoils given out (11:22).* "Give as alms the things within" (11:41).
  - Possessions (12:15; 16:1).
- **"if I have extorted (ἐσυκοφάντησα) anything"**: συκο-φαντέω. *Its first part is the fig (σῦκον) of the sycamore-fig (συκομορέα) he went up (19:4).*
- **"I give back (ἀποδίδωμι) fourfold"**:
  - *Giving back: "until you give back (ἀποδῷς) the last coin" (12:59); "give back (ἀπόδος) the account" (16:2).*
  - He gives back more than what was taken. The measure running over (6:38).

### 19:9–10

*εἶπεν δὲ πρὸς αὐτὸν ὁ Ἰησοῦς ὅτι Σήμερον σωτηρία τῷ οἴκῳ τούτῳ ἐγένετο, καθότι καὶ αὐτὸς υἱὸς Ἀβραάμ ἐστιν· ἦλθεν γὰρ ὁ υἱὸς τοῦ ἀνθρώπου ζητῆσαι καὶ σῶσαι τὸ ἀπολωλός.*

- **"today salvation (σωτηρία) has come to this house"**:
  - *Salvation:* "your faith has saved you" (7:50; 8:48; 17:19; 18:42).
  - *The house:* "peace to this house" (10:5); "went down to his house justified" (18:14).
- **"since he also is a son of Abraham"**: *"this daughter of Abraham", the bent woman loosed (13:16).* Son and daughter of Abraham: the loosed and the saved. Lazarus in Abraham's bosom (16:22).
- **"the Son of Man came to seek (ζητῆσαι) and to save the lost (τὸ ἀπολωλός)"**:
  - *Zacchaeus "sought (ἐζήτει) to see" him (19:3). The Son of Man came to seek him. The seeking goes both ways, as the reception did (8:40 / 9:11).*
  - *The lost: the lost sheep (τὸ ἀπολωλός, 15:4–6); "was lost and is found" (15:24, 15:32, test).*

### 19:11–12 (19:12 grey)

*Ἀκουόντων δὲ αὐτῶν ταῦτα προσθεὶς εἶπεν παραβολὴν διὰ τὸ ἐγγὺς εἶναι Ἰερουσαλὴμ αὐτὸν καὶ δοκεῖν αὐτοὺς ὅτι παραχρῆμα μέλλει ἡ βασιλεία τοῦ θεοῦ ἀναφαίνεσθαι· εἶπεν οὖν· Ἄνθρωπός τις εὐγενὴς ἐπορεύθη εἰς χώραν μακρὰν λαβεῖν ἑαυτῷ βασιλείαν καὶ ὑποστρέψαι.*

- **"adding (προσθείς)"**: adding (12:25, 12:31).
- **"because he was near (ἐγγύς) Jerusalem"**:
  - *The road, near its goal. The second time since 9:53 that the printed text names Jerusalem on the road (17:11; 19:11).*
  - Drawing near (18:35, 18:40).
- **"they thought (δοκεῖν) that the kingdom of God was about to shine up (ἀναφαίνεσθαι) at once"**:
  - *ἀνα-φαίνω, "appear up, shine forth".* The expectation is of a rising appearance, at once.
  - Seeming (δοκέω): "what he seems to have" (8:18); "in an hour you do not think" (12:40).
- **"a man of noble birth (εὐγενής)"**:
  - *Birth, origin:* "this foreigner (ἀλλογενής)" (17:18); "I do not know where you are from" (13:25).
  - The well-born is the one who goes away.
- **"went (ἐπορεύθη) into a far country (χώραν μακράν)"**:
  - *The younger son "went away into a far country (χώραν μακράν)" (15:13, test). The same words.*
  - The son went to scatter. The nobleman goes to receive.
- **"to receive (λαβεῖν) a kingdom for himself, and to return (ὑποστρέψαι)"**:
  - *λαμβάνω. The days of "his being taken up" (ἀνάλημψις, 9:51).*
  - *Here the going away is to receive a kingdom.* "Having received (λαβόντα) the kingdom" (19:15).
  - Returning (8:37–40; 10:17; 17:15–18). The lord returning from the wedding (12:36).
- *The parable answers the thought of a kingdom shining up at once. There is a going away to receive, and a return.*

### 19:13–14 (19:14 grey)

*καλέσας δὲ δέκα δούλους ἑαυτοῦ ἔδωκεν αὐτοῖς δέκα μνᾶς καὶ εἶπεν πρὸς αὐτούς· Πραγματεύσασθε ἐν ᾧ ἔρχομαι. οἱ δὲ πολῖται αὐτοῦ ἐμίσουν αὐτόν, καὶ ἀπέστειλαν πρεσβείαν ὀπίσω αὐτοῦ λέγοντες· Οὐ θέλομεν τοῦτον βασιλεῦσαι ἐφ’ ἡμᾶς.*

- **"ten servants… ten minas"**: ten (15:8; 17:12).
- **"trade while I am coming"**: what is given is to be worked with in the absence. The steward of 12:42–48 ("my lord delays").
- **"his citizens (πολῖται) hated (ἐμίσουν) him"**:
  - *The younger son "joined himself to one of the citizens (πολιτῶν)" of the far country (15:15, test).*
  - Hating (6:22, 6:27; 16:13). *Forward:* "you will be hated by all" (21:17).
- **"they sent an embassy (πρεσβείαν) after (ὀπίσω) him"**:
  - *The king who "sends an embassy (πρεσβείαν) and asks the terms of peace" (14:32, test).* Here the embassy is sent to refuse.
  - Behind him: "come behind me" (9:23).
- **"we do not want (οὐ θέλομεν) this man to reign over us"**:
  - *"How often I wanted… and you did not want (οὐκ ἠθελήσατε)" (13:34, test).* The elder son "did not want (οὐκ ἤθελεν) to go in" (15:28, test).
  - The will that refuses his reign.

### 19:15–19 (grey)

*καὶ ἐγένετο ἐν τῷ ἐπανελθεῖν αὐτὸν λαβόντα τὴν βασιλείαν… παρεγένετο δὲ ὁ πρῶτος λέγων· Κύριε, ἡ μνᾶ σου δέκα προσηργάσατο μνᾶς. καὶ εἶπεν αὐτῷ· Εὖγε, ἀγαθὲ δοῦλε, ὅτι ἐν ἐλαχίστῳ πιστὸς ἐγένου, ἴσθι ἐξουσίαν ἔχων ἐπάνω δέκα πόλεων…*

- **"when he returned (ἐπανελθεῖν), having received (λαβόντα) the kingdom"**:
  - *The Samaritan: "when I return (ἐπανέρχεσθαι), I will repay you" (10:35, test).*
  - Having received the kingdom: the going away is complete.
- **"the silver (ἀργύριον)"**: "take no silver" for the way (9:3). What is given is his.
- **"Lord, your mina has worked to gain ten minas"**: "your mina". The servant claims nothing as his. *It is the opposite of the Pharisee's "I fast, I tithe" (18:12).*
- **"Well done, good (ἀγαθέ) servant"**:
  - *Good: "no one is good except one" (18:19).* The servant is called good for what he did with what was given.
- **"because you were faithful in a very little (ἐλαχίστῳ)"**: *16:10.*
- **"have authority (ἐξουσίαν) over ten cities"**:
  - *Authority:* his authority (4:36; 5:24); given to the twelve (9:1) and the seventy (10:19).
  - Set over all the possessions (12:44).
- **"your mina made five… be over five cities"**: the measure, according to what was done.

### 19:20–23 (19:20–21 grey)

*Κύριε, ἰδοὺ ἡ μνᾶ σου ἣν εἶχον ἀποκειμένην ἐν σουδαρίῳ· ἐφοβούμην γάρ σε ὅτι ἄνθρωπος αὐστηρὸς εἶ, αἴρεις ὃ οὐκ ἔθηκας καὶ θερίζεις ὃ οὐκ ἔσπειρας. λέγει αὐτῷ· Ἐκ τοῦ στόματός σου κρίνω σε, πονηρὲ δοῦλε… καὶ διὰ τί οὐκ ἔδωκάς μου τὸ ἀργύριον ἐπὶ τράπεζαν; κἀγὼ ἐλθὼν σὺν τόκῳ ἂν αὐτὸ ἔπραξα.*

- **"which I had laid away (ἀποκειμένην) in a cloth"**:
  - *κεῖμαι, "lie, be laid up": the rich man's "goods laid up (κείμενα) for many years" (12:19).*
  - The gift laid away, kept, not worked with.
- **"for I was afraid of you, because you are a severe man"**:
  - Fear (12:4–5, 12:32): the fear that is not of the right object.
  - The servant's picture of his lord: severe.
- **"you take (αἴρεις) what you did not lay down, and reap (θερίζεις) what you did not sow"**: taking (αἴρω: 8:18; 9:3; 11:22, 11:52); reaping and sowing (8:5; 10:2; 12:24).
- **"out of your own mouth I will judge you, evil (πονηρέ) servant"**:
  - *Judgement as one's own:*
    - "your sons will be your judges" (11:19);
    - "why do you not judge for yourselves?" (12:57);
    - "with the measure you measure" (6:38).
  - *The mouth:* "out of the overflow of the heart the mouth speaks" (6:45).
  - He is judged by the picture he holds of his lord.
- **"why did you not give my silver to the table (τράπεζαν)?"**: *the rich man's table (16:21).* Forward: "the hand… on the table" (22:21).

### 19:24–26 (19:24–25 grey)

*καὶ τοῖς παρεστῶσιν εἶπεν· Ἄρατε ἀπ’ αὐτοῦ τὴν μνᾶν καὶ δότε τῷ τὰς δέκα μνᾶς ἔχοντι— καὶ εἶπαν αὐτῷ· Κύριε, ἔχει δέκα μνᾶς— λέγω ὑμῖν ὅτι παντὶ τῷ ἔχοντι δοθήσεται, ἀπὸ δὲ τοῦ μὴ ἔχοντος καὶ ὃ ἔχει ἀρθήσεται.*

- **"take (ἄρατε) the mina from him and give it to the one who has ten"**: taking and giving.
- **"to everyone who has, more will be given; from the one who has not, even what he has will be taken (ἀρθήσεται)"**:
  - *The law of 8:18, the second time.* There: "even what he seems (δοκεῖ) to have". Here: "even what he has".
  - What is held and not worked with is not had.

### Test: 19:27 (U−)

*πλὴν τοὺς ἐχθρούς μου τούτους τοὺς μὴ θελήσαντάς με βασιλεῦσαι ἐπ’ αὐτοὺς ἀγάγετε ὧδε καὶ κατασφάξατε αὐτοὺς ἔμπροσθέν μου.*

- **"these enemies (ἐχθρούς) of mine, who did not want me to reign over them"**:
  - *The citizens of 19:14 ("we do not want this man to reign over us").*
  - Enemies: "love your enemies" (6:27, 6:35); "authority over all the power of the enemy" (10:19). *The printed 20:43: "until I put your enemies (ἐχθρούς) as a footstool for your feet".*
- **"bring them here and slaughter them before me"**.

*The reading.*
- Read by the letter, the master's order to slaughter stands against how the text forms judgement elsewhere:
  - from one's own mouth (19:22);
  - one's own measure (6:38);
  - the rebuke of the fire charity wanted (9:54–55).
- Read by the method, as the text's persons are read throughout, the "enemies who did not want me to reign" are what in a person refuses his reign. Their being put to death "before him" is the removal of the refusal in his presence. That is:
  - the treading of the enemy's power (10:19);
  - the enemies put under his feet (20:43);
  - "whoever loses his soul will make it live" (17:33).
- *The enemy as person is loved (6:27). The enemy as power in oneself is put under foot (10:19), and, here, put to death.*

**Verdict: continues, and the printed parable leans on it.**
- The printed text carries the strand of the kingdom: received (19:12), refused by the citizens (19:14), "having received the kingdom" (19:15).
- It never brings back the citizens who refused. Their end is 19:27, the only place the strand closes.
- U−. *Recorded for task 14.*

### Test: 19:28 (U?)

*Καὶ εἰπὼν ταῦτα ἐπορεύετο ἔμπροσθεν ἀναβαίνων εἰς Ἱεροσόλυμα.*

- **"he went on ahead (ἐπορεύετο ἔμπροσθεν)"**: the road. Zacchaeus "ran ahead" (19:4).
- **"going up (ἀναβαίνων) to Jerusalem"**: *the road's verb "go up", with its goal. With 18:31 (test), the only places it is said.*

**Verdict: continues.** It introduces 19:29–46.

### Test: 19:29–46 (A−)

*Καὶ ἐγένετο ὡς ἤγγισεν εἰς Βηθφαγὴ καὶ Βηθανίαν πρὸς τὸ ὄρος τὸ καλούμενον Ἐλαιῶν, ἀπέστειλεν δύο τῶν μαθητῶν λέγων· Ὑπάγετε εἰς τὴν κατέναντι κώμην, ἐν ᾗ εἰσπορευόμενοι εὑρήσετε πῶλον δεδεμένον, ἐφ’ ὃν οὐδεὶς πώποτε ἀνθρώπων ἐκάθισεν, καὶ λύσαντες αὐτὸν ἀγάγετε. καὶ ἐάν τις ὑμᾶς ἐρωτᾷ· Διὰ τί λύετε; οὕτως ἐρεῖτε ὅτι Ὁ κύριος αὐτοῦ χρείαν ἔχει… καὶ ἤγαγον αὐτὸν πρὸς τὸν Ἰησοῦν, καὶ ἐπιρίψαντες αὐτῶν τὰ ἱμάτια ἐπὶ τὸν πῶλον ἐπεβίβασαν τὸν Ἰησοῦν· πορευομένου δὲ αὐτοῦ ὑπεστρώννυον τὰ ἱμάτια ἑαυτῶν ἐν τῇ ὁδῷ. ἐγγίζοντος δὲ αὐτοῦ ἤδη πρὸς τῇ καταβάσει τοῦ Ὄρους τῶν Ἐλαιῶν ἤρξαντο ἅπαν τὸ πλῆθος τῶν μαθητῶν χαίροντες αἰνεῖν τὸν θεὸν φωνῇ μεγάλῃ περὶ πασῶν ὧν εἶδον δυνάμεων, λέγοντες· Εὐλογημένος ὁ ἐρχόμενος βασιλεὺς ἐν ὀνόματι κυρίου· ἐν οὐρανῷ εἰρήνη καὶ δόξα ἐν ὑψίστοις. καί τινες τῶν Φαρισαίων… εἶπαν πρὸς αὐτόν· Διδάσκαλε, ἐπιτίμησον τοῖς μαθηταῖς σου. καὶ ἀποκριθεὶς εἶπεν· Λέγω ὑμῖν, ὅτι ἐὰν οὗτοι σιωπήσουσιν, οἱ λίθοι κράξουσιν. Καὶ ὡς ἤγγισεν, ἰδὼν τὴν πόλιν ἔκλαυσεν ἐπ’ αὐτήν, λέγων ὅτι Εἰ ἔγνως ἐν τῇ ἡμέρᾳ ταύτῃ καὶ σὺ τὰ πρὸς εἰρήνην— νῦν δὲ ἐκρύβη ἀπὸ ὀφθαλμῶν σου. ὅτι ἥξουσιν ἡμέραι ἐπὶ σὲ καὶ παρεμβαλοῦσιν οἱ ἐχθροί σου χάρακά σοι καὶ περικυκλώσουσίν σε καὶ συνέξουσίν σε πάντοθεν, καὶ ἐδαφιοῦσίν σε καὶ τὰ τέκνα σου ἐν σοί, καὶ οὐκ ἀφήσουσιν λίθον ἐπὶ λίθον ἐν σοί, ἀνθ’ ὧν οὐκ ἔγνως τὸν καιρὸν τῆς ἐπισκοπῆς σου. Καὶ εἰσελθὼν εἰς τὸ ἱερὸν ἤρξατο ἐκβάλλειν τοὺς πωλοῦντας, λέγων αὐτοῖς· Γέγραπται· Καὶ ἔσται ὁ οἶκός μου οἶκος προσευχῆς, ὑμεῖς δὲ αὐτὸν ἐποιήσατε σπήλαιον λῃστῶν.*

- **"as he drew near (ἤγγισεν)… to the mountain called of Olives (Ἐλαιῶν)"**:
  - Drawing near (10:9; 18:35).
  - *The mountain:* Nazareth's hill (4:29), prayer (6:12), the swine (8:32), the going up (9:28).
  - Olives: oil (7:46; 16:6). *The printed text names the Mount of Olives at 21:37 and 22:39, as the place he goes out to at night and to pray. It never says how he came there.*
- **"a colt bound (δεδεμένον)… loose (λύσαντες) it… Why are you loosing it? The Lord has need (χρείαν) of it"**:
  - *Loosing what is bound:* "does not each of you loose (λύει) his ox or donkey…?"; "must she not be loosed (λυθῆναι) from this bond?" (13:15–16).
  - Need (5:31; 9:11; 15:7).
- **"on which no one of men has ever sat"**: *the tomb "where no one had yet been laid" (23:53).* What is new, at the entry and at the burial.
- **"throwing their garments on the colt, they set (ἐπεβίβασαν) Jesus on it"**:
  - *ἐπιβιβάζω: the Samaritan "set (ἐπιβιβάσας) him on his own animal" (10:34, test). The only other place.*
  - The one going down from Jerusalem was set on an animal by the Samaritan. Here Jesus is set on a colt going to Jerusalem.
  - Garments: truth that clothes (8:35; 9:29).
- **"as he went (πορευομένου), they spread their garments in the way (ἐν τῇ ὁδῷ)"**: the road, laid with garments.
- **"at the descent (καταβάσει) of the Mount of Olives"**: *going down: the descent from the mountain (6:17; 9:37).* The arrival at Jerusalem is a descent.
- **"the whole multitude of disciples began, rejoicing, to praise (αἰνεῖν) God with a loud voice, for all the powers (δυνάμεων) they had seen"**:
  - *"All the people gave praise (αἶνον) to God" (18:43).* The loud voice: the Samaritan (17:15).
  - Powers (4:36; 6:19; 8:46; 10:13, test).
- **"Blessed is the coming king (ὁ ἐρχόμενος βασιλεύς) in the name of the Lord"**:
  - *"Are you the coming one (ὁ ἐρχόμενος)?" (7:19–20).* "Until you say, Blessed is the coming one in the name of the Lord" (13:35, test).
  - *The king: the nobleman who went "to receive a kingdom" (19:12, 19:15).*
- **"in heaven peace, and glory in the highest (ὑψίστοις)"**:
  - *Peace:* "do you think I came to give peace on the earth?" (12:51). Here, peace in heaven.
  - *Glory (9:26, 9:31–32). The highest: "sons of the Most High (Ὑψίστου)" (6:35); "Son of God the Most High" (8:28).*
  - *At the descent of the mountain, glory in the highest.*
- **"Teacher, rebuke (ἐπιτίμησον) your disciples… if these are silent (σιωπήσουσιν), the stones (λίθοι) will cry out (κράξουσιν)"**:
  - *The blind man: those in front rebuked him to be silent (σιγήσῃ), and he cried out (ἔκραζεν) all the more (18:39).*
  - The stones: a stone's throw, where he prays (22:41); *the stone rolled away (24:2).*
- **"as he drew near, seeing the city, he wept (ἔκλαυσεν) over it"**: weeping (7:13, 7:38; 8:52; 13:28).
- **"if you had known… the things for peace; but now they are hidden (ἐκρύβη) from your eyes"**:
  - *The law of veiling:* "hidden" (8:17; 10:21; 12:2; 18:34, test). *The eyes held (24:16), and opened (24:31).*
- **"your enemies (ἐχθροί) will… surround you and hold you in (συνέξουσιν) on every side"**:
  - Enemies (19:27, test; 20:43).
  - *Holding in (συνέχω):* "how I am held fast (συνέχομαι) until it is accomplished" (12:50); "the men holding (συνέχοντες) him" (22:63).
  - *The printed 21:20: "when you see Jerusalem surrounded (κυκλουμένην)… its desolation has come near".*
- **"they will not leave stone upon stone, because you did not know the time (καιρόν) of your visitation"**:
  - *"This time (καιρόν) how do you not know how to test?" (12:56).*
  - Visitation: "God has visited (ἐπεσκέψατο) his people" (7:16).
- **"entering the temple, he began to cast out (ἐκβάλλειν) those selling (πωλοῦντας)"**:
  - *Casting out: the daimons (4:35; 11:14–20); the beam from one's own eye (6:42).*
  - Selling: "they bought, they sold" (17:28).
  - The temple: "two men went up into the temple to pray" (18:10).
- **"It is written: My house will be a house of prayer (οἶκος προσευχῆς); but you have made it a den of robbers (λῃστῶν)"**:
  - *Prayer:* going up to pray (9:28; 18:10).
  - *Robbers: the man going down from Jerusalem "fell among robbers (λῃσταῖς)" (10:30, test).*
  - The house: "your house is left to you" (13:35, test); "today salvation has come to this house" (19:9).

**Verdict: continues, and the printed text needs it.**
- *By its words.* Every phrase is in a thread of the printed text. It joins the printed 21:20 (Jerusalem surrounded) and 21:37 / 22:39 (the Mount of Olives).
  - *The printed 20:2 asks "by what authority do you do these things (ταῦτα ποιεῖς)?" Its "these things" has its first object in 19:45–46.*
- *By its mechanics — and this is the reason that matters.*
  - The road set at 9:51 ("he set his face to go to Jerusalem") is named again at 17:11 ("as he was going to Jerusalem") and 19:11 ("because he was near Jerusalem"). In the printed text it never arrives.
  - From "near Jerusalem" (19:11) the printed text goes to "in the temple" (20:1). There is:
    - no going up (19:28);
    - no drawing near to the mountain;
    - no descent of the mountain with "peace in heaven, and glory in the highest";
    - no weeping over the city whose peace is hidden from its eyes;
    - no entering the house of prayer.
  - *The inner story of the whole text is a road to Jerusalem, and the printed text leaves out the arrival.*
- A−: restoring it is against Marcion's text. The user has already approved that, and it must say so. *Recorded for task 14.*

### Test: 19:47–48 (U?)

*Καὶ ἦν διδάσκων τὸ καθ’ ἡμέραν ἐν τῷ ἱερῷ· οἱ δὲ ἀρχιερεῖς καὶ οἱ γραμματεῖς ἐζήτουν αὐτὸν ἀπολέσαι καὶ οἱ πρῶτοι τοῦ λαοῦ, καὶ οὐχ εὕρισκον τὸ τί ποιήσωσιν, ὁ λαὸς γὰρ ἅπας ἐξεκρέματο αὐτοῦ ἀκούων.*

- **"teaching daily (τὸ καθ’ ἡμέραν) in the temple"**: daily (9:23; 11:3; 16:19). *Forward: "by day he was teaching in the temple" (21:37).*
- **"the chief priests and the scribes sought to destroy (ἀπολέσαι) him, and the first of the people"**:
  - *"Rejected by the elders and chief priests and scribes" (9:22).* *Forward: "the chief priests and scribes sought how to kill him" (22:2).*
  - The first (πρῶτοι): the own's first.
- **"all the people hung on him, listening"**: hearing (9:35).

**Verdict: continues.** It does not close a gap: 20:1 opens "on one of the days, as he was teaching the people in the temple".

### What came out of the reading of chapter 17

**Up to see, down to receive (19:1–10).**
- A rich chief tax collector seeks to see who Jesus is, as Herod did (9:9). He cannot, because of the crowd, as the friends of the paralysed man could not (5:19). He is small in stature: no one adds to his own height (12:25).
- He runs ahead and goes up a tree to see.
- Jesus, coming to the place, looks up — the verb of the blind man's healing — and says: "Hurry, come down; today I must remain in your house."
- He hurries, comes down, and receives him rejoicing.
- He stands, not to himself as the Pharisee did, but to the Lord. He gives half of what he has to the poor, and gives back fourfold.
- "Today salvation has come to this house… The Son of Man came to seek and to save the lost."
- *Here the printed text acts out 18:14 in an event:*
  - the small one goes up, as the two went up to pray;
  - he is called down, as the tax collector went down to his house;
  - salvation comes to his house, as the tax collector went down justified.
- *Going up is to see. Coming down is to receive. What is given is salvation, in the house, "today". The "must" of the passion is here the "must" of remaining with the sinner. It returns at Emmaus: "remain with us… and he went in to remain with them" (24:29).*

**The kingdom received by going away (19:11–26).**
- They think the kingdom will "shine up" at once.
- The parable: a well-born man goes into a far country — the words of the younger son's going (15:13, test) — to receive a kingdom, and to return.
- His servants work with what he gave: "your mina has gained". They are given authority over cities.
- The one who laid it away in fear is judged out of his own mouth. What he has is taken.
- The citizens who will not have him reign are introduced (19:14). In the printed text they are not brought back.

**The arrival is missing.**
- The printed text names the road's goal at 9:51, 17:11 and 19:11, and does not arrive.
- The unprinted 19:28–46 is the arrival:
  - going up;
  - the bound colt loosed because the Lord has need of it;
  - garments on the way;
  - the descent of the Mount of Olives with "peace in heaven, and glory in the highest";
  - the stones that would cry;
  - the weeping over the city whose peace is hidden from its eyes;
  - the house of prayer cleansed of those who sell.
- *By the mechanics of the whole text it is needed.* It is the third passage the reading finds needed, and the one the user has already approved restoring against Marcion's text.
- 19:27 closes the strand of the citizens who refuse his reign. Read inwardly, it is the refusal itself put to death before him (10:19; 20:43). The printed parable leans on it.
